# Switchboard architecture

Requirements live in [prd.md](prd.md). This document describes components, state and protocols.

Session and triage mechanics come from Ground Control (`D:\git\ground-control`), where they are verified in its [mechanics.md](../../ground-control/docs/mechanics.md); IDs from there carry a `GC` prefix. Unprefixed M IDs are entries in Switchboard's own [mechanics.md](mechanics.md). Re-verify a version-fragile Ground Control mechanism there before relying on it.

## Packages

npm workspaces, TypeScript strict and Node ≥ 22.13, following Ground Control's layout. No package imports `vscode`: the [web page](#web-page) is the only interface, and Open in VS Code only starts VS Code on a workspace file or folder.

| Package | Responsibility | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/core` | Domain types, source and runner interfaces, store, playbook loader and renderer, flow rules | — |
| `packages/claude` | Claude Code runner, classifier, debrief fork and [Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions) | core, conversation, the holder client |
| `packages/codex` | Codex runner, classifier, run records and hooks ([Codex runner](#codex-runner)) | core |
| `packages/holder` | The [terminal holder](#terminal-holder): its host, client, attach client and protocol. The client imports no native module; the host is installed per build in the Switchboard home with its native terminal dependency | nothing in Switchboard |
| `packages/conversation` | Shared conversation log, rules and artifacts, and Claude's transcript, screen and guarded input mechanics ([Conversation](#conversation)) | core |
| `packages/voice` | The voice process and its protocol and client ([Voice](#voice)). The main entry imports no native module; the process is installed per build in the Switchboard home with its native audio dependencies | core |
| `packages/github`, `packages/zoho` | The built-in GitHub and Zoho Desk sources | core |
| `packages/protocol` | Types only: the wire types of the conversation API and the web page's routes ([Conversation API](#conversation-api)) | core and conversation types |
| `packages/web` | The web page's front end and theme, served by the hub ([Web page](#web-page)) | protocol; browser-safe core and conversation modules |
| `packages/hub` | The hub: polling, flow execution, the loopback HTTP/SSE server, session reports, debriefs, supervision of the terminal holder and the voice process, page scopes, the conversation service and the web page's routes | core, the sources, the runners, conversation, protocol, web, holder and voice |
| `apps/hub` | The hub's entry point and its commands, bundled as one file, and the standalone build of everything `hub.js install` installs | hub, holder, voice; the web page's and the browser extension's builds |
| `extensions/chrome` | The [browser extension](#browser-extension) for GitHub project boards, embedded link tabs and the web page's Switchboard tab group | browser-safe core modules, the hub's browser endpoint |
| `extensions/firefox` | The Firefox build of `extensions/chrome`, from the same source | `extensions/chrome` |

The ad-hoc source is local: the core's flow creates its tickets from the operator's prompts and from task reports, and nothing polls it. State, the hub bundle, its record and its log live in the Switchboard home, `~/.switchboard`. Settings and playbooks live in the [config folder](#config-folder), which the hub names in the Switchboard home's `config.json`, so the operator can keep them in a git repository.

## Components

| Component | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| Hub | Separate Node process that runs the core: polls sources, creates work items, runs triage, starts setup and task sessions, enforces one session in a turn per ticket (PRD R17), and serves the web page, the browser extension and the report command. |
| Source plugins | Supply tickets, decide whether each is in the assigned state, and build each ticket's triage context: GitHub, Zoho Desk, external plugins and ad-hoc. |
| Playbook loader | Reads the playbooks in settings.json and renders their templates. |
| Classifier | Runs one-shot triage and returns structured output. |
| Runner | Starts, stops and tracks agent sessions for one provider behind the `SessionRunner` interface in `packages/core/src/runner.ts`, which the hub runtime calls: Claude Code, Codex, and Claude Code's [Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions). |
| Worktree linker | Links worktrees to tickets from session reports, falling back to branch-name convention. |
| Store | Local SQLite database holding tickets, work items, triage results, sessions and worktrees. |
| Conversation layer | Runs in the hub: follows each live Code Mode session's transcript and screen, keeps its conversation, and serves each web page the conversations of the work item it selected ([Conversation](#conversation)). |
| Web page | The only interface, served by the hub: lists the work items, runs the controls, shows each work item's conversation, state, artifacts, changes, history and console, and edits the settings ([Web page](#web-page)). |
| Voice process | PRD R105: a Node.js process per Switchboard home, started by the hub while a page wants it, that decides which session the voice speaks for and speaks the replies the hub offers ([Voice](#voice)). |
| Terminal holder | PRD R94: a Node.js process per Switchboard home, started by the hub, that holds Code Mode sessions in pseudo-terminals, replays their screens to attached terminals, and lists and kills them ([Terminal holder](#terminal-holder)). |

## Data model

The store owns the durable state; the schema and its migrations are in `packages/core/src/store.ts`.

| Entity | Records |
|---|---|
| Ticket | One ticket of a source: its keys, assigned and pool state, snooze, scope time, repository, blockers and, for a [hosted conversation](#hosted-conversations) an older hub stored, its host window. |
| WorkItem | One pass of work on a ticket under one playbook: status, prompts, guidance, review position and how it ended. |
| Triage | One classifier run and the context it was given. |
| Worktree | A worktree linked to a ticket, by report or by convention. |
| Link | A link reported by a session, or found in the ticket's text by triage (PRD R61) until a session reports it. |
| Paste | A drafted text a session named for the operator to copy (PRD R60). |
| Comment | An operator comment on a work item (PRD R87). |
| Message, Reply, Checkpoint | A Code Mode session's conversation and how far its transcript has been read ([Conversation](#conversation)). |
| Session | One agent session of a work item: kind, provider, native IDs, working directory, observed state, outcome, and the provider-specific state the hub needs to recover or continue it. |

- **Source boundary.** A source sets each ticket's `ref`, the reference people use (such as `#<n>`, `owner/repo#<n>` outside the home repository, or a forum topic key), its `sourceKey` and, when its API needs a different one, `sourceId`; core holds no per-source rule. `Ticket.sortAt` is the source's sort time, which orders the Unstarted group. `Ticket.repo` is the ticket's repository in lowercase; empty means the source's home repository ([Repo roots](#repo-roots)).
- **Scope time** (PRD R53): `Ticket.scopeAt` is when the ticket last entered its source's scope. Core stores the source's own entry time whenever the source gives one, because a source's scope can be wider than the assigned state and the pool; otherwise a ticket keeps the time it has, or takes the poll's time when it first appears assigned or in the pool.
- **Pool triage.** A pool triage belongs to the ticket until a pick creates the work item and attaches it. A ticket that leaves the pool without a pick loses its finished pool triages. A triage still running for it is kept until it ends, so it still counts toward triage concurrency and a work item on that ticket waits for it rather than starting a second; a ticket that returns to the pool drops that running triage and is triaged afresh.
- **Moved tickets** (PRD R83): a ticket a ticket report moved from ad hoc to a source keeps its ad-hoc key, so the key is never used again and its window and workspace file keep their name.
- **Blockers** (PRD R90): `Ticket.blockers` is the open blockers from the last complete poll that reported them; a source that does not report blockers leaves the stored list unchanged. A queued work item waits while the list is not empty.
- **Triage record.** `Triage.context` keeps the clipped text the classifier received, so the operator can see why a playbook was chosen. The focus a row opens (PRD R79) is that of the latest triage with a playbook, else the ticket's URL, so a running or failed triage keeps the earlier focus.
- **Work item times.** `WorkItem.readyAt` is set once, when the work item first becomes `awaiting-approval` or `finished`, and nothing else changes it, so the work item keeps its place in Ready for review when it runs again. `WorkItem.orderAt` is the operator's position in Ready for review (PRD R73).
- **Prompts and guidance** (PRD R68, R77): `WorkItem.taskPrompt` is the prompt the task session ran, or an ad-hoc or task work item's own prompt, which Start with prompt… may replace; a playbook's prompt cannot be replaced. `WorkItem.guidance` is kept until a task session starts with it appended, so a failed start keeps it for Retry. `WorkItem.reportedPrompt` keeps a task report's original prompt, so a repeated report finds its open child even after its prompt was replaced, and `WorkItem.parentId` names the work item whose session sent it.
- **Ending** (PRD R10, R11): `WorkItem.doneReason` is `session`, `operator` or `unassigned`; `unassignedAt` and `warning` record a ticket that left the assigned state while work ran.
- **Session state.** `Session.state` is the observed state, with a detail naming why a session waits (a start prompt or a permission prompt) or that its turn ended with a question open in its conversation and `Session.outcome` the reported or decided outcome (PRD R36). `Session.released` records that a held session's end or an app-server thread's release was confirmed, so it is sent once (PRD R39). Native IDs a fork retired (PRD R78), a Codex session's reported question (PRD R70), a registration (PRD R81), a usage limit (PRD R111) and a Code Mode session's launch and process tree are stored on the session so a restarted hub can recover or continue it; they are left out of the activity log, whose events name them.
- **Persona** ([Personas](#personas)): a Code Mode session records its persona, and the version of the tone section its prompt last got.
- **Conversation rules.** No session records a rules version: every session is read as if it carries the current conversation rules.
- A session's `cwd` follows its playbook's `workdir`: the ticket's worktree when one exists, otherwise the source's repo root.
- A `Paste` belongs to the work item, not the ticket, because a returned ticket's new work item drafts new texts; a later paste with the same label replaces the earlier one in its place.
- **Migrations** run with foreign keys off, because a table rebuild drops a parent table, and each fails unless `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` is empty before it commits.
- **Snapshot cost.** The snapshot reads work items through indexed queries, so its cost does not grow with the done history.

## Hub lifecycle

Follows Ground Control's hub (GC R35, GC M25). One hub runs per Switchboard home; session reports and the `hub.js` commands reach it through its record, and the web page and the browser extension through its fixed ports.

- **Install:** `npm run install-local` builds every workspace and runs `hub.js install --wait` from apps/hub's standalone build, `apps/hub/dist/install`, whose carried builds `apps/hub/carried.cjs` lists. `hub.js install` run from a build folder copies the builds it carries (the hub bundle, the web page's assets and the browser extension, and the holder and voice builds with their native modules) into the Switchboard home, and the running hub restarts onto the new hub bundle (Restart onto a new build, below); `--wait` waits for that restart.
- **Start** (PRD R117): `hub.js start` reports the running hub when one answers for the home; otherwise it starts the installed bundle detached on the runtime that runs the command, without the Electron and VS Code variables of its environment, on the [config folder](#config-folder) config.json names, logging to `~/.switchboard/hub.log`, and waits for its record. The login start, the `switchboard:` link and the supervisor below all run it; nothing else starts a hub.
- **Environment:** every `hub.js` command removes `NO_COLOR` and `FORCE_COLOR` from its own environment at startup, so a hub, the hubs the supervisor and `hub.js start` start, a successor on restart, and the holder, voice process and sessions a hub starts run without them, whichever shell started the first; without colour a held Claude Code draws its suggested prompt as typed text (mechanics M149).
- **Record:** the hub listens on `127.0.0.1` on an ephemeral port and writes `~/.switchboard/hub.json` with `protocol`, `version`, `build` (a hash of its bundle), `configDir`, `execPath` (the runtime it runs on), `port`, `token`, `pid`, `startedAt` and, from a hub that restarts onto a new build by itself, `restarts: true`; a record without `configDir` or `execPath` reads as no hub. The hub holds a lock for its lifetime, a named pipe on Windows and `~/.switchboard/hub.sock` elsewhere; only the lock holder writes or removes the record, so concurrent starts leave one hub. A forced exit on Windows leaves the record behind, so clients accept a record only when `GET /status` with its token returns the recorded pid.
- **Endpoints:** on the hub's port, `GET /hub` returns identity without a token, the [web page](#web-page)'s routes use its own sign-in, and every other route requires `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. The page's stream and commands are the [Conversation API](#conversation-api) and the page's own routes ([Web page](#web-page)); every page's stream gets `config` after the hub reloads the settings or playbooks. `/contract` gives the contract hook its session's contract ([Claude Code runner](#claude-code-runner)), and `/register` registers a session or answers 409 with the refusal ([Session reports](#session-reports)). `/attach` gives the [attach client](#attach-client) the terminal holder's address for a session, resuming an ended Code Mode session first, with 404 for an unknown session and 409 for any other refusal. `/holder/list`, `/holder/stop` and `/holder/restart` list and stop held sessions and [restart the holder](#restarting-the-holder) ([Visibility](#visibility-of-held-sessions)), and `/holder/open` opens a held session's attach client in the terminal app, answering 404 for an ID that names no held session and 409 with any other refusal.
- **Restart onto a new build:** the hub watches the installed bundle, and when it is another build, restarts onto it once it is quiet: no request in flight, no session starting, being attached, resumed or ended, no message being typed or sent, no holder restart, triage, source or roster read, operator action, voice speech, enrolment or utterance being heard, or debrief running, no phone that has yet to take the voice back after the last restart, and the hub not stopping. It logs each reason it waits for. The successor takes over the hub lock once the old hub releases it; meanwhile the old hub answers every `POST` 503 "The hub is restarting onto a new build; try again in a few seconds.", which the session report commands retry. The old hub checks it is still quiet once the successor is ready, and otherwise ends the successor and waits again. A successor that fails is ended and logged, and the old hub keeps running its own build, until another build is installed or `hub.js restart` asks again. The terminal holder and its sessions keep running across the restart. A handover file (`hub-handover.json`) names the successor while it loads, so a hub started meanwhile, as by the supervisor or a login start, waits for it rather than take the lock first, and carries what the old hub hands the successor: the voice's device and the paired device's sign-in that holds it, the target and its generation, Spoken replies and the mic, so a restart is transparent to the voice ([Remote access](#remote-access), Voice on a phone). The file is written before the old hub lets go of anything; a write that fails ends the successor, and the old hub keeps running its own build. From the handover on, the old hub sends the voice process nothing, so neither the phone's socket closing nor its pages leaving move the voice. A web page reconnects in place and offers a reload for the new build, never reloading by itself, because a reload loses what the operator was doing and ends a phone's audio until its next tap ([Web page](#web-page), Connection). `hub.js restart` (`POST /restart`) asks for the same restart onto whichever build is installed, its own included, and reports what the hub waits for; it is refused when no bundle is installed or a restart is already under way.
- **Snapshot worker.** The snapshot and the dock view, the list's view model ([Rows](#rows)), are built in a worker thread, once per burst of changes, because a build reads the store for long enough to hold back conversation events on the main thread. The worker opens the store read-only and builds in one read transaction, so a commit between two queries cannot mix two states, and the hub publishes each snapshot and dock view whole, so no view mixes two builds. A worker that fails is logged and replaced by builds on the main thread. `buildSnapshot` in `packages/hub/src/snapshot.ts` is the projection both run.
- **Login start and `switchboard:` link** (PRD R117): `hub.js autostart on` registers a login-time start and a handler for `switchboard:` links, which the web page's Switchboard is not reachable screen opens on this computer ([Web page](#web-page), Connection); each runs `hub.js start` for this home, naming no config folder, so the start reads config.json and a new config folder needs no new entries. Neither passes the link's URL to the command, so a page that opens a `switchboard:` link can only start the hub. The login entry is per home and the link per user, so the last home to turn it on owns the link. It refuses, changing nothing, under VS Code's runtime, without an installed bundle, with a config.json the hub could not start on, and for a path the entries could not quote safely, and on Windows without `USERNAME`, which names the supervisor task's user. `hub.js autostart off` removes only the entries that start this home's bundle, so entries another home or program wrote under the same names stay.
- **Supervisor** (PRD R117): `hub.js autostart on` also installs a supervisor, `hub.js supervise` for this home, which starts the installed hub again, on its own runtime and on the [config folder](#config-folder) config.json names, as the entries do, when the hub has stopped without being stopped on purpose; `off` removes it and `status` shows its command and whether it runs. On Windows it is a hidden Task Scheduler task that starts at the user's logon and runs the supervisor through `wscript.exe`, so no console window opens; Task Scheduler starts it again after a failure, and the logon trigger repeats, starting it only when it is not running. On Linux it is a systemd user unit that runs with the graphical session, as the login start does, with `Restart=on-failure` and `KillMode=process`, so stopping or restarting the unit never stops the hub it started. The supervisor works with the hub's own lifecycle rather than replacing it:
  - It starts no hub while any process holds the hub lock, a starting or hung hub included, or while a restart onto a new build hands over to a successor that still runs, so it never starts a second hub or takes the lock before a successor.
  - A hub that stops on purpose (`hub.js --stop`, `POST /shutdown`, a signal or the test idle exit) writes `hub-stopped.json` before it lets go of the lock, and the supervisor leaves the hub stopped until another start takes the lock and removes the file. A restart onto a new build writes none, so a successor that fails to take over is started again; any other exit, including a forced one, is a crash.
  - It leaves the first start of a session to the login start for a short grace period, and waits longer before each start while the hub keeps failing, until the hub has held the lock for a while.
  - Its own lock keeps one supervisor per home: a named pipe beside the hub's on Windows, and on Linux an abstract socket, which no file outlives, so no start has to remove a stale one; a stop request must carry the secret in `supervisor-token`, which only the user can read, because another account can reach an abstract socket. `autostart off`, and `on` before it runs the new definition, ask a running supervisor over that lock to exit, rather than having Task Scheduler end it, which could end the hub it started. On Windows `off` first writes `supervisor-off` and deletes the task, so a supervisor the task had launched but that holds no lock yet exits once it does; `on` removes the file.
  - It logs to `hub.log` with a `supervisor:` prefix, and runs the build it started with until it next starts.
- **Clients:** each open web page holds one conversation stream ([Web page](#web-page)), and no other client keeps one. The hub runs until it is stopped (`hub.js --stop`, `POST /shutdown`), whether or not any page is open (PRD R43, R117); only tests make it exit when idle. The supervisor starts a hub that crashed again (Supervisor, above), and a page whose stream ends reconnects in place ([Web page](#web-page), Connection).
- **Browser endpoint:** for the browser extension, which cannot read `hub.json`, the hub also listens on the fixed port `127.0.0.1:47615` (`BROWSER_PORT` in core). Without a token it serves `GET /snoozed`, the URLs of tickets snoozed now, kept because the extension is loaded separately and can be older than the hub, and `GET /cards` (PRD R67), one card per ticket URL the list shows, with the playbook name, group, snooze flag and link targets its row shows. A ticket with several work items gives the pool entry, else the open work item, else the latest. `POST /snooze {url, until}` and `POST /unsnooze {url}` find the assigned or pool ticket by URL, ignoring case, and send the core's `snooze` or `restore` event (PRD R66): 200, 404 when no ticket in scope has the URL, 409 with the core's refusal, 400 for another body or an oversized one. Both answer 403 to an `Origin` that is not `chrome-extension://` or `moz-extension://` and 415 to a content type other than `application/json`, before reading the body, so a web page's request either fails its CORS preflight or is refused, and only an extension with host permission for the port can snooze; any such extension can, because an unpacked extension's ID depends on its folder ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M23). The endpoint sends no CORS headers, so web pages cannot read it, and answers 403 to a `Host` other than the loopback address and port, against DNS rebinding. A hub that cannot take the port, such as a second hub for another home, logs it and runs without the endpoint. `GET /iframe` vouches for the configured page origins and setting; every WebSocket upgrade is refused.
- **Entry point:** one bundle, `hub.js`, serves the hub, reading settings and playbooks from the [config folder](#config-folder), and runs its commands: `--stop`, `start`, `restart`, `install`, `autostart`, `config`, `supervise`, `report` for session reports, `attach` for the [attach client](#attach-client), `holder` to list and stop held sessions, and `web` for [web page](#web-page) links and [remote access](#remote-access) pairing. Flags move or turn off its fixed ports, as tests do, and shorten the Code Mode idle limit for tests and live acceptance, which the hub then logs.
- **Terminal holder** (PRD R94, R95, [Terminal holder](#terminal-holder)): `HolderLink` in `packages/hub/src/holder.ts` finds or starts the holder. The flow ends the sessions of a holder that is gone and is told of a held session past the idle limit.
- **Runtime:** the hub opens the store, loads settings and playbooks and reloads them on change, and polls each enabled source on its interval. After a poll it reads `git worktree list` in the source's repo root for the branch convention and links only tickets that have no worktree yet; entries git marks `prunable`, such as a deleted directory, count neither for the convention nor for report validation. While any session has started it reads the agents' rosters and hook markers. Before a roster read or a release stop can end a setup session, it reads the convention again, so an unreported worktree still counts.
- **Startup:** the hub checks that `sessions.nodePath` runs, for each agent with the PATH that agent gets, and shows a failure with the hook errors, because hooks and session reports run that Node.js and a hook that cannot start fails silently; a change to the Node.js or agent paths checks again. It installs each agent's hooks into the operator's own Claude and Codex settings when `sessions.installHooks` is on, runs again any triage it abandoned, and asks each session's runner about every session still `starting` (PRD R43). A runner adopts a start only on an unambiguous match, so the hub never takes over or stops a process it cannot identify: an unconfirmed or ambiguous start stays owned with a visible warning, reports from it are refused until its identity is confirmed, and Stop session clears an overdue start with no matching process without stopping anything. Then it reads the roster.
- **Platforms:** Windows and Linux run the hub, the web page and the browser extension (PRD § Scope). Source commands run through Git Bash on Windows and `bash` elsewhere, and hook scripts run with `node`. Paths compare without case or separator differences on every platform, so on Linux two paths that differ only in case count as one.
- **Home override:** `--home=<dir>` on every `hub.js` command names the directory that holds `.switchboard`, in place of the user home, and the config folder is then the one that home's config.json names, else that `.switchboard`. It does not change the user profile that Claude and `gh` see. Tests and live acceptance depend on it.

### Config folder

The hub owns the config folder, so it does not depend on what started the hub. `~/.switchboard/config.json` holds `configDir`, an absolute path, and the hub's start and every `hub.js` command that needs the folder read it there. `readHubConfig` in `packages/core/src/hubConfig.ts` and `loadConfigFolder` in `packages/hub/src/configFolder.ts` are the authority.

- **Import.** While there is no config.json, the first start, or `hub.js config`, imports the folder once and writes config.json (`hub.js install` and `hub.js autostart on` too, but not a dry run): from the start's `--config`; else the running hub's folder, so an upgrade keeps the folder the hub runs on; else VS Code's user setting `switchboard.configDir`, which the retired VS Code extension kept, read without changing it from the user `settings.json` (`Code/User` under `%APPDATA%` on Windows, `~/Library/Application Support` on macOS and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `~/.config` elsewhere); else the Switchboard home. Only the user's own home reads the environment's VS Code settings; another home, such as a test's, looks under itself, so it never imports the operator's folder.
- **config.json wins.** A start whose `--config` names another folder uses config.json's and logs that it ignored the flag, so a stale login entry or a restart's arguments cannot move the hub to a folder without the operator's playbooks. A config.json that is not JSON or names no absolute `configDir` refuses the start, for the same reason, rather than being imported over.
- **Changing it.** `hub.js config` shows the folder and where it came from, and `hub.js config get configDir` prints it alone. `hub.js config set configDir <folder>` takes an absolute path or one starting with `~`, the home, as the setting does; refuses a relative path, a missing one and one that is not a folder, changing nothing; writes config.json; and restarts a running hub that reads another folder, as `hub.js restart` does.
- **Shown.** The Advanced page of the web page's Settings names the folder and the command that changes it.

## Rows

- The row rules (groups and their order, each row's controls, click, time, mark and texts) live in `packages/hub/src/rows.ts` and the core's `group.ts`, which import only types and browser-safe modules, so the web page's list and its History tab draw the same rows from the hub's view and have no rules of their own. `dockView` in `dock.ts` builds the list's view model from them, with each row's window ([Web page](#web-page), Open in VS Code), pastes, links and Done date bands, the snooze choices (PRD R49), the sources, repo roots and schedules; the page's rows are built from it (`pageList` and `pageRow` in `page.ts`). It and `DockWindow`, `DockTerminal` and `dockRowOf` keep the name of the retired dock they first served. `packages/hub/test/fixtures/dock-view.json` is a recorded view that the hub's tests compare `dockView` with.
- Groups follow PRD R27, in the order Ready for review, Blocked on you (only when it has rows), In progress, Unstarted, Snoozed, Done. A work item belongs to the first that applies: Done; Unstarted while unstarted, triaging or failed at triage; Ready for review once it has been ready, even while it runs again, waits, asks a question, blocks or fails; Blocked on you while it is blocked, failed or waiting on a question; Unstarted while queued; else In progress. Ready for review is ordered by the operator's position, else by when the work item became ready (PRD R73); Blocked on you and In progress by when the wait or the run began.
- **Turn.** Blocked on you is on the operator's turn, and so is a work item in Ready for review or In progress that blocks on the operator, awaits approval or has finished; the others there are on the agent's turn. Unstarted and Done rows have no turn, and neither does a hosted conversation whose session is idle between turns, because an open conversation between turns is not working. An unsnoozed work item on the operator's turn needs the operator: the [needs-you alerts](#needs-you-alerts) follow it.
- **Controls.** Each row has at most one main control, the action that row most needs: open a session where it waits, Start, Approve, Retry, or Triage again; a row has none when its click already opens what it needs, and In progress and Done rows have none. Every applicable control is in the row's menu, and hub controls carry the flow event the page's `row-action` posts ([Web page](#web-page), Row actions). Ready for review rows can be moved within the group and reset to their ready order (PRD R73).
- **Waiting questions** (PRD R103): the page's row carries how many of its work item's questions are open, the conversation chip's count of its latest session, and 0 when that session's conversation is not `full` or the work item is done. The hub keeps each session's count per conversation revision, so a list build reads no conversation that has not changed, and after a question change, such as an answer, a dismissal or a withdrawal, sends each page its list again when its rows changed for that page. The row shows them as the question icon alone, with no count, on line 1 after the title, which alone gives up room, so line 2's pills never push it out; its accessible name and tooltip say how many (the operator's decision, 2026-11-25).
- **Marks and details.** The row mark shows what differs within the group, with its meaning in the tooltip (PRD R27), and `markOf` in `group.ts` decides it; the glyph and icon tables beside it are shared by every surface, so each draws the same mark. A queued row names what it waits for, from `queueWaits` in `packages/core/src/queue.ts`, the same check the scheduler uses before it starts it. Rate-limited rows stay on the agent's turn ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)).
- **Time** (PRD R27, R53): each group measures from its own start: Blocked on you from the block or wait, Ready for review from `readyAt`, In progress from the run's start, Unstarted and Snoozed from the ticket's scope time, and Done from the work item's scope time to its end. A run that waits or moves from setup to running stays one run, and time spent queued is not counted. A done work item keeps its own copy of the scope time, because the ticket's moves when the ticket returns.
- **State staleness** (PRD R58) is computed from the latest of the work item's latest state, the run's start and the time the session last began running. Line 3 dims a state whose turn ended and names one whose session ended the last state ([States](#states)). The hub sends a view at least every minute (the `tick` timer), so ages stay current without other events.
- The Unstarted group holds unstarted, triaging and failed-at-triage work items, queued work items not yet ready whose live session does not wait on the operator, and pool tickets with no open work item, of enabled sources only, sorted together by importance, then oldest first. A pool ticket can be started only once its triage has a result, and the core refuses a pick without one.
- **Pool.** The page's `pool-action` posts `pick` with `{ticketId, guidance?}` and `retriage-pool` with a `ticketId` ([Web page](#web-page), Row actions).
- **Snooze** (PRD R49): a `snooze` action carries the ticket, a wake time or null for indefinite, and an optional comment; the hub refuses a malformed one with 400 and the core refuses a wake time that has passed with 409. The choices come from `snoozeChoices` in `packages/core/src/snooze.ts`, which the browser extension also uses, so the page and the board offer the same times. The core ends every due snooze at the start of each event, and the hub's `tick` makes a wake need no other event. Every end of a snooze clears its comment, and a new snooze replaces the old one. A poll that finds a ticket neither assigned nor in the pool ends its snooze.
- **Source toggles** (PRD R3): the page's `page-source {name, enabled}` turns a source on or off in the store, not in settings.json, and rebuilds the polls. A configured source with no stored state is off, and loading settings forgets sources they no longer list, so a source removed and added again starts off. A toggle sends `config` to every page, so an open Settings reads its source states again. An unknown source, or `adhoc`, is refused with 409. A disabled source is not polled and its pool is hidden; its work items stay.
- **Refresh:** the page's `page-poll` polls each enabled source now and restarts its interval; a source with a read in flight is not read again. With no source built it answers 409 with each enabled source's build error, or says no source is on.

- **Pull requests** (PRD R92): a source that can read pull requests (the GitHub source) reads, after each of its successful polls, the pull request links of every ticket with a work item not done, whatever the ticket's source, skipping one it last read as merged or closed. The hub keeps the results, state, review decision and checks, in memory by URL and adds them to the snapshot's links. A page coming to the front reads them again, rate-limited and queued behind any read of the same source; a failed read is logged and keeps what was read. No webhooks.

### Needs-you alerts

PRD R65. The hub compares each snapshot with the one before and announces the work items that started needing the operator on one surface, never after a hub start's first build. `alerts.surface` picks it: among the pages that may notify, `auto` the installed page used last and `web` that or else any such page used last; with no such page, and for `system`, a system notification. A settings.json that still says `dock` loads with a warning and works as `auto`.

- **On a page.** The page gets `needs {items: [{workItemId, message}], sound}` on its conversation stream and shows the last message in its toast, neutral, whose click selects that work item, and a browser notification per work item, whose click selects it ([Web page](#web-page)); `sound` is set only when the voice played no chime and spoke no alert for it, so one event makes one sound.
- **System notification.** The hub shows it itself (`systemAlerts.ts`), one per work item, with the ticket title and the mark's tooltip apart and sounding under the same rule; its click opens the local page's URL at `#w=<id>`, so it needs nothing running to answer it. On Windows it is a WinRT toast from Windows PowerShell, since PowerShell 7 cannot load WinRT types; the script is fixed and reads the toasts as data on its standard input, so no ticket title is parsed as PowerShell and no number of alerts reaches the command line's length limit. The toast is under the app ID `Switchboard`, which the helper registers under `HKCU\Software\Classes\AppUserModelId` so Windows shows the toast without a Start menu shortcut; its protocol activation opens the URL with the default browser. On Linux it is `notify-send --action --wait`, whose Open runs `xdg-open`, shown again without the action where notify-send predates libnotify 0.7.9; it runs in a shell detached from the hub, because GNOME keeps a notification in its message tray, and a click after the hub restarts must still open the page. Only a shell that cannot start is logged. On macOS it is `osascript`, whose notification opens nothing when clicked. A helper that fails is logged, except on Linux as above, and the alert is not retried.

## Browser extension

`extensions/chrome` is a Manifest V3 extension (PRD R56, R125, R123, R127) that the operator loads unpacked. On GitHub project boards and tables it hides snoozed cards, shows each card's playbook and status, and adds Switchboard's items to a card's menu; on issue and pull request pages it hides the sidebar and widens the content on request; in Chrome it also hosts embedded link tabs and keeps external links in a Switchboard tab group.

`extensions/firefox` builds the same source for Firefox and changes only what Firefox requires ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M52); the build fails when Chrome's manifest or stylesheet no longer has the shape it adapts.

- **Firefox differences.** Firefox runs a background script in place of the service worker, needs an add-on ID, and matches nothing with a host pattern that has a port, so its host permission covers every port of the loopback host. It has no key-derived ID and no messages from web pages, so it has no embedded link tabs and no tab group for the page's links, and asks for none of their permissions. Firefox lists MV3 host permissions as optional; until the operator grants them, the board shows nothing.
- **Firefox distribution.** Release Firefox keeps only add-ons that addons.mozilla.org signed, so the build is signed as an unlisted add-on, which is not published, and each signed version is kept as a release of the private GitHub repository at the commit it was built from. addons.mozilla.org signs each version once, so each build's version is Chrome's with a build number added, and signing refuses a version already released or lower than one released. Firefox cannot download from the private repository, so the add-on does not update itself. The `@switchboard/firefox` workspace's scripts sign, release, run a development copy and package an unsigned build.

- **Hub access.** A content script on github.com cannot read a loopback address, so the service worker makes every hub request. The hub serves the extension on a fixed loopback browser endpoint, because an extension cannot read `hub.json`. The endpoint sends no CORS headers, so web pages cannot read it, and refuses a Host other than the loopback address, against DNS rebinding. Its routes are `GET /snoozed` (the snoozed tickets' URLs), `GET /cards` (each ticket's card: playbook, group, mark, the row's stripe colour and whether it is snoozed, PRD R67), `POST /snooze` and `POST /unsnooze` (PRD R66), and `GET /iframe`. Host permissions cover loopback and GitHub for the iframe host; a copy for another device adds its page's origin.
- **Another device** (PRD R121; [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M128). apps/hub's standalone build carries the build (`chrome` in `apps/hub/dist/install`), and an install puts it in `<home>/web/chrome/` beside the page's other files. The signed-in page downloads it from `/page/switchboard-extension.zip` as one `switchboard-extension` folder. A paired device's copy adds `remote.json` with the page's HTTPS origin, a host permission for that origin, and that origin to `externally_connectable`, so the page there can hand it links for the [tab group](#browser-extension); this computer's copy is the build as it is. With `remote.json` the service worker reads and posts `/v1/browser/snoozed`, `cards`, `snooze` and `unsnooze` on that origin instead of the loopback endpoint. They are page routes: the request passes the remote access checks and needs the page's sign-in, which Chromium sends with an extension's requests to a host it has permission for, `SameSite=Strict` as it is, so the copy holds no secret and works only in the browser that paired; a post must also come from an extension's `Origin` with a JSON body, as on the endpoint. Open in Switchboard on such a copy opens the card's work item on the page (`/page/#w=<id>`, from the card's `workItemId`) in a new tab. Firefox would need its signed build, which cannot take a file per hub, so the page offers only the Chrome build.
- **Failure contract.** A failed snoozed read counts as none snoozed. A failed cards read, including a 404 from an older hub, removes every pill and adds no menu items. A card with an unknown group, mark or stripe fails the read; one with no mark or stripe, as from an older hub, draws without it.
- **Snoozed cards** (PRD R56). The content script acts only on a page with a project board or table, and hides the snoozed cards of each column or group behind a toggle that shows them. GitHub renders only the cards and rows in view and empties the rest, so the script remembers each card's issue from when it last rendered, counts a group's snoozed rows from what it has seen in the current view, and leaves a card it has never seen rendered shown.
- **Pills** (PRD R67). Each card or row whose issue matches a card from `/cards` shows a pill with the playbook, or the group when there is none, and the work item's mark. The hub computes group and mark with core's `groupOf` and `markOf` (`packages/core/src/group.ts`), so the board and the page's list follow one rule; that module imports only types, so a browser bundle imports it as `@switchboard/core/group` without the store.
- **Card menu** (PRD R66, R69). The menu that GitHub opens for a card or table row gains **Open in Switchboard** for a ticket `/cards` lists and, outside Done, **Snooze in Switchboard…** or **Unsnooze in Switchboard**. The items follow each accepted cards read while the menu is open. Snooze choices come from core's `@switchboard/core/snooze`, which keeps Node modules out of the browser bundle. After a snooze or unsnooze succeeds the board and pills read again; a failure shows the hub's error.
- **Open in Switchboard** (PRD R69). The menu item, or a click or Enter on a pill, opens the web page at the ticket's work item, `<page origin>/page/#w=<work item ID>`, or `/page/` for a pool ticket or a card the hub has not sent, in a new tab from the click, before GitHub would open its side panel. The page origin is this computer's, the first origin the hub's `GET /iframe` vouches for, read ahead by the service worker because an extension cannot read `hub.json`, and read again every 30 seconds, when the tab shows and after each click, because the page's port is a setting, or the remote page's for a copy another computer's hub gave; before the hub has answered, the click says Switchboard is not answering.
- **Issue and pull request layout** (PRD R127; [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M156, M157). The settings live in github.com's own `localStorage`, not `chrome.storage`, because the Firefox build asks for no `storage` permission and a content script at `document_start` must read them synchronously, before GitHub paints. That script sets an attribute on `<html>` for each setting that is on, on an issue or pull request path only; `content.css`, which the browser injects before the page's DOM, hides the sidebar and lifts the width limits under those attributes. Once the DOM is parsed, the same script keeps the attributes and the buttons current as GitHub renders and moves between pages without a reload, and follows a change another tab or frame saves. The layout script and `content.css` run in every github.com frame (`all_frames`), because a content script otherwise runs only in a tab's top frame and an embedded link tab (PRD R125) is a frame of the extension's host page; the extension's host permission for github.com keeps that frame's `localStorage` the tab's, so a setting applies in both. The board's content script still runs in the top frame only. The rules match GitHub's hashed class names by prefix and its `data-testid`s, so a GitHub redesign can stop them silently; the layout fixtures record what they match.
- **Page updates.** A pass over the page changes only what differs, so it does not trigger its own mutation observer and a focused pill or menu item keeps its focus.
- **Switchboard tab group** (PRD R123, Chrome only; [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M135). The web page sends the extension, by its fixed ID through `externally_connectable`, `{type: 'links'}` when it starts and each time it shows again, and the extension answers `{links: true}`; while it has that answer and no embedded link tab takes the link, the page sends each http or https link it opens as `{type: 'open', url, until}` instead of opening a tab, and opens the tab itself, as without the extension, when the answer is not `{opened: true}` or does not come within 2 seconds (`LINK_ANSWER_MS`), inside the about five seconds Chrome keeps a click's activation for a new tab; after a missed answer it opens tabs itself until the extension answers the next probe. `until`, half a second before the page stops waiting, on the clock the page and the extension share, is the last moment the extension may start the link: one whose turn comes later, or whose message arrives later, is refused unopened, so a slow open seldom lands beside the page's own tab, though one already started can still finish after it. The extension hears only its own page: a loopback page for this computer's copy, the origin in `remote.json` for another device's. It opens one link at a time, so a second finds the tab the first made. The group is the one titled `Switchboard` in the last focused normal window, else in any window, else a new cyan one in that window, or in a new window when the browser has none. A link loads in the group's tab of its kind (`tabKind` in `src/tabs.ts`: GitHub issue, GitHub pull request, else host and first path segment), which only comes to the front when it shows that URL already; otherwise a new tab joins the group; either way the tab and its window come to the front. The kind a tab was opened for is kept in `storage.session`, one key per tab, because a site such as Zoho Desk redirects a link to another address; it is dropped when the tab closes or leaves the group, and a tab with none, dragged in or kept from before a browser restart, has its address's kind. The `tabGroups` permission finds and names the group and `tabs` reads its URLs.
- **GitHub's DOM.** The selectors are GitHub's, not a contract; the fixtures in `extensions/chrome/test/fixtures` record them, with the date each was captured.

## Store

- One SQLite database, `~/.switchboard/switchboard.db`, opened through Node's built-in `node:sqlite` (M1). No native module, so a Node.js upgrade requires no rebuild.
- The hub is the only process that opens the database; its snapshot worker thread reads it through a read-only connection. The web page, the `hub.js` commands and the report command go through the hub's HTTP server, so there is no cross-process locking.
- WAL mode; one write transaction per flow step, so a crash leaves each work item in its last committed state.
- Core accesses the database only through a store interface, which isolates changes to the `node:sqlite` API. Tests use `:memory:`.
- Schema changes are numbered migrations applied by the hub at startup, recorded in `PRAGMA user_version`.
- Activity log (PRD R59): `events` holds each flow event that wrote a row or was refused, with its time, type, actor (`operator`, `session`, `source`, `triage` or `hub`), the ticket, work item and session it names, its detail and any refusal; `changes` holds each row it wrote, with each changed field's old and new value. Each change is logged in the transaction that writes it. Operator actions, reports, failed starts and failed stops are stored even when they change nothing; other events only when they change a logged field. Details leave out prompts, contexts and polled lists, except a start's prompt when the operator changed it (PRD R77); fields that change on every poll or status change, and the order fields, are not logged, and long text is cut. A refusal the flow never sees, such as a report that fails validation, is logged by the hub with only the report's kind and short fields, never a prompt; a refusal naming no known ticket is not stored. The tables have no foreign keys, so a log outlives the rows it describes, and are kept forever except that Delete purges a work item's activity. The log began with its migration, which recorded what the existing rows showed as reconstructed events. A per-ticket revision counter, advanced by every write to its log, lets a reader tell whether its copy is current.
- A Code Mode session's conversation (messages, replies, states and their speech state), its transcript readers' checkpoints and the web page's sign-ins (`pageSessions`, [Web page](#web-page), [Remote access](#remote-access)) are written outside the activity log ([Conversation](#conversation)).
- Which sources are on is in the `sourceStates` table, because the page's list header toggles it (PRD R3).
- Not in the database: settings and playbooks, in `settings.json`, which the operator edits and can keep in git; session transcripts, which Claude Code stores, and which sessions find by `nativeSessionId`; and the [debrief](#friction-debriefs) state and log.

## Repo roots

PRD R16, R21. `packages/core/src/repo.ts` owns a ticket's folder. A ticket records its repository, `owner/name` in lowercase, or none, which means its source's home repository, the source's `repo` setting. A ticket's root is, in order: the ticket's own `repoRoot`, as an ad-hoc, schedule or reported ticket has; the source's `repoRoot` with `{owner}` and `{name}` filled in, when it holds `{name}`; that `repoRoot` for the home repository when it does not; otherwise an error naming the setting and repository. Two repositories, in any sources, that resolve to the same folder, compared as Windows compares paths, are refused for both, so neither runs in the other's clone; a refused root fails the start even when the ticket has a linked worktree. Session start, the R68 task report's default and allowlist, the R6 repo list (each home root and each stored repository's root, never a pattern) and worktree linking use this one rule. A source's `repoRoot` may hold `{name}` and `{owner}` once each, `{owner}` only with `{name}`; `sources.adhoc.repoRoot` and a schedule's `repoRoot` name one folder. Switchboard never clones: when a pattern gave the folder, a start fails with `No clone of <repo> at <path>.` when it is not a checkout, and any other missing folder fails the start.

## Playbooks

A playbook is an entry under `playbooks` in settings.json, keyed by its name, `<namespace>:<name>`; `template` holds its prompt template and the other keys its settings. Switchboard has no built-in namespaces or playbooks; the entries define them.

```json
"playbooks": {
  "support:defect": {
    "description": "Zoho ticket reporting behavior that may be a defect",
    "autoStart": false,
    "requiresWorktree": false,
    "permissionMode": "auto",
    "reports": ["done"],
    "template": "Research Zoho ticket {{ticket.sourceKey}}.\n{{ticket.context}}\nReport progress with `{{report}} stage {{session.id}} <stage>`."
  }
}
```

- Template variables are the ticket's source, key, ref, repository, number, title, URL and context, the session ID and `{{report}}`, the report command prefix (`TEMPLATE_VARIABLES` in `packages/core/src/template.ts`). Rendering fails on an unknown variable.
- Keys (`parsePlaybook` in `packages/core/src/playbook.ts` is authoritative): `description` and `template` are required. `kind` is `task` or `triage`. A task playbook sets whether it starts on its own (`autoStart`) and needs a worktree (`requiresWorktree`), its agent (`provider`, PRD R22) with permission mode and model, the reports its sessions may send beyond the defaults (`reports`), whether Approve sends the session's work (`approvalSends`, only with the `approval` report), which other playbooks count toward Returned (`returnOf`, PRD R51), the page triage's focus must be on (`focus`, PRD R79), and whether it runs in the ticket's worktree or always in the repo root (`workdir`). A triage playbook sets whether its classifier rates immediacy and severity (`rates`). Omitted keys take defaults, a playbook's agent settings from that agent's `sessions` settings. Any other key, or a value of the wrong type, leaves that playbook out with its error while the rest load.
- `description` tells the classifier when to choose the playbook, so adding a playbook is adding its entry. Edit the triage playbook only for a rule that must take precedence over the descriptions.
- A source's triage playbook has `kind: triage`. Its body is the classifier's system prompt and may use only `{{playbooks}}`, which renders the choices as `- name: description` lines; a body without it gets the list appended. Triage playbooks are never offered as choices.
- Each source's `namespace` setting limits the classifier's choices to that namespace's task playbooks, such as `dev` for GitHub and `support` for Zoho Desk and source plugins.
- Report contract (PRD R23). Every session gets the report contract as system instructions apart from its prompt, so the session list shows only the prompt, and again after each compaction (mechanics M30, M31). The contract opens with the line `Report to Switchboard with these commands:`, which skills may look for, so that line does not change; it lists only the reports the session may send, with the session ID and each report's condition. A playbook's task session may send `stage`, `link` and the playbook's `reports`; with neither `done` nor `approval`, the contract tells it to end its turn when the work is finished, so the work item becomes `finished`, in Ready for review. Setup and approved-prompt sessions may send `stage`, `worktree`, `done` and `link`, and an ad-hoc prompt all but `playbook`. The contract leaves out `task`, which the hub still accepts, so a session sends one only through a skill the operator invokes (PRD R68). The hub refuses any report the session's contract does not offer, and the report command exits with code 3.
- Questions and approvals. A Claude session that needs the operator asks with `AskUserQuestion` and waits, which keeps its context live and puts the row in Blocked on you; a Codex session, which cannot wait mid-turn, sends the `question` report and ends its turn. When the work is ready but publishing it needs the operator's approval, the session reports `approval` with the prompt that would publish it, a summary and any drafted file, and ends its turn; Approve runs the prompt in a new session with no memory of this one, so the prompt names what it acts on. Before an approval, a session offered `paste` reports each drafted text the operator pastes into the source by hand.
- Link checks (PRD R52). A session reports a `link` for each GitHub issue or pull request that tracks or delivers the work, not a related, base or parent one. When the ticket's source can check a link, the hub asks it first: the GitHub source refuses a pull request in its repository that neither closes the issue nor names it in its head branch, and one GitHub cannot resolve. A check that fails or takes too long accepts the link with a log line.

## Triage

Triage follows Ground Control's classifier (GC R38, M31).

1. The source plugin builds the triage context: the ticket's text, each field clipped at a word boundary, with authors, status changes and assignees. The hub puts the current time before the context, so the triage playbook can tell how recent a request is.
2. The classifier runs on `sessions.provider`, with `triage.model` as an optional override; a model name known to belong to the other provider is dropped with a log line, and unknown names pass through. The run has no tools, settings, MCP servers or saved transcript, and a Codex run also no project instructions, web search, apps or hooks; it gets the triage playbook as its system prompt and the context on stdin, which avoids the Windows command-line length limit (M31). Skipping the user's settings also skips the model endpoint they configure, so the hub carries that endpoint into the run: for Claude, the routing and proxy variables of the user settings' `env` block (M3), not model variables; for Codex, the model provider and endpoint from `config.toml` (M42). A missing file carries nothing; one that cannot be read or parsed fails the triage without running it, because the run would bypass the operator's endpoint. A failed run does not fall back to the other provider.
3. The result schema is `{playbook}`, an enum of the source namespace's task playbook names, plus immediacy and severity when the triage playbook has `rates: true` (PRD R50), and `focus` when the source returns anchors (PRD R79). Core's `validateClassification` checks both providers' results; without `rates` any rating is discarded, and a rating outside its enum is stored as empty.
4. The focus (PRD R79): a source's context can tag pages and comments with anchors, each with its URL and page; the GitHub source tags the issue, its pull request and each comment, and Zoho Desk returns none; each external plugin decides which anchors it supplies. A choice whose playbook has `focus` names its page in the choices. The flow stores the chosen anchor when it is on the playbook's page or the playbook names none, else the playbook's page anchor, else the ticket's.
5. Nothing new (PRD R80): the `triage` action carries when an earlier work item of the ticket handled its activity, and with which playbook: the start of its first task session that started, or when the operator marked it done without one, or a handled-assignment registration (PRD R85). A source can report the time of the latest activity by anyone but the operator that can ask for an action; the GitHub source counts comments, reviews and the head commit in the context, and Zoho Desk reports none; external plugins may report their own activity. The hub tells the classifier about the earlier work, so earlier activity is background. When no activity is later than the earlier work, the triage is stale: the work item stays `unstarted` whatever the playbook's `autoStart`, with an error the row shows and Start clears, and History shows it.

Triage has no tools, settings, MCP servers or saved transcript, so it is not a session and does not count toward the one-session-in-a-turn rule (PRD R17). The hub runs at most one triage per work item at a time, at most `triage.concurrency` at once, each within `triage.timeoutSeconds`.

A classifier error, an unparsable result, a timeout, or a playbook name outside the enum fails the work item with that error (PRD R37). A triage interrupted by a hub shutdown is not a failure: the work item stays `triaging`, and triage runs again when the hub starts.

## Flow

1. Poll a source. For each ticket, compare its assigned state with `Ticket.assigned`. A poll that fails or is incomplete, such as one that hits the page limit, updates nothing.
2. When a ticket enters the assigned state, create a work item (`triaging`) and run triage.
3. Store the triage result. If the playbook's `autoStart` is false, or the triage found nothing new (PRD R80), the work item is `unstarted` until the operator starts it. `awaiting-approval` is used only for an approval report, and Approve applies only to it.
4. If the playbook's `requiresWorktree` is true and the ticket has none, start a setup session running the source's `setupPrompt`. Setup succeeds when a worktree is linked, by report or by the branch convention, and the session's process ends or, for a released session, its turn ends, since an interactive tab keeps the process. A Code Mode setup session (PRD R95) also succeeds at a turn end with a worktree linked, since its process runs between turns, unless the turn stopped at a usage limit ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)). A process end with no worktree linked while the ticket is assigned fails the work item with "Setup ended without a worktree.", or blocks it when the session stopped at a usage limit ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)); a turn end without one leaves it in `setup`, and a later resume of the session returns a failed or blocked one to `setup`.
5. Render the playbook and start the task session (`running`). The operator's guidance from Start follows the rendered prompt after a blank line; an approved prompt takes none (PRD R77). The session sets its state, reports a worktree link if it creates one, and done or approval.
6. When the task session reports done, the work item is done with reason `session`, unless the hub's read of the ticket still lists it as assigned; then a second read settles it, done, or `blocked` when the ticket is still assigned (PRD R10). When its turn or process ends with no report, the work item is `blocked` with the missing report as its reason, or `finished` when the prompt offered neither done nor approval. A turn that fails blocks it with the error, because the session can still be resumed. A `blocked` or `finished` work item whose session runs again becomes `running`. A Code Mode or Codex app-server task turn that ends while its conversation holds an open question leaves the work item `running`, waiting on the operator, unless the session reported, the ticket left the assigned state, an approval is held for the turn or the work item is a hosted conversation; the turn settles as above once no question is open, answered, withdrawn or dismissed, or when the session ends (PRD R103). A message that answers no question leaves the wait in place for the next turn's end; a dismissal outside a turn ends it at once.
   - When a session of an `awaiting-approval` work item runs again, as when the operator answers its question or resumes it, the work item becomes `running` and holds its approval. A new approval or done report from that run applies as usual. A turn end, a process end or an operator stop with no report returns it to `awaiting-approval` with the held approval, and the cause of a failed turn or a stop as its error. Only Approve, or Retry after the approved run failed to start, runs an approval's prompt. A run whose `running` state Switchboard never sees, such as a resume between two roster reads with hooks off, holds nothing.
7. When a ticket leaves the assigned state, an open work item that never had a session is deleted with its triages and pastes, and one with no active session is done with reason `unassigned` and no warning. A sessionless work item whose triage is running is marked instead, so the triage keeps its slot, and is deleted when the triage ends unless the ticket returned. Work item IDs are never reused, so a late result for a deleted triage is ignored. With an active session, this waits until the session reports or ends (PRD R10).

Pool tickets (PRD R47, R48):

- When a poll shows a ticket newly in the pool, the core starts a pool triage for the ticket; its result or error stays on the ticket.
- A pick of a pool ticket whose triage has a result creates the work item with that playbook and the pool triage, and queues it as an operator start, skipping the wait in step 3. Switchboard does not claim the ticket (PRD R48).
- A ticket that enters the assigned state while it has an open work item gets no second work item; the claim made by the task session is seen this way.

An ad-hoc prompt creates a ticket and an `unstarted` work item, with the typed prompt as the task prompt and the chosen repo root as the working directory; Start then goes to step 5, and Start automatically queues it at once (PRD R6). A `task` report creates the same kind of ticket and work item as a follow-up of the reporting work item (PRD R68), and its origin outlasts the parent's deletion. A `register` event creates one `running` with the registering session already started, released and external, and no task prompt (PRD R81). A work item with no playbook is labelled `task`, `registered` or `ad-hoc` by its origin (`adhocLabel` in `group.ts`).

Schedules (PRD R82) live in `packages/core/src/schedule.ts` and run on each `tick`, before the event itself. Each schedule has a ticket of source `schedule` keyed by its name, assigned, with the schedule's repo root, kept in step with settings.json; History shows each change of the schedule's timing and its removal. When the latest time the schedule came due is after the last check, the walk logs a `schedule` event and, unless the ticket has an open work item, inserts a run: a work item with the schedule's playbook and no triage, `queued` when the playbook has `autoStart`, else `unstarted`, and `failed` with the cause when the playbook is missing or not a task playbook. A schedule that was moved or added again counts its times from then, so it does not run for a time before; a time that passed while the hub was stopped runs once. Run now refuses an unknown name and a ticket with an open work item, and otherwise queues a run. A run starts in step 5 in the ticket's repo root. Its done report is accepted without a source read, because no source holds the ticket (PRD R10).

Steps 4 and 5 start a session only when no other session of the ticket is `starting`, `running` or `waiting` and a slot is free; otherwise the work item is `queued` (PRD R17, R38). An `idle` or `failed` session, such as an approval session left open, holds nothing back, so a work item can have several live sessions. Its active session is its latest session while that has not ended; only the active session holds a slot, and only the latest session changes the work item's status.

## Core decisions

`packages/core` decides; the hub acts. `handle(store, event, context)` runs inside one store transaction, applies one event, and returns the actions for the hub to run, or a refusal for an operator action or report that does not apply. The context carries the clock, settings, loaded playbooks, the report command prefix and ID generators. The hub runs each action and sends its result back as an event, so every failure reaches the work item. The `FlowEvent` and `FlowAction` types in `packages/core/src/flow.ts` list every event and action; the table groups the main events by sender.

| Events | From |
|---|---|
| `poll`, `poll-failed` | A source read. `poll` lists the source's tickets from a complete read; a ticket missing from it has left the assigned state. `poll-failed` changes nothing. |
| `triage-succeeded`, `triage-failed`, `triage-cancelled` | The classifier. A cancelled triage runs again. A triage that cannot start, because the source's triage playbook is not set or loaded, no task playbook matches, or the triage prompt does not render, is stored as a failed triage with the error, so the work item counts as failed at triage. |
| `session-started`, `session-start-failed` | The runner's `start-session`. |
| `roster`, `marker` | A successful roster read, with each listed session's state and each session's newest live fork (PRD R78); a hook marker for a live session. The flow continues a session as its fork only when the session is its work item's latest; it then retires the copy's native ID and applies the fork's state. |
| `report` | The report command: `stage`, `worktree`, `done`, `approval`, `link`, `playbook`, `paste`, `question` or `task`. |
| `stop-succeeded`, `stop-failed` | The runner's `stop-session`, for an operator stop or a held session's end or an app-server thread's release. A result for a copy a fork retired changes nothing. |
| `register` | The register command (PRD R81): a Claude session the operator registers as an external `task` session of a new work item, offered the ad-hoc reports, which Switchboard does not stop. It refuses an empty title and a native ID that is already a session's. |
| `end-legacy` | Hub startup: ends each active session an earlier hub ran in Claude's background service or as `codex exec`, which nothing observes now (PRD R93, R107), settling its work item as a holder loss does, with the cause saying so. |
| `ticket` | The ticket report (PRD R83): moves a session's ad-hoc work item onto a source's ticket, with an optional playbook from the source's namespace. It refuses a session that is not its work item's latest, a work item that is not ad-hoc or is done, and a source ticket that has a work item, triage, worktree or link; a bare stored ticket is deleted and replaced. The ticket keeps its repo root, so later sessions start where the ad-hoc ones did. An external session takes the playbook's reports, because its contract is only in its conversation; a session Switchboard started keeps its own. |
| `worktree-found` | The branch convention. It links a worktree only to a ticket that has none. |
| `held-resuming`, `held-resumed` | Resuming an ended held session (PRD R95, R107). `held-resuming` stores the launch before the spawn, so a hub restart during the spawn can recognise it, and can first make an ended registered session held. `held-resumed` makes the session `idle` under that launch; outcome and work item status stand ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). |
| `holder-lost`, `held-idle` | The hub (PRD R94, R95, [Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). `holder-lost` ends each active session of a terminal holder that is gone: one in a turn or waiting blocks its work item, a starting one fails its start, and one whose turn had ended is released with its work item's status kept. `held-idle` ends a held session left idle and detached for the idle limit, like a release after its turn, and releases a Codex app-server thread's subscription; neither applies while the session waits on its usage limit. |
| `limit-found`, `limit-attempt`, `limit-skipped` | The hub ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)): a usage limit found at a turn end, an attempt to continue after it, and a wait the operator ended by typing. All are always logged. |
| `move`, `reset-position` | The web page (PRD R73): a manual place for a work item in Ready for review, open and unsnoozed, among the others, and a return to readiness order. |
| `comment` | The web page (PRD R87): a non-blank comment on any work item, open or done. |
| `adhoc`, `run-schedule`, `start`, `approve`, `mark-done`, `reopen`, `retry`, `stop`, `delete`, `pick`, `retriage-pool`, `snooze`, `restore`, `tick` | The web page and the browser extension; `tick` re-runs scheduling after settings or playbooks change, and every minute wakes due snoozes and checks the schedules. `stop` can name one Code Mode session of the work item (PRD R94), which ends that session without changing the work item when it is not the active one. `retry` (PRD R34) applies to a `failed` work item with no active session: one that failed at triage triages again; any other is queued with its playbook, approval and guidance. `reopen` (PRD R86) applies to a done work item that is its ticket's latest, with no open work item and no other session handling the ticket's assignment; a reopened work item that held an approval returns to it. `delete` (PRD R120) applies to any work item: it stops the active session, deletes the work item's triages, sessions, pastes and logged activity, detaches its follow-ups, and ends the ticket's snooze when the ticket has no work item left and is not in the pool. History keeps the delete event and any ticket-owned changes, such as a reported link or worktree; a later event for a deleted session is refused and not stored. |

Actions are `triage`, `start-session`, `stop-session` and `resume-session`. After every event the core starts triage for `triaging` work items, then pool triage for pool tickets without a result, up to `triage.concurrency` in all; then it starts `queued` work items in queue order while fewer than `sessions.concurrency` active sessions hold a slot, skipping a ticket that has another session `starting`, `running` or `waiting` (PRD R17). A work item's active session holds a slot while the work item is in `setup` or `running` and not waiting on a question, and its ticket is not snoozed. A task that follows a setup session starts when the setup session ends, without waiting for a slot, and for a Code Mode setup session when its turn ends with a worktree linked (PRD R95).

## Claude Code runner

Every Claude session Switchboard starts is held in the [terminal holder](#terminal-holder) ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions), PRD R93); `sessions.codeMode` only chooses whether it runs with Code Mode. `ClaudeRunner` in `packages/claude` follows the Claude sessions Switchboard does not hold, through `claude agents --json`: registered sessions the operator runs in their own terminal or editor (PRD R81), until they end. It starts and stops none, and it installs the Claude hooks every Claude session reads. Mechanics come from Ground Control (GC M IDs) and [mechanics.md](mechanics.md).

- **CLI.** Every `claude` command the hub runs (holder spawns, roster reads, triage, debriefs) and Open in terminal run `sessions.claudePath`, read at each use; an absolute path's directory goes first on that command's PATH. A command that cannot find the CLI says so and names the setting.
- **Environment.** Pass `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` through exactly as the hub inherited it, and never set it: setting it, even to `~/.claude`, moves where Claude reads folder trust (GC M62). Strip VS Code's `ELECTRON_*` and `VSCODE_*` variables and `NODE_OPTIONS`.
- **Permission mode.** `auto` by default. In 101 Ground Control runs to 2026-10-03, the 95 in `auto` never waited for or were refused a permission; the only permission stall was in manual mode. Questions (`AskUserQuestion`) stall in every mode and show as `waiting`. The operator answers them, so Switchboard neither disallows the tool nor keeps a Bash allowlist.
- **Trust.** Claude asks before it starts in a directory it has not trusted; a linked worktree inherits its main checkout's trust (GC M62). A held session waiting on that dialog starts waiting on it (PRD R41).
- **Followed sessions.** A registered session is active while the last successful roster read lists it in an interactive entry with a pid, and `ended` once a read that looked for it does not; its hook marker, else the roster's own state, gives its state. A background entry, of a session an earlier hub ran with `claude --bg`, is never followed. A failed read changes no session.
- **State.** Hook markers decide the observed state, following Ground Control's mapping (GC M2, M20); `markerState` in `packages/claude/src/hooks.ts` is authoritative. Its rules: a prompt, a resolved tool batch or an answered question or plan means `running`; a permission request, an elicitation, a question or plan, or a waiting notification means `waiting`, and a result of another tool does not end a wait, because a parallel tool can finish while one still waits for permission; `Stop` means `idle`, or `running` while background tasks remain, with a detail that counts the agents, shells and other tasks among them (mechanics M124); `StopFailure` means `failed` with its error, and `rate_limit` is a usage or rate limit ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)). A wait's detail tells a permission prompt from a question (mechanics M57).
- **Hooks.** With `sessions.installHooks` on (the default), the hub installs its hooks in Claude's user settings (`<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR or ~/.claude>/settings.json`). It preserves other entries, refuses to edit malformed JSON, adds missing entries at startup, and removes the entries of another Switchboard home whose hub does not hold its lock, never the profile's own `~/.switchboard`, so an acceptance hub that shares the operator's settings leaves the operator's hooks. Held sessions need the hooks, so with the setting off a Claude start fails; turning it on while the hub runs installs both agents' hooks, and turning it off stops reading markers and leaves the hooks installed.
  - The marker hook writes one marker per Claude session, `<home>/activity/<nativeSessionId>.json`, atomically, skips subagent events and debrief forks (`FRICTION_DEBRIEF=1`), always exits 0 and prints nothing; `SessionEnd` deletes the marker. A session that is killed or crashes never sends `SessionEnd`, so at start and once a day the hub deletes each marker last written over 7 days ago whose session ID, by file name or content, is no stored session's native or retired ID, and logs how many it deleted; a marker of a stored session is never deleted (`pruneMarkers` in `packages/claude/src/install.ts`).
  - The hub reads the markers every few seconds and soon after the folder changes, and every Claude session on the computer writes one, Switchboard's or not. So it keeps each file's marker with its size, mtime and file ID and reads again only a file whose stat changed, one written within 2 s of its last read, since a rewrite in the same file-system tick can keep its stat, or one that did not parse; the hook's rename over the marker gives it a new file ID, so a rewrite never goes unseen; the runners and checks of one marker read share one read of the folder (`readMarkers` and `markerPass` in `packages/claude/src/install.ts`, mechanics M165).
  - The contract hook runs synchronously on `SessionStart` for `compact` and `resume`, because compaction drops the contract from the system prompt (mechanics M30). It asks the hub's `POST /contract` and returns the answer as additional context, which Claude saves in the transcript, so later resumes keep it. After a compaction the hub answers the session's contract with the conversation rules for a held session; after a resume it answers only a session that needs the current conversation rules ([Conversation](#conversation)). For another session, or when the hub does not answer, the hook prints nothing.
- **Forks** (PRD R78, mechanics M36). On each roster read the hub looks for a fork of each registered work item's latest session: a live interactive copy in the session's project directory whose first assistant request ID is in the transcript of the session's current or a retired copy. A copy created after the current one is `newer`, and the newest such copy continues the session; a retired copy is `older`. A check reads only the transcripts in the copy's project directory, because reading every session's transcript would hold up the hub's requests, such as `/register`. `packages/claude/src/fork.ts` holds the rules.
