A report from an `older` copy is refused, and one from a `newer` copy is recorded and starts a roster read; the hub checks again just before it applies a report. A Codex session's report ignores the Claude session ID, which a Codex process can inherit.
- **Sessions from before held sessions** (PRD R93). At startup, before it reconciles starts, the flow ends each active Claude session that is neither held nor registered, an earlier hub's `claude --bg` session: a start in flight fails, a session in a turn or waiting blocks its work item, or returns it to `awaiting-approval` when its approval was held, saying Switchboard no longer runs background sessions, and one whose turn had ended leaves its work item as it was, except a setup session, whose end settles setup. Its process is stopped with `claude stop`, never `claude rm` (GC M33); a failed stop shows on its work item. Opening it resumes it in the holder as an [adopted session](#code-mode-sessions); until then the page treats it as an ended held session.
- **Opening a session** (PRD R29, R59). A held session opens on the web page, in its conversation and its Console tab ([Web page](#web-page)), and in the terminal app through Background sessions' Open in terminal, which runs its [attach client](#attach-client) (`/holder/open`, `openTerminal` in `packages/hub/src/launch.ts`). A registered session stays in the operator's own terminal or editor until it ends, and is then resumed in the holder as an [adopted session](#code-mode-sessions). Open in VS Code opens only the work item's workspace file or folder ([Web page](#web-page)), never a session: VS Code runs without Switchboard's extension, so nothing is queued for it and nothing claims.

### Code Mode sessions

Every Claude session Switchboard starts (PRD R93–R95). `CodeModeRunner` in `packages/claude/src/codeMode.ts` implements `SessionRunner` for Claude sessions with `Session.codeMode` set, which means held, through the [terminal holder](#terminal-holder)'s client and the same hook markers as `ClaudeRunner`; `Session.withoutCodeMode` marks a held session that runs without Code Mode. The runtime selects a session's runner by `Session.execution: "app-server"` ([Codex runner](#codex-runner)), else `Session.codeMode`, else its provider. Experiments: [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M63–M83, M148.

- **Setting.** The flow reads `sessions.codeMode` ([Settings](#settings)) for each new Claude session: on, the session runs in Code Mode; off, it runs as a plain interactive `claude` with `withoutCodeMode`, everything else the same. A start stores the Claude session ID and a launch ID on the `starting` session before the spawn, so a hub restarted during the start finds the process in the holder; a failed start clears them, so it is never resumed and Retry starts a new session. A resume keeps the session's own mode. Codex sessions are never held here. A held session needs `sessions.installHooks`; without it the start fails.
- **Command.** The holder spawns `claude --session-id <uuid> --permission-mode <mode> [-n <name>] [--model <model>] --append-system-prompt <sections> --settings <json> [<prompt>]` in the session's folder, with no shell and no `--bg`. Interactive conversations omit `-n`, so Claude can title them. The hub makes the UUID, so the Claude session ID is known before the start (mechanics M63). The appended prompt holds the contract; the "Code References in Text" and "Focus view in this editor" sections of the Claude VS Code extension's panel prompt, copied word for word so a session writes as the panel does; Switchboard's link section, which tells the session to start a relative link target with `./` because VS Code's terminal makes a relative path clickable only so (mechanics M83); and the conversation rules ([Conversation](#conversation)). `--settings` selects the classic renderer, whose finished output scrolls into the scrollback the screen copy reads, and Focus view, which the panel's Focus view section requires. The holder holds the process under the Switchboard session ID, which a Claude session ID change does not touch.
- **Environment.** With Code Mode, the hub's environment without VS Code's variables, `NODE_OPTIONS`, Claude's session markers, the variable that forces the full-screen renderer, and the gateway credentials, with `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE=1`, `SWITCHBOARD_LAUNCH=<launch ID>`, the session's artifact folder ([Artifacts](#artifacts)) and `TERM=xterm-256color`, because the holder emulates an xterm and without `TERM` Claude Code draws its prompt as `>`, which typing must also read for a session started before (mechanics M121). `NO_COLOR` and `FORCE_COLOR` are dropped, because without colour Claude Code draws its suggested prompt as plain text, which typing reads as a draft (mechanics M149); `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` passes through as inherited. User settings `env` outranks the process environment, so `--settings` points Claude at the direct API with blank credentials, to use the subscription sign-in (mechanics M64). An `apiKeyHelper` in user settings cannot be overridden, so Claude starts without Code Mode and the start check fails it. Nested Claude runs inherit the environment but not `--settings` (mechanics M77). Without Code Mode the environment keeps the credential variables and drops `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_CODE_MODE`, and `--settings` sets only the renderer and Focus view, so the session uses the operator's own API settings, gateway included (mechanics M148).
- **Start check.** The start succeeds when the holder's retained start of output shows `Code Mode (experimental) is on` (mechanics M63) and a marker of this session ID and launch exists. It fails, and the process is killed, when Claude prints `Code Mode (experimental) isn't available:` (mechanics M64, M71), with Claude's line as the cause, when a marker arrives while the output lacks the line that Code Mode is on, when the process exits, or when the check runs out of time. Whichever line comes first counts, since later output can quote either. A live process showing a dialog, or with no marker of its launch after a short wait, is waiting on a dialog (mechanics M70): the start succeeds with `startPrompt`, and the evidence is checked again at its first marker. A session without Code Mode has no line to look for: the launch's first marker starts it (mechanics M148).
- **Liveness and state.** The holder's `list`, read with each roster read, decides liveness: a session is alive while the holder lists its process live. A failed `list` fails only this runner's read. A held process is never classed as an interactive roster entry (mechanics M67). Markers count only with the session's ID and launch, so a killed launch's last marker is never read (mechanics M68). The background mapping applies, except: `SessionStart` `startup` means `running`, or `idle` for a session started with no prompt (mechanics M87); `resume`, `clear` and `fork` mean `idle`; and `compact` leaves the state as it was, because `/compact` typed in the terminal ends with no `Stop` while an automatic compaction runs mid-turn (mechanics M65, M68, M73). `AskUserQuestion` exists in Code Mode, so the contract keeps it (mechanics M65). While `sessions.installHooks` is off, a held session keeps its last state and its row says it is unobserved.
- **End rule** (PRD R95). A held session keeps running between turns: no turn end releases it, whatever its kind or its work item's state. The hub, not the holder, ends it, only through the idle end: no terminal attached and no input for `CODE_MODE_IDLE_MS` (1 hour, the PRD's limit) after the time of its turn-end marker, read in the same roster read so a turn that ended during it counts from then, or any state while it is unobserved because hooks are off; output counts only when the latest marker is no turn end, as with hooks off or after a compaction, because an idle Claude repaints its status line on its own, as for its update check, often enough that no observed idle session reached the limit (`heldIdleSince` in `packages/claude/src/codeMode.ts`, [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M161). A session's own schedules are not read: `ScheduleWakeup` waits at most an hour from its call, so its turn starts before the limit passes, and a `CronCreate` job, held only in Claude's memory, ends with the session (PRD R95). The held sessions' list shows the same idle time; never while it waits on its usage limit ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)). The end is deferred while a terminal is attached or attaching, and skipped when the session's marker shows a turn has started since; the end measures the idle limit again from the input, output and marker read then, and waits while it has not passed, since a turn can begin and end, or the operator type in a terminal attached meanwhile, after the read that found the session idle. A deferred end never holds back the next session's start, because R17 and R38 count only sessions running a turn. A release marks the session `releasing`, and `released` only once the holder confirms the exit, so no observation takes a pending end for an ended session. An idle held session costs about 430 to 480 MB working set and 690 to 760 MB private memory with the operator's MCP servers (mechanics M82).
- **Attach serialization.** Ending and attaching are serialized per session in the hub. `POST /attach {sessionId}` refuses an unknown session with 404 and a session that is not held and cannot be adopted with 409, then reserves the session against an end for a short time, so a release does not end a session whose terminal is connecting. The attach waits for every end or stop of the session in progress, answers at once while the holder lists the session live, `starting` included, so a start prompt can be answered, resumes an ended session, and refuses one still ending or starting. Concurrent attaches share one attempt, so a session is resumed once. The answer is `{port, token, session}` of the holder the hub is connected to.
- **Stop.** The holder's `kill` ([Ending a session](#ending-a-session)), offered while the holder lists the process live, after the turn too; the work item is then `blocked` "Stopped by you." (PRD R40). Delete stops a held session whenever the holder lists its process live, including a `starting` one and one the operator resumed through Open in terminal. A failed stop leaves the process listed as an orphan.
- **Resume.** `POST /attach` for an ended session applies the resume guard, then spawns `claude --resume <id>` in the holder with the start's environment, a new launch ID and no prompt, which reads as idle (mechanics M68), with the mode and model a start of that work item would use now. Claude resends the start's recorded system prompt until a compaction (mechanics M30), so the resume appends the panel, link and conversation sections and the contract hook restores the contract. The hub stores the new launch on the still-ended session before the spawn, so a hub that stops during the spawn finds the process under it and adopts it. When the session is deleted or changed during the spawn, the hub ends the new process and refuses the attach. A resumed session is `idle`; its outcome and its work item's status stand until a new turn. Never resume while the holder lists the session live, because resuming a live session starts a copy (mechanics M18).
- **Adopted sessions** (PRD R81, R93). An ended Claude session that was never held, a registered one or an earlier hub's background session, resumes in the holder and is held from then on; until then its row and the web page treat it as an ended held session with Resume. The hub refuses the resume for a session that is not its work item's latest, while Claude's roster lists the session with a process, as in the operator's window or Claude's background service, since a resume would run a second copy (mechanics M18), when that roster read fails, and for a session without a transcript, which `claude --resume` needs. Code Mode, the permission mode and the model come from the end of the transcript (mechanics M117), since Claude keeps a session in the mode it started in (mechanics M68, M119), else from the current settings. The resume puts the report contract first in the appended prompt, because a registered session's start never had it; a session that ran without Code Mode resumes without it, through the operator's own gateway and sign-in. Its conversation starts at that first resume: the transcript is read from where it ended then, because its earlier lines, often days of work in the operator's window, would otherwise all arrive as new messages and replies on every page that shows it. From then on it works as any held session.
- **Resume guard** (mechanics M69). A Bash command can outlive its `claude` outside its process tree. The hub keeps each held session's last-seen process tree, pids with creation times, and refuses a resume while any member is still running, naming the pids, or when the process table cannot be read. Stop session on the work item then ends those leftovers, one subtree root at a time so a reused pid is never ended. A process that left the tree is missed; a container that would end it is deferred (PRD Q10).
- **Holder loss.** When the holder's lock is free, the holder is gone, and the hub ends each active session it held with "The terminal holder stopped.", so interrupted work is never read as finished: a session in a turn or waiting on the operator blocks its work item, or returns it to `awaiting-approval` when its approval was held, as after a Stop; a session whose turn had ended, or that reported done, is released and its work item's status stands; a `starting` session fails its start. Nothing resumes it until the operator opens it. A holder whose record does not answer while its lock is held is a failed read, not a loss. Every holder lookup that finds the lock free applies the loss, so a start that replaces a gone holder does not hide it.
- **Identity** (PRD R78, R93). Held sessions are never followed as forks. A report whose Claude session ID is not the session's is refused, and the contract hook answers only the session's own ID. `/clear`, `/branch` and `/resume <id>` in the terminal give the same process and launch a new Claude session ID (mechanics M73): once the roster lists the held process, or a member of its tree, with that ID (mechanics M67), the session moves to the new ID and the old one is retired; a retired ID the held process resumes is followed back. A marker with the launch and another ID but no such start is a nested `claude -p` (mechanics M77) and changes nothing. `/register` refuses a session that runs in the holder for a work item.
- **Reconcile.** After a hub restart the runner applies the start check to each `starting` session from the holder's retained start of output; a session the holder does not hold fails. Held processes of no active session are orphans ([Visibility](#visibility-of-held-sessions)); none is killed.
- **Open and terminal** (PRD R95). A held session's terminal command, in every state, runs `<sessions.nodePath> "<home>/hub.js" attach <Switchboard session ID>` ([Attach client](#attach-client)), so the terminal and its reuse survive a Claude session ID change. The page's Console tab types into it, and Background sessions' Open in terminal opens it in the terminal app.
- **Exclusions.** Debriefs skip Code Mode sessions, because `claude -p --resume` refuses them (mechanics M74). Triage, the report command and the contract hook are unchanged (mechanics M65, M66).

## Codex runner

Every Codex session runs as a thread in Codex's app-server daemon (PRD R107): `AppServerRunner` in `packages/codex`, selected by `Session.execution: "app-server"`. A Codex session without it is an earlier hub's [exec run](#legacy-exec-sessions). A stop action carries the provider and execution method, because a deleted session's row is gone when its stop runs.

- **Daemon and connection.** One app-server client per hub is shared by the runner, the conversation adapter and the debrief scheduler. It starts Codex's daemon under the session's Codex home and connects through `codex app-server proxy`. The daemon owns process lifetime: closing the proxy or the hub never stops it, and Switchboard never stops, restarts or updates it. Transport errors reject outstanding calls without replaying them, and a new connection invalidates stale requests and subscriptions. Codex's home, sign-in and configuration are inherited; tests and live acceptance inject a separate home.
- **Threads.** A start creates a thread with its permissions, model, report contract, conversation rules, artifact environment and persona tone, and records the native thread ID before it submits the prompt with a client message ID. A promptless conversation start leaves the thread idle. Native thread and turn events decide state; hooks do not. Reads rejoin after a connection loss and confirm starts from history. An unconfirmed start stays `starting` with a visible cause and recovery controls, and its prompt is never submitted again automatically. A missing thread does not prevent other sessions being observed.
- **Lifetime.** A task thread whose turn ended stays subscribed until the idle limit of a held session passes with no typing in its [Codex terminal](#codex-runner), and is never released while it waits on its usage limit ([Rate limits](#rate-limits)); a message resumes it. Setup, done and superseded sessions release their subscription after a turn; a release does not interrupt newly started work. Stop session interrupts the named active turn and unsubscribes only that thread, leaving other sessions and the daemon running.
- **Resume.** A message to an ended app-server session that is its work item's latest resumes its thread with the start's folder, permissions, artifact environment and model, which a thread the daemon loads again from disk takes and a loaded thread keeps its own sandbox against (mechanics M95), then sends the message under the new launch, or gives it back unsent with why. Concurrent resumes share one attempt. A thread resumed by another client, as by Open in terminal's `codex resume --remote`, reaches `POST /contract` through Codex's `SessionStart` hook and is resumed the same way.
- **Sandbox on Windows.** Commands in Codex's sandbox cannot read the operator's stored sign-ins: `gh` needs `GH_TOKEN` passed through the Codex shell environment, and git needs OpenSSL with `gh` as its credential helper; a skill that needs more runs in `bypassPermissions`. Settings' Codex permission mode points here rather than carrying the steps.
- **Instructions.** The current instructions are injected as a developer context item when a resume needs an update and after a completed compaction, because resume overrides are ignored for loaded threads and compaction can drop injected updates (mechanics M92). The session stores the delivered instructions' hash and the compaction it followed, so unchanged instructions are not injected again on a restart, and history detects a compaction missed while disconnected.
- **Conversation.** `CodexConversationAdapter` imports native history and completed items into the shared conversation log, with no invented transcript lines, and leaves tool results out. Scope, history, events, delivery, unread, artifacts and speech stay in `ConversationService` ([Conversation](#conversation)). Each provider's adapter polls independently, so a slow Codex request cannot delay Claude reads.
- **Input and recovery.** `turn/start` accepts input at idle or during a turn, carrying the stored message ID as its client message ID. A known refusal or unsent write returns the draft; a transport failure never decides a thread's state. A lost response is reconciled from history, and unknown delivery stays visible as `uncertain`. Input is never replayed automatically. Selected skills and image mentions travel as native inputs; compaction is an API operation.
- **Requests.** Native requests, such as questions and command or file approvals, carry connection-scoped IDs and thread identity. Only a page showing the session can answer, through `POST /v1/conversation/answer`, and the adapter validates the pending request and the offered choice. Unsupported request types use the native terminal. Rejoining replays pending requests (mechanics M92).
- **Open and terminal.** An app-server session opens on the web page, in its conversation and its Console tab, which shows the [Codex terminal](#codex-runner) below. That terminal, and any the operator opens, joins the thread with `codex resume --remote <control socket> <thread>` against the same daemon. Switchboard never hands a live app-server thread to the Codex VS Code extension. Native terminals and the page can coexist.
- **Codex terminal in the holder.** For the web page's console ([Web page](#web-page)), the hub holds Codex's own terminal app on the session's thread in the [terminal holder](#terminal-holder), as a second client of the thread, started with `--no-alt-screen` so its finished output stays in the scrollback (mechanics M108). It starts when a console views a live app-server session whose thread has had a turn, since Codex cannot resume a thread before its first, and again after it exited. The hub still sends every message and answers requests through the API; what is typed in the terminal reaches the thread as from any client. The terminal keeps a released thread loaded, so it ends with every stop that leaves the thread, and the hub ends one still held for an ended or released session at its next holder read. Keys typed in it count as use for the idle release. Holder stops and the holder's restart end only the terminal, never the thread's turn.
- **Settings changed outside Switchboard.** Codex's terminal can change the thread's model, permissions and collaboration mode for every later turn, the hub's included (mechanics M108). Switchboard neither blocks nor reverts them, since the operator changes them on purpose; it compares each update with what the thread started with and notes in the conversation when the settings differ, and when they are back.

### Legacy exec sessions

Switchboard no longer runs `codex exec`. `CodexRunner` in `packages/codex` only installs Switchboard's marker and contract hooks in `<CODEX_HOME or ~/.codex>/hooks.json`, under the same rules as Claude's, and has `codex app-server` trust them, trusting only Switchboard's own entries; a change to `sessions.codexPath` or `sessions.nodePath` installs and trusts them again without a hub restart. Every Codex process the hub starts gets `CODEX_HOME` as the hub inherited it, else `~/.codex`, and runs `sessions.codexPath`. At startup the flow ends each active exec session an earlier hub started, as for Claude's background sessions ([Claude Code runner](#claude-code-runner)), saying to resume it with `codex resume`; its row's Open in terminal does that, and its conversation is not shown.

## Rate limits

PRD R111. A turn that stops at a usage or rate limit waits for its reset; evidence in [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M100.

- **Reading a limit.** For a Claude session whose turn ended `failed` with `rate_limit` or `idle`, the hub reads the current turn's limit from the end of its transcript (`packages/claude/src/limit.ts`): a usage limit or a throttle, and its reset, exact from the refused request's data or read from its text relative to the line's own time, so a later read gives the same reset. A grace-allowance note before a turn that ended normally is a usage limit without a reset. Claude Code's own wait line marks a session Claude Code will continue itself; because Claude writes it just after the `Stop` hook, the hub reads the transcript again for a recent turn end. For a Codex app-server thread, a failed turn's usage or rate-limit error gives the limit, and the account's rate-limit updates give its reset. A turn end that settles a work item carries its limit, so a grace-allowance setup is never finished before its limit is known.
- **Keeping it.** The flow stores `Session.rateLimit` (`packages/core/src/limit.ts`): the reading, when the run first hit it, the launch it was found in, the hub's attempts and the next attempt's time, a margin after the reset. A session waits on its limit while it has one and its state is `idle` or `failed`; a turn that ends without a limit clears it.
- **Its work item.** A held Claude session or app-server thread waiting on its limit keeps its work item `running` or in `setup`, never finishes setup at that turn end, and is neither ended nor released until it continues or the hub gives up. When its work item is done or a newer session starts, nothing will continue it, so its limit is cleared. A registered session in the operator's window is theirs to continue: its work item becomes `blocked`, or its approval waits again, saying the turn stopped at a usage limit; a limit the hub read is a wait, so it never fails the work item. A setup blocked so offers Retry, which queues it to run setup again, as for a failed one.
- **Continuing.** Each minute the hub continues each held session or app-server thread whose next attempt has come, when it is its work item's latest, under the launch the limit was found in, with its work item in `setup` or `running` and its ticket not snoozed. It types Claude Code's own continue text as a message no page sent, through the same queue and guards as a composer message ([Typing a message](#typing-a-message)), resuming an ended app-server thread first, and checks before the write that the session has not continued, its limit not changed and its work item still runs. Attempts back off; after a bounded number of attempts or time the hub blocks the work item with why and clears the limit. A session the operator typed in from an attached terminal during the wait is left to the operator: its limit is cleared and its work item blocked, saying why. `hub.log` records each attempt and skip.
- **The queue.** While a session of a provider waits on a usage limit with a reset ahead, the scheduler starts nothing in that provider until the reset; a throttle and a limit with no reset hold nothing.
- **Not continued.** A registered session in the operator's window is not continued, and Codex's account limits are not checked before an attempt.

## Terminal holder

PRD R94. `packages/holder` holds Code Mode sessions, and the [Codex terminals](#codex-runner) of the web page's console, in pseudo-terminals. It knows nothing about Claude, Code Mode, hooks or work items: `CodeModeRunner` ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)) holds everything Claude-specific. It is built for Windows and Linux; its experiments ran on Windows only ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M63–M82). This section is the contract other clients, such as claude-collab's voice front end, code against.

### Responsibilities

- Spawn a program with arguments, working directory, environment and size in a pseudo-terminal (node-pty), under an ID and an opaque label the caller gives.
- Keep each session's screen and scrollback in `@xterm/headless`, and the start of its output, so start evidence outlives scrolling.
- Let clients attach: replay the screen, then stream output; pass their input and size to the program.
- End a session on request by closing its pseudo-terminal, so the program runs its own cleanup, and end its process tree if it does not exit soon after ([Ending a session](#ending-a-session)); report its exit, and exits it did not cause.
- Keep an exited session's screen for a while, so a client attaching after the exit sees the last screen and the exit code. At the exit the holder serializes the screen and scrollback once and frees the terminal, so an exited session holds text rather than a terminal's buffer (mechanics M168); `screen`, `attach`, `view` and a `snapshot` of a kept size answer an exited session as they would have just before the exit.
- List every session with its label, pid, folder, start time, last input and output times, attached clients, its process tree and the tree's memory.

Not its job: reading a screen for meaning, deciding when to kill or resume, retrying, resuming after its own restart, or keeping anything but its record.

### Protocol

`packages/holder/src/protocol.ts` defines the messages and limits.

- **Transport.** TCP on `127.0.0.1`, an ephemeral port, JSON lines in UTF-8.
- **Handshake,** the same in every protocol version. The first line, within a few seconds, is `{"type": "hello", "token": "<token>"}`; a wrong token or a late or malformed hello closes the connection with an `error`. A right one is answered `{"type": "welcome", "protocol": <n>, "build": "<build>", "pid": <pid>, "features": [...]}`, where `features` names the requests added within the protocol that this holder serves (`snapshot`, `write`, `view`); a client never sends a request the holder does not name, because an unknown type closes the connection. A client checks `pid` against the record and `protocol` against its own; with another protocol it sends only `list`, `kill` and `shutdown`, which keep their shape in every version, so any client can list and stop the sessions of a holder of another protocol.
- **Token.** One token grants everything: attaching and typing, spawning any program and killing any session (PRD R94).
- **Limits.** A client line over 1 MiB, an unknown type or an invalid field closes the connection with an error, and fields have length limits. A client accepts reply lines up to 64 MiB, because a `list` or `screen` reply can pass 1 MiB. A replay is sent in chunks paced to the client's reading, because a full replay is about 1 MiB (mechanics M72). A client that falls too far behind is dropped, never the session.
- **Requests,** each with an `id` that the reply echoes as `re`:
  - `spawn {session, label, file, args, cwd, env, cols, rows}` → `spawned {session, pid}` once the program has started, or `error`, also for a `session` it holds live or is starting. node-pty 1.2 starts a Windows program only once its output reader is connected, so the holder answers then, with the pid, and a program that cannot start, such as in a missing folder, whose output pipe fails, or that has no pid within 15 seconds, is answered with an `error` and its pseudo-terminal freed (mechanics M169). A start counts as a live session for `shutdown` and the idle exit; a `kill` during it answers as for a session not held. Before it exits on a signal, the holder waits for the programs still starting and ends them with the others.
  - `list` → `sessions [{session, label, pid, cwd, startedAt, lastInputAt, lastOutputAt, exitedAt, exitCode, clients, tree, memoryBytes}]`, where `tree` lists the process tree's `{pid, created}`, the creation time telling a reused PID apart. The process table is sampled in the background, and `list` answers from the last sample (mechanics M75).
  - `kill {session}` → `killed {session, exitCode}` once the process has exited, or `error` at the deadline ([Ending a session](#ending-a-session)).
  - `screen {session}` → `screen {session, head, text}`: the retained start of the output and the visible screen as plain text.
  - `snapshot {session, scrollback}` → `snapshot {session, data, cols, rows, rev, keyAt}`: the screen and up to `scrollback` lines above it serialized with their attributes, as an attach replays them; for an exited session, which keeps only the hub's sizes (none, 400 lines and all), the smallest kept one with at least `scrollback` lines, so more scrollback than asked but never less; `rev` rises on every output, resize and input, so a reader skips a screen it has read, and `keyAt` is when a key was last typed in a terminal. The conversation layer reads replies and the input box from it ([Conversation](#conversation)).
  - `write {session, data, key?}` → `written {session, rev}`: input from a client that is not attached, so no resize and no `clients` count; it sets `keyAt` only with `key: true`, a key typed in a terminal, and is refused for a session that is not held or has ended. The hub types the page's messages with it ([Typing a message](#typing-a-message)), and the web page's console its keys with `key` ([Web page](#web-page)).
  - `size {session, cols, rows}` → `sized {session, rev}` (`view` feature): resizes the pseudo-terminal without attaching, so the program draws for the console that types.
  - `watch` → `watching`, then a stream of `exited {session, exitCode}`, `attached {session, clients}` and `detached {session, clients}` on this connection.
  - `shutdown` → `error` while any process is live or starting, naming the starting sessions in `starting` on every refusal, since `list` shows a session only once its program has started; otherwise `bye`, and the holder exits.
- **Attach.** `attach {session, cols, rows}`: the holder resizes the pseudo-terminal to the client's size, so the program redraws for it (mechanics M72), and sends the screen and scrollback as `replay {data}` lines and `replayed`, then `output {data}` as the program writes. The replay first resets the terminal modes the serializer only turns on and then restores the program's mouse encoding, which the serializer leaves out (mechanics M101), because the client reuses its terminal. The client sends `input {data}` and `resize {cols, rows}`. On exit the holder sends `exited {exitCode}` and closes. The last client to attach, resize or type sets the size. Input from several clients interleaves; the holder does not lock input.
- **View** (`view` feature). `view {session}`: the holder sends `resized {cols, rows}`, the size the program draws at, then the replay and `replayed`, then `output {data}` with a `resized` at each resize in its place among the output, and `exited` at the end. A viewer never resizes the pseudo-terminal and is not counted in `clients`; any message from it closes the connection with an error. The hub watches a session for the web page's console with it ([Web page](#web-page)).
- **Versions.** A field added within a protocol is optional, so an older client keeps working; any other change is a new `protocol` number.

### Record and lock

- `<home>/holder.json` holds `protocol`, `build`, `port`, `token` (32 random bytes, hex), `pid` and `startedAt`, written only by the lock holder and readable by the operator only, as `hub.json` is.
- The lock is the named pipe `\\.\pipe\switchboard-holder-<hash of home>` on Windows and `<home>/holder.sock` elsewhere; one holder runs per home. A holder that cannot take it exits, after retrying briefly while the recorded holder does not answer, because a holder ended by force can keep its pipe for a moment. A client accepts a record only when `welcome` answers with its pid, as for a hub record a forced exit left.

### Lifecycle

- **Start.** `HolderLink` (`packages/hub/src/holder.ts`), before a Code Mode start or resume and a Codex terminal's start, finds the recorded holder, or, when none answers and no process holds the lock, starts the installed build detached and hidden with `sessions.nodePath`, without VS Code's variables and `NODE_OPTIONS`, logging to `<home>/holder.log`; a holder that does not start fails the session's start with the reason and the log's path. The holder runs on the operator's Node.js, because node-pty is a native module. The hub's bundle imports only the holder client, which has no native dependency, so a missing or broken native module fails only Code Mode starts and Codex terminals (mechanics M76).
- **Install.** `hub.js install` installs each holder build in its own folder, `<home>/holder/<build>/`, with the pinned node-pty's helpers and prebuilt binaries, because Windows refuses to replace a loaded `.node` file while a second build runs beside it (mechanics M76); `<home>/holder/current.json` names the build a hub starts. Installs take an install lock, so installs run together take turns; an install never replaces a complete installed build, and removes build folders no holder runs or is about to start from. A failed install is written to `hub.log` and fails only Code Mode starts.
- **Version.** The build ID is the holder's own version, separate from Switchboard's: a hash over the path and content of every file of the holder's build (`writeManifest` in `extensions/switchboard/esbuild.js`), so it changes only when the holder's code, its dependencies or the bundler's output change, and nothing is bumped by hand. A running holder is **from an older build** when its `welcome.build` is not the installed build, or it lacks the protocol or a request this build sends (`olderHolder` in `packages/hub/src/holderCli.ts`); `hub.js holder list` and `holder stop --all` mark it so ([Visibility](#visibility-of-held-sessions)).
- **Hub restart or update.** The holder and its sessions keep running. A hub accepts any holder that speaks its `protocol`; one of another build keeps running, and the web page offers to [restart it](#restarting-the-holder). A holder of another protocol that holds live sessions is kept, listed and stopped through the version-stable requests, and Code Mode starts and resumes fail until its sessions end; then the hub shuts it down and starts the installed build.
- **Idle exit.** The holder exits a while after it last held a live process, whatever clients are connected. The hub starts it again at the next Code Mode start or resume.
- **Exit.** Before it exits, when idle, on `shutdown` or on `SIGINT` or `SIGTERM`, the holder refuses further spawns and ends each live session as `kill` does; idle and `shutdown` exits come only when none is live.
- **Crash or logoff.** The pseudo-terminals close with the holder, and each `claude` exits within seconds (mechanics M69), but a shell command it was running can run on, unlike after a `kill` (mechanics M78); the hub applies [holder loss](#code-mode-sessions).

### Ending a session

Every end the hub asks for is a `kill`: a release after the turn, when the work item is done or a newer session starts, the idle end, Stop session, Delete and `hub.js holder stop`. The holder ends the program in two steps under one deadline:

- **Close.** Closing the session's pseudo-terminal leaves the program running, so Claude Code ends its shell commands, logs `SessionEnd` and exits within about 2 seconds (mechanics M78). A process a command left behind outside the tree runs on (PRD Q10). On Windows the holder keeps reading the output, so the last screen stays attachable; on Linux the close ends the reading, so the screen is the one before the close.
- **Tree kill.** When the program has not exited a few seconds after the close, or the pseudo-terminal cannot be closed, the holder ends the process tree, as the [Codex runner](#codex-runner) does, which skips Claude's cleanup and `SessionEnd`, and logs why.
- **Windows.** node-pty's own `kill()` kills `claude` before its cleanup can run (mechanics M78), so the close calls ConPTY's close through node-pty's private agent fields, after checking that they exist (`packages/holder/src/close.ts`). node-pty is pinned in `packages/holder/package.json` for this, at 1.2.0-beta.18, the first line with a lock around its list of pseudo-consoles: on 1.1.0 programs ending together crashed the holder (mechanics M169). The built-holder test fails when an upgrade breaks the close, and the holder falls back to the tree kill meanwhile. node-pty also frees a pseudo-terminal's output thread and input pipe only in `kill()`, so when any program exits, by itself or ended, the holder frees them through the same private fields, checked the same way; otherwise each ended session keeps a thread and its screen (mechanics M168).
- **Linux.** node-pty's `destroy()`, which closes the master side and sends the program `SIGHUP`. Not verified on Linux.

### Visibility of held sessions

- **CLI.** `hub.js holder list`, `hub.js holder stop <id>` and `hub.js holder stop --all [--yes]` (`packages/hub/src/holderCli.ts`) find the home as `report` does, so any installed bundle reaches the same hub and holder. With the hub up they go through `POST /holder/list` and `POST /holder/stop {id}`, so an active session stops through the flow as any Stop does; with the hub down they talk to the holder directly, whatever its protocol, and the hub's next roster read ends what they stopped. Each names a holder from an older build first. `list` shows every held session, orphans first, with its work item, IDs, folder, state, idle time, memory and attached terminals. `stop` takes a Switchboard session ID, a current or retired Claude session ID or its 8-character prefix, and refuses an ambiguous or unknown one. `stop --all` names the holder and the live sessions that end with it, ends nothing without `--yes`, and with it stops each live session, shuts the holder down and ends its process tree if it does not exit, so the next Code Mode start launches the installed build. The exit code tells done, a usage error, no hub or holder answering, and a refusal apart.
- **Orphans.** A live held process whose session is no active session of the store, such as after a failed Delete stop or a store reset, or one whose session ended while it still runs. Each roster read, whether or not a Code Mode session is active, lists the holder without starting one; a failed read keeps the last list. The hub logs each orphan once, lists it and offers Stop; it never kills one by itself.
- **Snapshot.** `held` (`HeldSession` in `packages/hub/src/held.ts`), absent when the holder lists none, is the holder's last list joined with the store, orphans first; the web page shows it in its Background sessions sheet, with each held session's Open in terminal and Stop, and a note in its list header while any is an orphan ([Web page](#web-page), List header).

### Restarting the holder

A holder from an older build ([Lifecycle](#lifecycle)) keeps running while it holds sessions, so the hub never replaces it by itself; sessions are rarely all idle. The web page shows a notice in its list header instead, and its Restart and resume, which a paired device's page leaves out ([Remote access](#remote-access)), does what `hub.js holder stop --all --yes` and a Resume of each session would.

- **State.** The snapshot's `holder` (`HolderState` in `packages/hub/src/held.ts`) gives the connected and installed builds, whether the holder is older, and the last restart's progress or result, with each session that did not resume; the page's list header carries it ([Web page](#web-page), List header).
- **Notice.** While the holder is older or restarting, the notice says so and how many sessions a restart resumes: the live held sessions whose session the store holds active. Its confirmation names each session in a turn, since the restart interrupts it, and counts the sessions that end without resuming. After a restart it shows the result and any session that did not resume, until dismissed.
- **Restart.** `POST /holder/restart {detail?}` refuses a `detail`, the confirmation the operator accepted, that no longer matches the one the notice asks, so a session that started a turn while the operator read it is never interrupted unnamed; it also refuses while a restart runs, with no holder connected, and for a holder that is not older. Otherwise it answers at once and runs in the background: it stops every live held session as Stop does, shuts the old holder down, ending its process tree if it does not exit, and starts the installed build. A holder that refuses is never ended: while its list shows no live session the hub asks again for up to 20 seconds, the holder's start time and a margin, then names a session still starting like a live one or fails the restart; once it has refused, an ask it does not answer within 5 seconds is no acceptance either. Only a holder that does not answer the first `shutdown`, so a hung holder can still be replaced, or does not exit after accepting, has its tree ended; `hub.js holder stop --all --yes`, the operator's explicit command, ends it either way; a Code Mode start or resume meanwhile waits for it, and a session started in the old holder meanwhile is stopped too, for a bounded number of rounds before the restart fails without ending the holder. It then resumes each session that was active, as the web page's Resume does, and keeps each refusal as that session's failure. A resumed session is idle; its work item keeps the status the stop gave it until its next turn, as after any Stop and Resume.

### Attach client

`hub.js attach <Switchboard session ID>` (`packages/hub/src/attach.ts`), run with `sessions.nodePath`, finds the home as `report` does, posts `POST /attach {sessionId}`, which may resume an ended session ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)), and connects to the holder the answer names at the terminal's size. It puts the terminal in raw mode, writes the replay and output, and passes input and size changes. When the session exits it says so and offers Enter to attach again, which resumes the session, so the terminal the terminal app keeps for the session can attach again. A refusal, a hub that is down or a connection the holder closes is reported with the option to try again. Closing it only detaches.

## Conversation

The conversation of a Code Mode or Codex app-server session, on the web page (PRD R96, [Web page](#web-page)): the operator reads the replies beside the session's terminal, types messages in the terminal or the composer (PRD R97), reads the session's latest state (PRD R98), opens the artifacts it writes (PRD R99), each unread until seen (PRD R100), switches between the work item's sessions (PRD R102), and answers the lettered questions a session asks with a card (PRD R103). With `voice.enabled` on, the replies to its own messages are read aloud while the voice speaks for its session (PRD R105, [Voice](#voice)), and the operator can dictate messages to it (PRD R108, [Dictation](#dictation)).

### Conversation rules

The conversation rules (`conversationRules` in `packages/conversation/src/rules.ts`) are the contract between a session and the readers of its conversation. A Code Mode session's appended system prompt and a Codex app-server session's instructions carry them. They explain only the tags and shapes the hub and the page read, and say so:

- A session writes each reply as it would anyway and puts a `[details]` line of its own before the part the operator can skip and still act correctly, such as evidence, checks, file lists and background, as claude-collab's replies did; what the operator needs to decide or act on stays above it, links, paths and code allowed, and nothing is restated or summarised (operator decision 2026-11-22). The text above the line is the primary response; the page shows the primary response first, at the size of the operator's messages, and the details at the theme's text size, whether or not the reply is spoken (operator decision 2026-11-22). Only that line splits a reply: one with no `[details]` line is all primary and shows whole (operator decision 2026-11-22; [Conversation](#conversation)).
- `[aside]` starts text written only to show progress; `[rewrite]` starts a reply that replaces the previous reply after a newer message; a `[state]` line of its own starts the session's state, which runs to the end of that text, with any reply before it ([States](#states)).
- Questions are the other place the rules shape what a session writes, because the card's usefulness depends on it: a card only for a real decision between known alternatives, never to gather details or while the operator is mid-thought, and none for a low-stakes choice the session can make on its own recommendation; at most four numbered questions at the end of a reply, each with a slug id (`{#slug}`), an optional bold topic, a line of context, and two to four lettered options that say what each changes, the recommendation marked, ending a question with "Select all that apply." when several may be chosen; the card adds a Comment field, so no Other option; `[withdraw: slug]` for a question no longer needed; answers arrive as an `[answers]` message, one line per slug ([Conversation view](#conversation-view), PRD R103). A Claude session is told to ask in a reply rather than with AskUserQuestion, even where the report contract or a skill says to, because that menu holds the whole session and the operator's messages until it is answered in the terminal, which the page answers only key by key ([Web page](#web-page), Prompts in the terminal); an open question keeps the work item waiting on the operator when the turn ends. Questions in a state get no card. A Codex session may ask with `request_user_input`, which the page shows.
- An Artifacts section names the session's artifact folder, by its path and `CLAUDE_ARTIFACTS_DIR`, and explains artifact names, how each type opens, where an artifact's files and pictures go and how to show a picture, front matter naming the title, updating, and the artifact tag that lists a file written elsewhere without a copy ([Artifacts](#artifacts)); it does not say when to write one.
- With `voice.enabled` on, a Spoken replies section explains that a `[spoken]` message's reply has its primary response read aloud up to its first list, table, code block, heading, quote or rule (`speechPart`), never its details, and the `[tone: …]` line an `[expressive]` message gets ([Voice](#voice)), and a Dictated messages section explains `[voice]`, `[voice] [partial]`, ignoring speech not addressed to the session, and the heard tag, `<!--heard:what came through=what was meant-->`, which the page hides ([Dictation](#dictation)). Both come before the artifact rules.
- A session with a [persona](#personas) gets its tone section last.

Apart from the primary response and questions, the rules set no length, shape, format or verbosity, so a session writes as it would without them, with its provider's defaults and the operator's output style (voice-design decision 21, which the primary response replaces for a reply's opening); a turn may write several replies between its tool calls (voice-design decision 24). A rule is added only for a tag something reads. No rules version is kept: the hub reads every session's tags as the current rules define them, and the contract hook gives a resumed or compacted session the current rules ([Claude Code runner](#claude-code-runner)).

### Window scope

A web page sees only the conversations of the work item it selected, enforced in the hub (voice-design decision 26). The page key separates pages; the hub token stays a machine credential, so the rule is not a defence against software running as the operator. The rule and its names (`WindowScopes`, `WindowScope`, the `window` field) come from the retired VS Code windows; only page keys remain.

- **Key.** Each page's conversation stream gets a random page key in its `hello` (`WindowScopes` in `packages/hub/src/windows.ts`), which dies with the stream; a reload gets a new one. The hub derives everything from the key, so no conversation route takes a work item ID except `select`, which binds the page to any work item the store knows ([Web page](#web-page), Page key).
- **Binding.** A page is bound to nothing until it selects, and then to the selected work item's group in its list (`pageGroups` in `packages/hub/src/page.ts`): the work item with any [hosted conversations](#hosted-conversations) its window hosts, the group's own work item first. Those are its **conversations**, the selected one kept even when it is done, until another is selected. Membership never follows a folder: another work item in the same folder, as every Support work item is, never joins. The binding is never stored, so a reload starts unbound and selects again from its route. `scopeGen` rises whenever the selected conversation or its current session changes, or a deleted work item unbinds the page, so a command meant for the earlier one is refused. Within the selected conversation the page sees its current session, or an earlier session it chose read-only ([Web page](#web-page), Earlier sessions).
- **Hosts.** Hosts are compared by `hostKey` (`packages/core`): without case on Windows and macOS, and a folder host with its case elsewhere, so two Linux folders that differ only in case stay apart. A host that is no path, such as a page key an older hub stored, hosts nothing.
- **Refusals.** A request outside the rule is refused 403 with a reason that names the fact that failed (`outOfScope` in `packages/hub/src/windows.ts`): the key is no connected page's, as after the page's stream ended or the hub restarted, so the page tries again once it reconnects; the page has no work item open; or the request names a work item, session or message the page no longer shows. A key that another sign-in or origin holds is refused as such. A stale `scopeGen` is refused 409 with "The window's conversation changed.", text an open page of an older build compares to keep a send's `opId`, so it stays as it is and the page says it in its own words ([Web page](#web-page), Requests in time).
- **What stays global.** The list, its header, the pool, the held sessions and the needs-you alerts are the page's own routes, not scoped to its selection; the CLI's routes (`/holder/*`, `/attach`, the report routes) stay global.

### Hosted conversations

An older hub started ad hoc conversations from a VS Code item or folder window (New conversation… there, or Switchboard: New Conversation…), each hosted by that window's binding (`Ticket.hostWindow`: an item window's workspace file, a folder, or, from the web page, a page key). No new ones start: the flow has no event that makes one, and the page starts a [standalone session](#standalone-sessions) or a new session of the work item instead. Stored ones still load and show as conversations, with a row and the usual controls (voice-design § 3.1), and share with standalone conversations the lifecycle below, as does every interactive ad hoc work item.

- **Shown.** The page's list puts a hosted conversation in its host's group (`pageGroups`): under the work item whose window file is its host, else in a group of its own named after its folder or workspace file, newest first after the host; line 2 names its host instead of the playbook, and its playbook slot reads `conversation` (`adhocLabel`). A host that is no path, such as a page key, hosts nothing, and the conversation is a group of its own.
- **Its window.** A hosted conversation has no window of its own (`windowOf` in `packages/hub/src/dock.ts`), and Open in VS Code opens its host's: the host's workspace file, written first from the host's own row, or the host folder; with no such row and the file gone, or a host that is no path, the conversation's own folder ([Web page](#web-page), Open in VS Code).
- **Contract.** A conversation's contract offers no `done` or `approval` report. A Claude conversation is refused while `sessions.installHooks` is off.
- **Provider choice.** The New session form offers Claude and Codex from `providers`, with `sessions.provider` preselected, and hides its switch when the hub finds only one provider's CLI (PRD R122). A cancelled choice creates nothing. The choice is stored on the work item (`WorkItem.provider`) and kept by queued starts and retries. A work item's sessions start in its playbook's provider, else its own choice while it has no playbook, else `sessions.provider`, so a chosen Codex conversation that a ticket report moves onto a playbook starts its next session in that playbook's provider. Choosing never changes settings or an existing session.
- **Idle start.** A promptless session waits at an empty input box and reads as idle, not running (mechanics M87). The contract and the conversation rules reach it only through its system prompt and, on resume and compaction, the contract hook.
- **Open between turns.** A conversation's turn end and its session's end leave its work item `running`, never finished or blocked for a missing report; a failed turn still blocks it. It holds a slot only while its session is in a turn, so an idle conversation never holds back the queue. Its row stays in In progress, with the idle mark and no turn while its session is idle or ended, a question card included (PRD R103). It ends by Mark done, Delete, or the held session's idle end (PRD R95), after which Resume or a message resumes it.
- **Title.** Its first message gives the placeholder a short subject label. Claude transcript title records and Codex thread name updates then set the work item's title; a provider's manual title outranks its automatic one. The launch leaves the native title unset so the provider may name it ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M132). A reported ticket stops this synchronization, preserving its ticket title.
- **Resume before a message.** A Claude session that ended before its first message has no transcript, which `claude --resume` refuses (mechanics M87), so resuming it starts it again under its session ID with its contract and no prompt.

### Standalone sessions

New session (PRD R122) is one form on the web page, opened from the list header's +. `page-new-session` takes `{opId, folder, provider, model?, prompt?, run?}`: with `run` `now`, the default, the flow's `new-session` makes an interactive ad hoc work item without `Ticket.hostWindow`, empty or with `prompt` as its first turn and its title (`summarizeMessage`), until the provider names it; with `queue` or `wait` it sends `adhoc` (PRD R6), queued or `unstarted`. Both store the model on the work item, which applies it to starts and retries; null is the provider's default. The answer is `{workItemId, folder, run}`, the page selecting the work item for every outcome; a repeated `opId` answers the first success. A conversation shares the [hosted conversations](#hosted-conversations)' report contract and idle lifecycle, promptless only without a first message, which a retry sends again only while every earlier session failed at its start; and it has its own row and window. Its row's playbook slot reads `session` (`adhocLabel`). Run a schedule now is the page's `page-adhoc {schedule}`, which sends `run-schedule` and answers the run's `workItemId`. The folder is checked as it is typed.

- **Folders.** `allowedFolder` (`packages/hub/src/folders.ts`) checks every folder the form sends, at creation and as the page's operator types: an absolute path to a readable folder, resolved through links to its real path. A paired device's page (`remote`, which the server sets on `page-new-session` and `page-folders` over any the page sent) may use only a folder inside the recent folders, repo roots or worktrees of work items not done, so a link out of them is refused. `page-folders {path?}` answers the starting places without a path, those three groups and, on this computer's page only, the home folder and the drives, and with one the folder's subfolders, leaving out dot folders, build output and, on a paired device, links out, at most `FOLDERS_LISTED`. The recent folders are the store's (`pagePrefs.recentFolders`, the last ten, newest first), shared by every page, set by each successful creation.
- **Models.** `providers` answers `modelChoices` beside the configured `models` (`packages/hub/src/models.ts`): Claude's from a list the hub keeps, the aliases plus the Claude models settings.json names, which need no update when a model version changes; Codex's from its app-server's `model/list` ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M136), asked once per hub start and again after a failure or a 5 s wait, which leave `sessions.codex.model` alone. Default sends `model: null`, so a later change of the setting applies.

### Reading a session

`ConversationService` (`packages/hub/src/conversation.ts`) owns scope, delivery, events, unread, artifacts and speech. A provider adapter reads each session: the Claude adapter by the rules below, the Codex adapter from Codex's native history and events ([Codex runner](#codex-runner)). `ConversationLog` stores authoritative entries for either provider and applies tags, states and rewrites. The conversation library (`packages/conversation`) is ported from claude-collab. The hub reads each active Claude Code Mode session's conversation while it runs, and a last time when it ends. A poll reads up to four sessions at once, each session's screen before its transcript, since the holder takes 15 ms or more to answer each busy session's screen, and a poll still running when the next is due skips it (mechanics M165):

- **Transcript, the authority.** Claude Code's transcript of the session is the record, followed through the session ID changes the hub confirms (`/clear`, `/branch`, `/resume`, mechanics M73), so a nested run's transcript is never read. A checkpoint per session lets a restarted hub resume where it stopped, and an entry the store already holds is never imported again. A new transcript after an ID change is read from its start when it holds nothing older than the last entry read (`/clear`), else from its end, because `/branch` and `/resume` copy history, with a note that the conversation continued from another session. A transcript can grow to hundreds of megabytes, and reading all of it holds up the hub, so the conversation reads only what was added since the checkpoint, to the file's end in segments of at most 8 MB of whole lines unless one line is longer, so a transcript of any size is read without one buffer and string for all of it (`readLines` in `packages/conversation/src/transcript.ts`, mechanics M165), and bounded parts of the file: its start, for copied history, and its end before the checkpoint, to link replies to messages after a restart. A failed transcript read is logged once per session and stops neither the session's title read nor any other session's read. The ad-hoc title read finds the title lines' bytes in 4 MB chunks from the offset it last reached and decodes only those lines, so it holds at most one chunk and moves on past a line that does not parse (`titleLines` in `packages/hub/src/claudeConversation.ts`, mechanics M165).
- **Read before a turn end.** When a held session goes idle after a turn, the hub reads its conversation at once, before the flow decides whether an open question keeps the work item waiting on the operator (PRD R103); a message that comes back not sent answers no question. A Codex app-server session's idle marker waits until the items the adapter holds back have been imported, and a Code Mode session's until its transcript shows the turn's end, a `stop_hook_summary` or `turn_duration` line, for at most about 2 s, because Claude Code can write a turn's last reply after the hook that reports the turn end.
- **Entries.** An assistant message's text is a reply, one reply per API message although Claude Code writes a line per content block; a reply saved only as thinking is still a reply (claude-collab findings 2026-11-17). A user line from the operator is a message, origin `terminal`, and a slash command shows as `/name args`. Lines Claude Code makes up, such as an added Poll call or "No response requested.", tool results, meta, sidechain and compaction entries, command output, shell escapes, reminders and interruption notes are left out. A message the operator sends while Claude is busy is added `provisional` when Claude Code queues it and moves to where Claude received it (claude-collab findings 2026-11-19); queue entries Claude Code adds itself, such as task notifications, the plain-text notice that the operator stopped background agents (mechanics M118), and the `/rate-limit-options` command and continue text it queues at a usage limit (mechanics M140), are left out, and a sent message that Claude receives as such an entry, has not received when the session ends, or has not received when a message sent after it is received, as when an earlier run exited with it queued, is `withdrawn`.
- **Order.** The conversation is in transcript line order, never by the lines' timestamps, which are not in order (claude-collab findings 2026-11-19). The items not in the transcript yet, a provisional message or a reply's screen copy, follow in the order they were first seen. A withdrawn item never holds back the items after it.
- **Screen, an early copy.** While a session works and shortly after, the hub reads its screen from the terminal holder's snapshot, from a holder that offers it, to show a reply before its transcript line lands. Code Mode sessions run in Claude Code's classic renderer so that finished output scrolls into the scrollback, where it can be read. A copy holds the reply's text alone: rows Claude Code draws beside it, such as Focus view's row of running tool calls, end it (mechanics M120), because a copy with them never matches its transcript copy.
- **Hold.** A screen copy waits briefly for its transcript copy, which is then published alone; one that comes later replaces the screen copy in place. The two copies are matched by their letters and digits, so Markdown syntax, wrapping and quotes do not count, and a transcript copy's link counts both as its text and as its text with its target, because the screen draws a link with its target (mechanics M130). A screen copy that gets no transcript copy is withdrawn, as are those still waiting when the session ends; a withdrawn rewrite frees the reply it replaced. A restarted hub takes up the published screen copies the stopped one left waiting, so each is replaced or withdrawn as before rather than left as a reply.
- **Replies.** A reply is split into its kind, aside or reply, its rewrite tag, its text (`opening`), its state ([States](#states)), its primary response (`speak`) and its tone line. The primary response is the text before the reply's first `[details]` line outside code, which the text leaves out; with none, the whole reply is primary, lists, tables, code and questions included. An aside has none, and an older reply's `[speak]`…`[/speak]` passage, from before the `[details]` rule, stays its primary response; a tag quoted as code or mentioned mid-sentence is text. A rewrite replaces the newest earlier reply that is not an aside, withdrawn or rewritten. A reply answers the first operator message on its transcript parent chain (claude-collab findings 2026-11-19), so several messages delivered together get one reply and one message can get several; when the chain reaches no message, as in history copied by `/branch`, the reply answers the latest message before it. The store keeps each session's newest messages and replies up to a fixed retention (`CONVERSATION_RETENTION`), outside the activity log, and deletes them with their work item; unresolved questions outlive retention (Question cards, Past retention).
- A session that ended while the hub was not reading it has the rest of its transcript read when a page shows it.

### Typing a message

The composer's `send` command (PRD R97) types a message into the page's current session through the terminal holder, from claude-collab's `session.js`. `ConversationService.send` stores the message, origin `typed` and delivery `open`, answers with it at once, and queues it in the session's `SessionInput` (`packages/conversation/src/input.ts`), which types one input transaction at a time per session, in order. Each message is one input transaction, or joins the one waiting to be pasted (**Joining**, below); a transaction reaches only the session and launch the page showed, never types a message twice, and never types over the operator:

1. **Guards.** The message waits while Claude Code shows a numbered choice or confirmation (its wait for a usage limit's reset is not one, because it takes a message, mechanics M100), while the input box holds a draft, or while an attached terminal typed a key moments before. Typing in the attached terminal is never blocked. A screen read or write the holder does not answer in time fails the message.
2. **Input reservation.** `Runtime.reserveInput` serializes the transaction with an end, Stop, Delete or resume of the session, as an attach does ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions), Attach serialization), refuses a session that ended or was resumed under another launch than the view showed, and defers a release of the held session while it types. The guards are read again under the reservation.
3. **Paste and Enter.** The text goes as one bracketed paste with a leading `/`, `!`, `#` or `&` escaped, so it is never a command; a leading `@` stays a file mention, because the escape loses the mention ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M88), and a slash command chosen from the composer's list keeps its `/`, so it runs ([Slash commands](#slash-commands)). Enter goes once the paste shows in the box, and is retried only while the text still sits there and the screen has changed since, so a stale screen never sends a blind Enter. The session, launch and current-session checks run again before every write. The write counts as input for the end rule's settle (PRD R95).
4. **Confirmation.** The message is `sent` once the transcript holds it, as a user entry or as the queue entry of a message typed while Claude works, written at or after the paste and found in what the transcript added since the paste, since Claude Code can write megabytes right after the message's line, read without Claude Code's paste wrapping and with a slash command read from its command tags ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M86, M88, M99). Otherwise the screen settles it, and it is never typed again. An empty input box, or one holding other text, means it went: it is `sent`. A box holding exactly the message, with no key typed in an attached terminal since the paste, is cleared one key at a time, each only while the checks pass, the transcript still lacks the message and the box still holds what is left of it ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M90), and the message is `not-sent`. A box holding the message with other text, one a terminal typed into, one that changed under a key, or one that cannot be read, is left as it is, and the message is `not-sent` with why. A message refused before its paste, or whose session ended, was resumed or was replaced by a newer session of its work item while it waited, is `not-sent` with why.

**Joining.** A message queued while the last transaction in the queue waits for its turn or its guards joins that transaction rather than waiting behind it. The joined text is each message as it would be pasted alone, escape and tags included, in the order sent, with a blank line between, so dictated and `[spoken]` messages keep their tags at the start of their paragraphs. A message never joins one whose paste has started, a slash command chosen from the composer's list never joins or is joined, the hub's continue text after a usage limit goes alone, and a message joins only one for the same launch and only while the joined text stays within `MAX_MESSAGE_CHARS` (10,000 characters); otherwise it becomes the next transaction, which the messages after it can join. The hub logs one line for each message that joins. Each message stays its own message in the store and on the page, which shows them as the operator sent them, each `open` until the joined text settles: they share its paste time, steps and outcome, so all are `sent` or all `not-sent`, each going back to its composer, and each keeps its own answers and dismissals.

**Tags.** A message sent while its reply will be heard ([Voice](#voice)) is typed with `[spoken] `, and `[expressive] ` after it while `voice.expressive` is on; a message that holds dictated text with `[voice] ` after those tags, and a part of one still being spoken with `[voice] [partial] ` and never `[spoken]` ([Dictation](#dictation)). A slash command chosen from the list never gets them. The reader strips them, so the message shows and matches as the operator wrote it.

**Matching.** A typed message is added `provisional` and matched to the transcript line that carries its pasted text, written near when it was pasted. Its queue entry makes it `sent`; the line where Claude received it makes it `sent`, gives it that line's place and UUID instead of adding a terminal message, and the replies whose chain leads to that line answer it. A line where Claude Code took the message's lines that hold only an image path as images, with an `[Image #N]` placeholder for each, matches when its placeholders are as many as those lines and the rest of the text, which must not be empty, is the same, unless messages awaiting it with that text name other images, which the line does not name, when none of them matches; messages typed as one are not matched this way ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M159). A typed message is never withdrawn as a terminal one is. Messages typed as one are matched together: messages pasted at the same time whose texts, each with any tags at the start of its paragraph, make up the line in order, unless a message alone that matches was pasted later; they all take that line's place and UUID, in the order sent, and the replies to it answer the last.

**Unconfirmed.** A typed message counted as `sent` that no transcript line shows becomes `unconfirmed`, with why, so it never looks sent or waits last in the conversation with the turn row under it: when `TYPED_LATE_MS` (2 minutes) passes after its paste with no queue line of it, at once when a message typed after it reaches the transcript, since Claude Code takes messages in the order typed, and when its session ends or moves to another transcript. A message whose queue line was read is exempt from the 2 minutes, however long Claude's turn runs; after a hub restart only the queue lines among the lines the reader indexes count. It keeps the place where it was sent, and the page shows it "not confirmed" with Send again, which puts its text back in the composer and sends nothing, since Claude may have it; an Answers message has no Send again, and its questions are open on their card again. A line that shows it later, in the same transcript, still confirms it as `sent`, and its queue line makes it `sent` and provisional again.

**Not sent.** A message that ends `not-sent` leaves the conversation: the hub deletes it from the store and sends a last `message` event with its text and why to the pages showing its session and the page that sent it, on which every page drops it and the page that sent it puts its text back in the composer ([Conversation view](#conversation-view)). A repeated `send` of a recent such message is refused with why rather than typed again.

**Restart.** A message a stopped hub left `open` is settled by the next hub once a page showing its session opens its stream: one never pasted is `not-sent`; a pasted one is settled from the transcript and the screen as above, with the messages of a Claude session pasted at the same time into the same launch settled together as their joined text, only in the launch it was typed into, and `not-sent` with why when the holder never shows the screen or no launch was kept. Since this hub does not know which page sent it, its last `message` event carries `giveBack: true`, and every page showing the session puts its text back in the composer.

**Known limit.** A key typed in an attached terminal between the last screen read and a clearing key is not caught, as a paste is not (voice-design decision 2); the clearing stops at the next read.

**Older holders.** Typing needs a holder that names `write` in `welcome.features`. A holder of the same protocol from an older build is never replaced while the hub runs, so `send` to one is refused with how to stop it and switch builds, and until then the session can be opened in the terminal ([Visibility of held sessions](#visibility-of-held-sessions)).

### Slash commands

The composer's `/` list (PRD R101) shows the slash commands of the page's current session, from `POST /v1/conversation/commands` (`SessionCommands` in `packages/conversation/src/commands.ts`): first the built-ins that finish without a menu in the terminal, `/clear` and `/compact`, since the composer cannot answer a menu, then the skills the session's transcript lists, in their order, with their descriptions ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M88). Before the session's first message, and after `/clear` until the next, the transcript lists no skills, so only the built-ins show. A command chosen from the list is sent with `command: true` while the text is one line that still starts with that `/name`, and typed unescaped so it runs; `send` refuses `command` for any other text. A `/name` typed by hand without choosing it, or recalled, goes escaped, as a message.

### Context meter

The hub reads each shown session's context size and sends it as `SessionView.context`, `{used, window, compactAt, model}` or null while it is not known (PRD R114); the computation is `packages/conversation/src/contextMeter.ts`.

- **Claude.** `used` is the latest main-chain request's size in the session's current transcript, counted as Claude Code's own panel counts it, leaving out subagent, made-up and API error lines; a compaction boundary sets it to the size after compaction, and a new transcript file, after `/clear` or a resume, starts unknown. `window` comes from the request's model, looked up in a table copied from Claude Code's ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M105); the transcript does not say whether the long window is on, so a size past a model's standard window reads as its long window. `compactAt` is the threshold Claude Code's own panel uses, or, where lower, the size at which the transcript shows the session last compacted itself, since Switchboard does not read the settings that move it. An unknown model has a null `window` and `compactAt`.
- **Codex.** `used` and `window` come from the latest token-usage notification for the session's thread; `compactAt` is null, since Codex does not report it. It is unknown until the first notification after the hub started.
- **Meter.** One renderer (`packages/conversation`) draws it in the composer's footer as a ring with no text, filling toward `compactAt`, else `window`, and warning as it nears it; an unknown size shows an empty track. Its menu repeats the size and can compact the conversation, which sends `/compact` through `send` as a chosen slash command, leaving the composer's draft alone.
- **Updates.** After each conversation read ([Reading a session](#reading-a-session)) the hub sends a page its `session` event again when the session's context differs from what that page was last sent, so the meter follows each request of a turn.

### States

PRD R98. A state is the part of a reply after its own `[state]` line, outside fenced code, to the end of the reply's text (`splitState`, from claude-collab's `status.js`); `[status]`, which sessions wrote before states, is read the same way but no longer taught. Its first non-empty line, without leading heading, bullet or quote marks and with bold unwrapped, is the headline; the rest, trimmed, is the body, or none. The text before the line is the reply's `opening`, empty for a reply that is only a state. A tag quoted inline, in a code block or with text on its line is text. A state is taken only from a reply's transcript copy, never its screen copy, which can still be withdrawn or growing. Each reply has at most one state, updated in place when a later line of the same API message adds to it, and the newest read is the session's. A reply that is only a state rewrites nothing, is not a rewrite target, and is left out of the timeline. A `stage` report stores a headline-only state for the reporting session, so registered sessions and skills that send stages keep setting it. The store keeps each session's newest states up to the same retention as replies, in the order read, outside the activity log, and deletes them with their work item.
