History's timeline shows every state the store keeps: a reply's from the states table, and a stage report's as its activity log entry, which names the state it stored. Entries run newest first by time, a log event before a state at the same time; a reply state's time is when the reply wrote it, so order within a turn is not promised.

A state's age is tied to the turn that wrote it by observed order, not by comparing a transcript's times with the hub's clock: when the store sees a session leave `starting`, `running` or `waiting`, its states are marked turn-ended (`turnEnded`). A state stored while its session is in no turn, or written more than 5 s before the session's current turn began (`sessions.turnAt`, set when the session is inserted, resumed or starts a turn after one ended; the slack covers Codex history's whole-second turn times), is stored turn-ended too, because its turn cannot be established. `stateStale` in core reads a state as current, `turn` (its turn ended, or its session is between turns) or `session` (its session ended or is gone, or it has none). The row and History dim an old state, and name it the last state for `session`.

History shows the scoped session's latest state, else its work item's latest from another session, which is never unread, so a page never marks another session's state seen. It pins that state at the head of the open work item's timeline, outside the time order, where no filter hides it, and leaves its reply entry out of the timeline; a pinned stage state's log entry stays, named Stage report, so its attribution and details still show. The row shows the work item's latest state, and a state stored from a reply rebuilds the list. Mid-turn states are not relied on: Claude can save text written just before a tool call only as thinking (mechanics M120), so the stage report stays the way to set a state between tool calls.

A state with no session is a work item's stage carried over from before states, whose writer was not recorded, so it reads as the last state; states carried over from statuses are turn-ended.

### Unread

PRD R100. A reply or state is unread until the operator sees it; the store records when (`seenAt` on the `replies` and `states` rows).

- **Where.** An unread mark shows only where the operator would not otherwise think to look, such as an older conversation in the dropdown. No list of work items shows one: the operator looks at a Ready for review item anyway, and at an In progress one when it moves to Ready for review, not before. So the web page's list shows none, and the snapshot and the dock view carry no count.
- **What counts.** A reply the timeline shows on its own (`countsUnread` in core): not a withdrawn screen copy, a reply a rewrite replaced, a reply that is only a state or a note Switchboard added. Of the states, only the session's latest, which History pins (the states in its timeline never report seen), and only once its body changes: a new state is unread when its body differs from the session's last body, an update when its body changes, and a headline alone, as a stage report sets, is stored seen; an unread body stays unread through a headline-only update. A screen copy replaced in place by its transcript copy keeps its ID, so it is seen once, and a seen reply stays seen when it grows. Only a work item's current session counts, so an earlier session's items, which a page shows only read-only, never count.
- **Seen.** The page counts an item as seen once it has been in view for a second while the page is visible and has the focus, as PRD R100 defines in view, and reports it with the scope generation and session it was shown in (`seen`, [Conversation API](#conversation-api)); an item the hub did not accept is reported again, as is whatever is still unread after a reconnect. Each item is marked on its own, so seeing a newer one never clears an older one, and being on the page, a newer item or the page's focus alone does not count. A hidden page marks nothing.
- **Mark.** Unread has one mark and one colour everywhere, VS Code's activity bar badge colours, which the page's theme copies: a count, or a dot where it marks one item, such as a conversation in the dropdown or the History tab ([Conversation view](#conversation-view)). The dot is named for a screen reader and on hover, Unread, and no tab or state carries a New label in its place. The selected conversation's own entry and button carry no dot, since its items are in front of the operator.
- **Counts.** The hub sends each page that lists a session's work item, while it is that work item's current session, `unread {workItemId, count}` when its count changes, checked after every message, reply or state added or changed, since a new item can push an unread one past retention, and after a `seen`. A page's dropdown entries carry each conversation's count (`ConversationEntry.unread`).

### Artifacts

An artifact is a file one level down in its work item's artifact folder, `<owner>/<file>` (PRD R99), from claude-collab's `artifacts.js`. The folder is `<session folder>/.wip/artifacts/w<work item ID>` (`artifactsDir` in core), its own per work item, so a later work item that shares the worktree never lists an earlier one's; the session finds it in `CLAUDE_ARTIFACTS_DIR` and in the conversation rules ([Code Mode sessions](#code-mode-sessions)). The folder's artifacts are not stored; the folder is the record. A file outside it that a reply's artifact tag names is an external artifact, which the store keeps.

- **Watch.** `ArtifactFolder` (`packages/conversation/src/artifacts.ts`) watches the folder and gives each artifact a type by its extension (`markdown`, `html`, `image`, `audio`, `video`, else `file`) and, for a Markdown or HTML file, a title from front matter `title`, else its first `#` heading or `<title>`, else its file name, and a kind from front matter `kind`, else the file name's first word, else `note`. The conversation rules ask only for a title; a `kind` an artifact still gives is kept but never shown (PRD R99). A name with a segment that starts with a dot is not an artifact, nor a file deeper down; a change in `<owner>/<stem>/`, the folder of files an artifact uses, is a change to that artifact. A file is resolved by real path, so a link or junction that leaves the folder lists nothing. A missing or deleted folder lists no artifacts until it appears.
- **Hub.** `ConversationService` watches the artifact folder and the external artifacts of each work item that a page with a stream shows, and stops once no such stream remains. `hello` carries `artifactsDir` and `artifacts`, newest first, and each change goes as an `artifact` event to the pages of that work item only.
- **External artifacts.** A line `[artifact: <path>]` alone in a reply, outside code, with an optional ` | title: <t>`, and a ` | kind: <k>` still accepted though the rules no longer ask for it, names a file outside the artifact folder as an artifact, so a session lists a file a skill already writes in its own folder, such as a report or a plan, without copying it (`splitArtifacts` in `packages/conversation/src/tags.ts`). The page never shows the line. Only a reply's transcript copy counts, as for heard tags, since a screen copy may be withdrawn. The hub resolves the path against the session's folder when it is relative and keeps the file's real path in the store by work item (`externalArtifacts`), with the latest tag's title and kind; it drops, with a log line, a tag naming no file, a file in a `.git` folder or the Switchboard home, which holds the hub's keys, or a file of the artifact folder, which lists it already. `ExternalArtifacts` (`packages/conversation/src/artifacts.ts`) lists each kept file while it is a file at that real path, named `@<id>/<file name>` so it never meets a name of the folder, with its type by extension, the tag's kind and title or else the file's as for the folder's artifacts, its time from the file's, and its real path as `file`; it watches each file's folder and reads every file, and up to 500 entries of its `<stem>/` folder, again every two seconds, so a change to it or to a file it uses, a deletion or a return shows. In the folder and outside it, a file rewritten with the same bytes, as a git checkout or rebase does, is not a new version and keeps its time; a file over 8 MiB is not compared, so any rewrite of it counts.
- **Opening** (voice-design § 4.4). Only an artifact of the list the page was sent opens, in a tab of its own on the page, served by real path inside its folder, or, for an external artifact, that listed file and any file under the folder it is in: an HTML or SVG page in a frame sandboxed to scripts only, a PDF in the browser's viewer, pictures, audio and video in the page, Markdown with its Mermaid diagrams drawn by the page's renderer, and text, data and code escaped ([Web page](#web-page), Artifacts and Markdown). An external artifact's whole folder is served, not only `<stem>/`, so an HTML report's pictures beside it show in its frame, as a review's `screenshots/` do (the operator's decision, 2026-11-25). It accepts that the page key, which the route takes without the cookie, reads any file under that folder, such as a whole checkout for a tagged `README.md`, from this computer or a paired device, beyond what the local file routes give a paired device; `.git`, the Switchboard home, a link or junction out of the folder and the name rules below still refuse. Only `<stem>/` counts toward the artifact's version, so a changed picture elsewhere in that folder shows when the artifact changes or the page reloads, since watching a whole checkout would be costly. A Markdown artifact's picture or relative link that the artifact route does not serve, such as `../`, an absolute path or a `file:` URI, is read by its absolute path, resolved against the artifact's own folder, through the local file routes and under their rules ([Web page](#web-page), Local files), so it grants no access those routes do not; a picture the page cannot show leaves a note naming it. An HTML or SVG page in its frame sends no cookie, so it reaches only what the artifact route serves. A new version of an open artifact replaces it.

### Conversation API

Version 1. The web page posts these commands with its cookie, its own `Origin` and its page key; on loopback the hub token reaches the same `POST` routes, as tests do, still naming an open page's key. The page's own routes are under [Web page](#web-page).

The page reads the API's views with the types in `packages/protocol`, the fields it reads, since it cannot import the hub, which bundles it. `packages/hub/src/protocol.ts` checks at build time that each hub view the page gets still carries every one of those fields with a type the page accepts, so a change to either side that breaks the other fails the hub's build. Those types are the authority for the views' fields; this section states what the fields mean where the protocol depends on it.

Every command below is a `POST /v1/conversation/<name>` whose body names `window`, the page key, `scopeGen`, the scope generation the page was shown, and `opId`. Unless a route says otherwise, a key no open page holds or a target outside the page's scope is refused with 403, a stale `scopeGen` with 409, and a malformed body with 400. A read repeats on a repeated `opId`; a command never repeats its change.

- `GET /v1/conversation/events?page=1` (cookie): the page's SSE stream, whose `hello` gives it its page key ([Web page](#web-page), Page key). Each event is `{v: 1, seq, type, at, …}` with `id: <seq>`. `hello`, when it opens and on every reconnect, carries the page's whole state: its scope ([Window scope](#window-scope)), with the dropdown's conversations, the group's own work item first; the sessions; the current session's newest items in conversation order, withdrawn ones left out, with `more`; the state History shows; the unread count; the artifact folder and artifacts; the voice state ([Voice](#voice)) and the view settings. `scope` followed by a fresh `hello` comes when the selected conversation or its current session changes, and `conversations` when a dropdown entry changes and the selection does not. The other events carry one change each: `session`, `message`, `reply`, `state` (the state History shows, sent again when it changes, is marked seen or its session's turn or session ends), `artifact` (with `removed: true` when it is gone), `unread {workItemId, count}` ([Unread](#unread)), `voice` and `settings`; `progress` while a reply of the page's session plays; and, to the page dictation goes to ([Dictation](#dictation)), `words` as an utterance's live words change and `draft` when the hub changed the composer's draft.
- **Sessions.** `SessionView.conversation` says what the page can do: `full` for a live Code Mode session with hooks on and for a Codex app-server session with a thread ID, ended or not, since a message resumes an ended one; `read-only` for a Code Mode session that ended or is unobserved; and `none` for a background session; each with its reason. `context` is the session's context size ([Context meter](#context-meter)). `background` marks a `running` session whose turn ended while background tasks it started still run, so it is in no turn, with how many agents, shells and other tasks they are; it is null otherwise.
- **Messages and replies.** A page places each item by its `ord` and whether it is in the transcript yet (`provisional`, [Reading a session](#reading-a-session)), so an item moves when its transcript line is read. A typed message's `delivery` is `open`, then `sent`; a `message` event with `delivery: 'not-sent'` and `error` removes a message that typed nothing or that Claude Code did not take, with `giveBack: true` when every page showing the session is to put its text back in the composer; `delivery: 'refused'`, sent only to the page that dictated it, is a spoken turn the hub refused before storing anything. A dictated message carries its recognised pieces and whether it is a `[partial]` part ([Dictation](#dictation)), and one that spoke over a reply what the reply had said ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)). A reply carries its kind, what it answers, its rewrite links, its unread flag, its primary response and tone line, and its playback state ([Voice](#voice)).
- **Artifacts and states.** An artifact's `path` is `<owner>/<file>` in `artifactsDir`, or an external artifact's `@<id>/<file name>` with its real path in `file`, and its `type` one of the [Artifacts](#artifacts) types. A state's `stale` is null, `turn` or `session` ([States](#states)); one of another session is never unread.
- `history {sessionId, before, limit?}` answers `{items, more}`, the items before the item `before` names (null for the newest; one that is gone, or another session's, has none before it), in conversation order, at most 100; 403 for a session that is not the page's current one, or the earlier one it shows. It only reads.
- `states {sessionId, before, limit?}` answers `{states, more}`, the current session's states before `before`, newest first, at most 50; refused as `history` is. It only reads.
- `seen {sessionId, ids}` marks at most 200 replies and states of the page's current session seen, each on its own, and answers `{unread}`, the session's count; the pages showing it get each marked item again and `unread`. Refused as `history` is, and with 403, marking none, for an ID that is not a reply or state of that session; 400 for more IDs. Marking again changes nothing.
- `send {sessionId, launch, text}`, or `send {sessionId, launch, answers}` from a question card, whose text the hub writes (PRD R103), types `text`, at most 10,000 characters, into the page's current session ([Typing a message](#typing-a-message)) and answers `{message}` at once, with `delivery: 'open'`; `message` events bring its delivery. A repeated `opId` that passes the scope checks answers the message it added to that session and types nothing again. Refused with 409 and the session's reason when its conversation is not `full`, with 409 when `launch` is not the session's, and with 400 for an empty or longer text. `command: true` types a slash command chosen from the composer's list without the escape ([Slash commands](#slash-commands)); 400 for any text with it that is not one line starting with `/name`. `heard` and `composerSeq`, from the composer, say which dictation its text holds and order it after the composer's updates ([Dictation](#dictation)).
- `card-draft {sessionId, card, draft}` keeps a card's draft for every page and the voice, and `card-dismiss {sessionId, card, slug}` dismisses an open question; both answer `{done: true}`, 409 for a card or question that is not open, and send the pages showing the session a `questions` event ([Conversation view](#conversation-view), Question cards).
- `commands {sessionId}` answers `{commands}`, the current session's slash commands as `{name, description}` without the slash, the built-ins first ([Slash commands](#slash-commands)); refused as `history` is. It only reads.
- `providers {}` reads `{defaultProvider, installed}`, the current `sessions.provider` and the providers whose CLI the hub finds, from `sessions.claudePath` and `sessions.codexPath`, without creating work or changing settings. Creation routes accept only `claude` or `codex` when `provider` is present; any other value, null included, gets 400. A repeated creation `opId` keeps its first result even when the retry names another provider.
- `select {workItemId, sessionId?}` binds the page to any work item the store knows, or to one of its earlier sessions, and answers `{scopeGen}`; the page gets `scope` and a fresh `hello` ([Web page](#web-page), Page key and Earlier sessions). 403 for an unknown work item or a session of another; a stale `scopeGen` is not refused, since the command names its whole target.
- `mic {on}` turns the mic on, moving the voice to the page's session first, or off, and answers `{on}`; 409 from a page the voice is not in. `composer {focused, draft?, listen?, seq?, heard?}` tells the hub the composer's focus and draft, at most 10,000 characters, or only its focus without `draft`, and with `listen: true` that its focus keeps capture running, for the focus rule, and answers `{done: true}` ([Dictation](#dictation)).
- `talk-here`, `stop-speaking`, `speak-replies {on}` and `replay {replyId}` ([Voice](#voice)): 403 for `replay` of another session's reply, 409 with the voice's refusal, such as voice on another session than the page's, no session shown, no voice process, `voice.enabled` off, a session that is not live or a reply with no primary response, and 404 from a hub with no voice; `speak-replies` answers `{on}`, and since Spoken replies are the hub's, not a conversation's, it needs only a page the hub knows, at any scope, so Settings sends it with no work item open.

### Conversation view

The web page's conversation pane (`packages/web/src`: `header.tsx`, `timeline.tsx`, `composer.tsx` and `side.tsx`, driven by `main.ts`) shows the selected work item's conversations (PRD R96–R104). It applies its stream's events to the page's model (`packages/web/src/model.ts`) and draws from it ([Web page](#web-page), Drawing). What it shares with the hub (question parsing, message metadata, reply bodies and text sizes, voice controls, menus and the composer's styles) lives in `packages/conversation`.

- **Header:** the work item's title, the session dropdown (PRD R102), the row's main control as a button and its actions menu ([Web page](#web-page), Row actions), and no view options menu: Show message times and notes and Fold reply details are settings ([Settings](#settings)), and Spoken replies is turned on from Settings (Speech, below) (operator decision 2026-11-26). Each of the dropdown's rows draws a session with its state codicon, its provider's mark and its name ([Web page](#web-page), Earlier sessions), and the unread dot ([Unread](#unread)) on a conversation not selected. Its button shows the shown session's state codicon and provider's mark, and the work item's title over the session's name, smaller and muted, said once where the session is named as its work item, so a phone shows the title rather than a cut session name. State codicons come from `sessionIcon` in `packages/core/src/group.ts`, which takes them from the list's marks with the same meaning (`MARK_ICONS`), so the page draws one icon per meaning. The dropdown lists the selected work item's conversations ([Window scope](#window-scope)) and the shown one's earlier sessions, and ends with New session in this work item… ([Web page](#web-page), New session in this work item); choosing a conversation posts `select`, after which every tab starts from that conversation's `hello`. A refusal shows in the toast.
- **Menus:** every menu the page opens (the session dropdown, the actions and row menus, and the tab strip's More menu) is drawn as VS Code's context menus, from the `menu.*` colours of the page's theme, and placed by `placeMenus` (`packages/conversation/src/menuPlace.ts` with `menu.css`) on every draw, resize and scroll: under the button that opened it with their left edges lined up, extending right and down as a desktop menu does; lined up with the button's right edge instead only when it would pass the page's right edge and has more room to the left, and above the button only when it would pass the bottom and has more room above; as tall as the room on the side it extends to, with no cap of its own, so it hides no row while the page has room for it; a menu that fits on neither side scrolls inside itself, and a fade with a chevron at each edge it has rows past shows that it scrolls, since a touch screen shows no scroll bar. Up and Down move between the rows, Enter or a click chooses, and Escape or a press outside closes the menu, giving the focus back to its button. The More menu also closes when a tab is chosen any way, by a press, a key or one of its rows, when another work item shows, when the page's layout moves the tabs to the other strip, and when its strip draws no More, so a More shown later starts closed. One Escape does one thing, to the innermost open part: an Escape the composer uses to close its list or stop a reply's speech closes no menu, drawer or maximized column as well. A question card's menu keeps the card's look but takes the same keys and presses, and opens above its button where the conversation's visible box has no room below it. A press in an embedded website's or an artifact's frame reaches the page only as its window's blur, so every open menu, not a sheet or dialog, closes when the page's window loses the focus, as a desktop app's menus do. A row's menu, which can open on a write such as Mark done, opens with the focus on the menu and no row chosen, so Enter alone runs nothing; it is named for the row's title.
- **Tabs:** Chat, History and the artifacts (PRD R98), with the page's other tabs ([Web page](#web-page), Tabs). History carries the unread dot ([Unread](#unread)) while its state is unread, an artifact's tab while this device has not shown its current version, and More its count with the dot while any row in its menu is unread. A tab's dot lies in its end padding, or in its top corner on a tab with a close mark, so showing it changes neither the tab's width nor its label's place. A page with no work item selected shows no tabs.
- **State:** History pins the state under the ticket's title (PRD R98, [States](#states)): Now, As of the last turn or Last state, the session that wrote it, its time and how long ago, the headline, and the body as Markdown; with none, that the session has set no state yet.
- **Timeline:** the conversation in its order ([Reading a session](#reading-a-session)), drawn as PRD R96 describes: the operator's messages as bubbles on the right at 110% of the theme's text size, replies whole as Markdown with no bubble, their primary response at that size and their details, code, lists and tables included, at the theme's size (`--sb-reply-scale` and `--sb-details-scale` in `packages/conversation/src/bubbles.css`), asides dimmed and smaller, notes centred and a rewrite in the place of the reply it rewrote, with the earlier version a click away. A message that was not sent, or was withdrawn, is left out; a provisional one is dimmed. Each item's metadata (its marks and time) is one line under it (`metaLine` in `packages/conversation/src/messageMeta.ts`); a day's divider comes before the first item and each turn that starts on a later day than the turns above it, so an item dated before them, such as a provisional message queued days ago, starts none; times show on the first item after each day's divider, or on every item with **Show message times and notes** (`web.messageTimes`, off by default) on, one setting for every page; the local storage key an earlier page kept it in is removed on load. Each artifact written no earlier than the oldest item shown is a row placed by its write time (`timeline` in `packages/web/src/model.ts`), since an artifact has no transcript line, and opens its tab ([Artifacts](#artifacts)); an earlier session's timeline ends where the next session of the work item started (`ConversationEntry.sessionStartedAt` and each `SessionEntry.startedAt`), so an artifact shows in the session that was the work item's newest when it was last written. A turn row sits after the newest item while the session is in a turn, starting or running other than with only background tasks left (`inTurn`), after any reply written mid-turn and in a turn that no message started, such as one a task notification starts; and, before a hook says a turn started, while no reply answers the operator's latest message, not counting an unconfirmed one, or a later one (`awaitingReply`). It goes when the turn or the session ends, and shows no tool activity. **Show earlier messages** pages back with `history`.
- **Speech** ([Voice](#voice)): a reply's primary response and its details after it are plain text set apart by their sizes alone, the same whether or not it was spoken, and the primary response shows once: a reply that is all primary, besides questions that end it and show in its card, is only its primary response and card; any other reply shows whole unless Fold reply details (`voice.briefReplies`, off by default) is on, when its primary response shows first with the details folded (focused mode, voice-design decision 21), and opening it is for good, with no Show less (operator decision 2026-11-22) (`replyBody` in `packages/conversation/src/replyBody.ts`). Fold reply details was named Brief replies until 2026-11-24, and keeps its key, `voice.briefReplies`, so no saved choice moves (operator decision 2026-11-24). The newest reply, not an aside or a note, shows whole even with Fold reply details on, until a newer reply comes and folds it; a reply opened by a click stays open (operator decision 2026-11-24). A newest reply whose details come before or around its primary response, as an older reply's `[speak]` passage can have, stays open once shown whole, since folding would move its primary response above them. Folding changes only what is drawn: what a spoken reply reads is its primary response either way. The page shows the first lines of the details under the primary response, two whole lines of text and then three more fading into the background on an ease-out curve, dropping fast at first and then holding a low tail that fades slowly through all three (operator decision 2026-11-24), counted from the lines drawn, so the space around a heading or a list counts as none; details that run less than a line and a half past their third line show in full, with nothing to open. The newest reply draws its details in the box the fade would, as an opened reply does, so opening a reply whose details follow its primary response, or folding the newest once a newer reply comes, changes only the fade's height and mask, and none of the lines it shows moves. A click or a tap anywhere on the reply but on a link, a button or a fold in it opens it, as does Enter or Space on the fade, which takes the focus as the reply's control, named "Show the rest of this reply" with `aria-expanded` false; there is no Show more link or other visible text, since the fade alone says more follows (operator decision 2026-11-24). The faded text is hidden from screen readers and its links take no keyboard focus, but a click on a link in its visible lines follows the link and leaves the reply folded (operator decision 2026-11-24); a tap that scrolls the page fires no click, so it opens nothing. Brief replies, now Fold reply details, replaced Expand replies, its inverse: the hub reads `voice.expandReplies` as its inverse in a file with no `voice.briefReplies`, and the settings editors read it as `voice.briefReplies` and save only that key (`withBriefReplies` in `packages/hub/src/config.ts`); the hub's `settings` event still carries `expandReplies`, the inverse, beside it. Fold reply details is set only in Settings, and the `settings` event redraws every page, a paired device's included, which follows the setting too; the local storage keys a paired device kept its own choice in, and Expand replies before it, are removed on load (operator decision 2026-11-26). A passage not found in the reply's text leaves the whole reply plain. Routine speech states show nothing; a passage the operator spoke over (`interrupted`) shows the part not heard dimmed, and, with Show message times and notes on, a reply whose speech `failed` is marked "Not spoken", its tooltip pointing to the hub log, which the page can show (`spokenMark` in `packages/conversation/src/messageMeta.ts`). Escape while the page's session speaks posts `stop-speaking`. While `voice.enabled` is on, the composer's footer keeps the message tools on its left and every voice control in one cluster beside Send (voice-design report IA-3): while the voice is here, Spoken replies' speaker while they are on or a reply plays, and the mic button ([Dictation](#dictation)); with Spoken replies off and nothing playing the composer shows no speaker, since they are experimental and are turned on from Settings' Spoken replies page ([Settings](#settings)), whose **Speak replies** switch posts `speak-replies {on}` at once (operator decision 2026-11-26); while it is elsewhere, only **Talk here**, and only for a `full` session, since the hub refuses Talk here for any other. While the voice is here but no device has it, after the phone left, **Talk here** stands before Spoken replies, and the list header's note says so, untoned, since it is a state rather than a problem, so a reply is never skipped unnoticed ([Remote access](#remote-access), Voice on a phone). There is no Stop button: while the page's session speaks, a click on Spoken replies turns it off, which stops the reply playing at once ([Voice](#voice), What is spoken); while Spoken replies is off a reply plays only as a Replay, and the same speaker then stops it and leaves Spoken replies off; Escape stops speaking from the keyboard. The message tools and the context meter keep the footer's left and never shrink under their buttons, and the voice cluster, the mic and Send stay together on its right; where both do not fit, the right group wraps whole onto a line of its own, so no voice control is ever drawn beside the context meter. One renderer draws the cluster (`renderVoiceControls` in `packages/conversation/src/voiceControls.ts`). There is no voice status text: the mic button carries the mic's state. The footer's buttons are redrawn only when their HTML changes, so a state event between a press and its release does not swallow the click.
- **Question cards** (PRD R103): a reply, not an aside or a note, that asks numbered questions with options lettered from A gets a card under it; History shows a state's questions as plain Markdown, with no card. `parseQuestions` (`packages/conversation/src/questions.ts`) reads the Markdown outside fenced code into each question's slug, topic, text and context, and each option's label, description and `(recommended)` mark; the context and a description keep their lines, blank lines and relative indentation as Markdown, and a numbered line indented to the question's text, before the first option and without a `{#slug}`, is a list in the context, not a question; a backticked slug that leads the question before a dash or colon is left out of its text and every view (a line without `{#slug}` still derives its id from the whole line, so answers stored before stay linked), and a description may follow `(recommended): ` as well as ` — `; a missing slug is derived from the topic or the first significant words. A slug used twice in a reply keeps its first question; each later one gets the first of `-2`, `-3`, … that no other question of the reply uses, cut to fit the 40-character limit, so each question of a reply has its own id. A numbered item without options is no question, and a letter out of order, a repeated number, or a lone run of options beside other questions gives no card. The `{#slug}` ids and `[withdraw: …]` lines are left out of every text the page shows.
  - **State.** `questionLedger` (shared by the hub and the web page) gives each question, keyed `reply:<reply ID>#<slug>`, a state: `open` once asked; `answered` or `dismissed` by the answers stored on a message; `withdrawn` by a later reply's `[withdraw: slug]` (or `all`), with its reason, or when a `[rewrite]` reply no longer asks it; `replaced` when a later reply asks the same slug while it is open. A rewrite keeps the state of the questions it asks again. A slug asked again after an answer opens a new question. A message that answers nothing changes nothing, so a question stays open until it is settled, and a turn that ends with one open gives the work item's row the Answer a question mark until none is. Replies from before the cutover, `questionsLedgerSince` in the hub's page preferences, recorded at the hub's first start on this version, keep the earlier rules: the first message after a card answers or skips it, and a later reply supersedes it.
  - **Past retention.** A question outlives the items retention deletes. Just before the store deletes a session's oldest items, the hub folds them into the session's carried questions (`questionCarry`): each question still open, dismissed with no answer message telling the session yet, or waiting for its reply's rewrite, as it stood. The hub's ledger starts from them, so such a question still counts, can be answered and is told after any number of later items and a restart. Questions deleted before the carry existed are not recovered, and cards from before the cutover are not carried. The web page draws the cards of carried questions whose replies it does not hold in a **Still open** section at the top of the conversation, from the hub's `carried` list: each carried card, as the hub's ledger has it, while one of its questions is open or its answer is still being sent. The page takes that state as current rather than reading the older items it holds against it again; only an answer it holds that is stored linked to a question still open settles that question before the hub's next `questions` event. Each is the usual card, with its answers, Comment, Skip this question, partial Send and one question at a time, and it leaves the section once its questions are answered, skipped or withdrawn; the waiting chip goes to it when it holds the oldest open question (operator decision 2026-11-23). The runtime and the conversation service share one ledger per session until its conversation changes (`QuestionIndex`), so one operation reads the store once.
  - **Answers are linked, not inferred.** The hub resolves each message once, when it stores it, against the questions open then (`resolveAnswers`): `[answers]` lines by slug, `slug B`, `slug comment: …`, a lone letter for the current question of the card the operator works in, and legacy `1A` against the newest card only; and stores the links in the message's `answers` column, `[]` when it answers none. A message stored before the column, or read from the transcript, is read by its text in conversation order. A card's Send posts `send {answers: [{card, slug, letters, other}]}`; the hub refuses a body that answers one question twice (400), checks each answer against the ledger, refuses one no longer open, or a message over the length limit, with why, and writes the `[answers]` message (`answerMessage`), one line per question, `- slug: question → A) label; C) label; Comment: text`, the options or the comment alone when only one was given, with `No answer (dismissed)` lines for dismissals no answer message has told the session yet.
  - **Drafts on the hub.** Each card's draft, its current question and the picks so far, is kept by the hub (`card-draft`) for every page and the voice, so a card started on one device goes on on another. A pick for a question asked again, by a rewrite or the same slug, moves to the new question when its options' labels match. An answer message that is not sent reopens its questions with their picks, and the dismissals it told are told by the next one. The web page keeps a card's draft as its own until a `questions` event carries the draft it sent last, so an event from before the hub took it cannot replace a newer comment; it sends a comment once the typing pauses in that card, to the session it was typed in, or before it opens another work item. The hub sends a session's questions in `hello` and as `questions` events: the cutover, the open count across the conversation, the oldest open card, the drafts, the dismissed keys and the cards carried past retention.
  - **The card** (`renderQuestionCard` in `packages/conversation/src/questionCard.ts` with `questionCard.css`) shows one question at a time, at most four at once with the rest after them: progress dots, the topic, question and context, full-width options with their labels and descriptions and no letters, each with a radio mark, or a check mark and a "Select all that apply" hint for a multi-choice question (one whose text or context asks for several, as the rules' closing "Select all that apply." does, which the card then leaves out of the question's text), a Comment field always under them, and Back with Next, or Send on the last step, which sends the answered questions only ("Send 2 of 3"). The context and the descriptions show their paragraphs, lists, emphasis, code and links (`renderCardText` in `packages/web/src/markdown.ts`), and any other Markdown, such as a heading, a table, a picture or raw HTML, as text, since sessions write lists there to set out what a choice covers; the question and the labels stay inline. The voice reads each list item and paragraph as a sentence. A pick and a comment go together, and either alone answers the question; a click on the picked single-choice option drops it, so a comment can stand alone. A pick does not move on, so a comment can follow it (operator decision 2026-11-25). While the card has the focus, 1–8 pick the option at that place, Down and Up move between the options, Enter goes on or sends, also from the Comment field, where Shift+Enter adds a line, and Backspace goes back; with no letters shown, letters pick nothing. The comment is the `other` field of the drafts, the `send` body and the stored answers, its name from when it was an Other answer, so no stored data changed; a message stored with `Other:` reads as a comment. Skip this question is in its menu. Questions closed while others are open show as rows under it with a state icon, one of Answered, Skipped, Unanswered (not answered by the next message, or a later reply came first), Withdrawn or Replaced, and the reason or a link to the replacing question; a card with none open shows its header and a link to the answer at its right, and folds to that header when every question is answered and none is still being sent; otherwise it keeps its rows, each with a state icon. A row names its question by its short form (`questionBrief`: the topic, else the sentence that asks) and an answer by the chosen labels (`answerBrief`), as "Short question: Choice". Slugs never show on the page; they stay in the data, the links and the `[answers]` text the session reads. The card never scrolls or takes a touch gesture of its own, so a pan over it scrolls the conversation. It takes no answer while the composer could not send.
  - **Answers in the conversation.** An answer message shows as an Answers bubble, its tag and slugs hidden, a "Short question: Choice" line per question and a link to its card at the right of its header, as the card has its Your answer link; any other message that answered questions quotes them over its bubble. A chip above the composer's box, under the session's note, on every tab, says how many questions are open across the conversation while the oldest open card is out of view, and goes to it. It takes a line of its own rather than float, so it never covers the conversation, the turn row, a note or the box at any size.
- **Codex requests:** a Codex session's pending questions and approvals (`SessionView.requests`) show as cards at the end of the timeline, in place of the turn row; a click posts `answer` with no dialog, and a refusal shows in the card. A request the page cannot answer says to answer it in the terminal. A taken request leaves with the hub's next state.
- **Background work:** while the session's turn has ended but its background agents, shell commands or other background tasks still run (`SessionView.background`, from the state detail), a quiet line with the spinner says how many, such as "3 agents running", at the end of the timeline when no turn row is due, until a `Stop` lists none. A turn row, as for a message sent meanwhile, takes its place and shows no count.
- **Waiting in the terminal:** while the session waits on the operator in its terminal (`waitingFor` `question`, `permission` or `start-prompt`) and has no Codex request the page answers itself, the turn row says what it waits for, in the warning colour, whether or not a reply is due; a permission prompt or question of a Claude Code Mode session the page reads from the screen and answers key by key ([Web page](#web-page), Prompts in the terminal), and the Console tab shows the terminal itself.
- **Composer** (PRD R97, R101, [Typing a message](#typing-a-message)): drawn by `composer.css` (`packages/conversation/src/composer.css`). Enter posts `send` with the scope generation, session and launch the page showed, so a message is refused rather than sent to a session the page did not show, and a transient failure is tried again with the same `opId` ([Web page](#web-page), Requests in time). Shift+Enter adds a line, and an Enter that ends an input method's composition sends nothing. The box empties once the hub took the message, and on a refusal keeps the text and shows why. A message that ends `not-sent` comes back to the page that sent it, even after its scope moved: its text goes after any draft and a blank line, with why, and only into its own work item's draft, never into another conversation selected since. Each work item has its own draft, kept in local storage. The composer is disabled, with the reason, for a session that is not `full`, and hidden with no conversation. The model is not shown, since the hub does not keep it. Its extras:
  - **File mentions.** `@` lists files and folders under the session's folder from the hub's `files` route ([Web page](#web-page), Files); a chosen one goes in as Claude Code's panel writes a mention: relative to the session's folder with forward slashes, absolute outside it, quoted where the panel quotes.
  - **Attached, dropped and pasted files.** The attach button, a paste or a drop uploads each file to the hub, which writes it and answers its path ([Web page](#web-page), Attached files); the message mentions it, so Claude attaches a picture as an image (mechanics M88).
  - **Slash commands.** `/` as the box's first character lists the session's commands from `commands` ([Slash commands](#slash-commands)), asked again each time; the chosen one is sent with `command`. Each `@` or `/` list shows only the answer for the text and the selection it was asked for: Escape, a choice, any other change of the text and another selection drop an answer still on its way, and another selection or a change of the text not typed in the box, such as a dictated piece, closes an open list, as does sending. Up and Down move through an open list and a bare Enter or Tab picks; Shift+Enter still adds a line, Shift+Tab moves the focus, and Enter or Tab with Ctrl, Alt or Meta does nothing. Each list is a listbox the box controls, and the box names its active option, so a screen reader follows Up and Down while the focus stays in the box; a list that found nothing says so in an option that cannot be chosen.
  - **Message history.** Up on the box's first line and Down on its last walk the session's messages the page holds, typed and from the terminal, giving the draft back after the newest, with any command chosen in it; typing ends the walk. A recalled `/name` is never a chosen command ([Slash commands](#slash-commands)).
  - **Context meter.** The selected conversation's context meter ([Context meter](#context-meter)); its menu's Compact asks first and posts `send` with `command: true`, so a refusal shows and the box's text stays.
- **Unread:** an unread state takes the unread mark (PRD R100, [Unread](#unread)); an unread reply carries none in the conversation shown, its dot staying the reply's muted bullet, and the dropdown's dots mark the conversations not selected that have unread items. The page reports what it showed ([Unread](#unread)).
- **Copy:** code blocks and replies have copy buttons, as in Claude Code's panel; a code block copies its text and a reply its Markdown.
- **Markdown:** drawn by the page's renderer with raw HTML off ([Web page](#web-page), Markdown); a link to a web page opens as the page's links do and one to a local file or folder in a file tab ([Web page](#web-page), Links and Local files).
- The conversation follows its end until the operator scrolls away: only the operator's own scrolling decides, so a layout change that alters a height re-scrolls to the end rather than stopping the follow. Scrolled away, new items stay below and a **New messages** pill takes the operator back. Spinning icons keep spinning under reduced motion, since a still loading glyph reads as a half circle rather than work in progress (the operator's decision).

### Voice

PRD R105, voice-design slice 9 (speech out), PRD R106, slice 13 ([Personas](#personas)), PRD R108, slice 10 ([Dictation](#dictation)), PRD R110, slice 14 ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)), and PRD R112, slice 12 ([Spoken alerts](#spoken-alerts)). Voice is off until `voice.enabled` is on ([Settings](#settings)).


- **The voice process** (`packages/voice`, `voice.js`) is a process of its own on `sessions.nodePath`'s Node.js, because its capture and playback (decibri) and its recognition (sherpa-onnx) are native modules, and it keeps them out of the hub. apps/hub's standalone build carries its build, with its native platform packages, the turn model's process (`turn.js`), the GPU check (`gpucheck.js`) and the Windows helper scripts; `installVoice` installs it in `<home>/voice/<build>/` as the holder's build is installed, under its own install lock, keeping the running process's build. `<home>/voice/` also holds the operator's data (models, terms, hotwords, the glossary, voiceprints and the fragments logs), which an install never touches. One process runs per home, held by a lock and named by a record, `<home>/voice.json`, with its output in `<home>/voice.log`. It holds no conversation, session or work item state, and exits some minutes after its last hub left, or when the hub sends `shutdown`.
- **Supervision.** `VoiceLink` (`packages/hub/src/voiceLink.ts`) connects to the process the record names, checking that the welcome's `pid` is the record's; it replaces a process of another build or protocol with `shutdown`, since the process keeps nothing worth keeping, refuses a lock held with no answer, and otherwise starts the installed build detached, without VS Code's variables. `VoiceControl` (`packages/hub/src/voice.ts`) wants the process while `voice.enabled` is on and a page with a selection is connected, an enrolment runs or a phone may still take the voice back after a hub restart; it restarts a failed process at most once a minute and stops it some minutes after nothing wants it. Why the process cannot run shows as a note in the page's list header, never in the conversation.
- **Protocol**, version 1 (`packages/voice/src/protocol.ts`): JSON lines over loopback TCP, the hub its only client; `hello {token}` → `welcome {protocol, build, pid, features}`, and a hub that connects replaces the one before. Hub to voice: `settings`; `targets {targets: [{sessionId, workItemId, repo?}], focus: {sessionId, via: 'window'} | null}`, the targets being the `full` current sessions of scoped pages, and the focus the focused page's selected conversation's current session; `talk-here {sessionId}`; `speak {gen, replyId, sessionId, text, tone, turn, voice, style, personality}`, `turn` the message the reply answers and the voice fields the session's persona's, else the settings' ([Personas](#personas)); `cancel {replyId, off?}`; `stop`; `earcon {kind: 'needs-you'}`; `shutdown`; for dictation `mic {on, restore?}`, `typing {on, listen?}`, `context {sessionId, lastReply}` and `heard {heard, meant}` ([Dictation](#dictation)); `enroll {action}` ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)); `alert` and `alert-cancel` ([Spoken alerts](#spoken-alerts)); and `device`, `phone-played`, `phone-mic` and `phone-state` ([Remote access](#remote-access), Voice on a phone). Voice to hub: `active {gen, sessionId | null, reason: 'focus' | 'talk-here' | 'gone'}`, also as its greeting; `speech {gen, replyId, sessionId, state, atMs, said, reason?}`, `state` `playing`, `played`, `interrupted`, `skipped` or `failed`, and `reason` why the process itself skipped or stopped it (no device, the operator away, Stop speaking, Spoken replies turned off, spoken over, a newer turn), which both logs name; `progress {gen, replyId, sessionId, chars, atMs}` while a reply plays; `audio {mic, engine, device, recognizer, error, halfDuplex?, fullDuplex?, roomHold?}`, also in its greeting, when capture's state, where recognition runs, or a capture, playback or speech failure changes; `words`, `piece`, `utterance`, `command` and `mic-off {reason}` ([Dictation](#dictation)); `enrollment {state, error}` ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)); `alert-done` ([Spoken alerts](#spoken-alerts)); `device {device}` in its greeting ([Remote access](#remote-access), Voice on a phone); and `phone-play`, `phone-pcm`, `phone-end`, `phone-flush`, `phone-stalled` and `phone-capture` while a phone has the voice ([Remote access](#remote-access), Voice on a phone).
- **The voice target** (`Router` in `packages/voice/src/routing.ts`): one session per machine, with a generation that rises at each change. It follows the focus after half a second (PRD R105); focus moving to another page's session drops the target at once, stopping speech and dropping what waits, so nothing plays until the new target is set. Talk here sets it at once: selecting a conversation in the focused page, the page's `talk-here` from the composer's button, and the mic. With the focus outside every page, the target is dropped (`gone`) after `voice.awayMinutes`. A target no longer in `targets` is dropped. The hub mirrors the target from `active`.
- **What is spoken.** A message sent from the page to the target's session while Spoken replies is on is typed with `[spoken]` ([Typing a message](#typing-a-message)). The hub offers a reply when its transcript copy (never a screen copy) is a reply, not an aside or a note, with a primary response, not rewritten or withdrawn, whose `answers` message has `spokenRequested`, in the target's session, while Spoken replies is on: `speak` with the current generation and `spokenText` of what the voice reads of the primary response (`speechOf`): its question markup dropped, as the page shows it, up to its first list, table, code block, heading, quote or rule, its Markdown marks and link targets dropped. A reply whose primary response opens with one of those has nothing to read: it is not offered, and Replay says so; any other reply is heard as `spokenRequested` and Spoken replies decides. Each reply is offered once; a reply rewritten after it was offered is cancelled, and turning Spoken replies off (one toggle per machine, off when the hub starts, since spoken replies are experimental, operator decision 2026-11-25, and kept across a restart onto a new build) cancels everything offered with `off`: the reply playing stops at once, `interrupted` where it stopped as Stop speaking leaves it, and the rest are skipped, while alert lines play on. Replay offers a reply again while the voice is in its page, whatever Spoken replies says, and is refused while that reply is still offered. When a reply offered or replayed has an open card, a second speak item, `card:<reply ID>#<slug>`, follows its passage in the same turn and reads the card's current question only (`questionSpeech`: its place, topic, text, context and options, "Select all that apply." for a multi-choice question whose text does not say it, then "Or say Comment and your words"); the reply's own reading stops before the card's questions, which are a list.
- **Speech** (`Speech` in `packages/voice/src/speech.ts`, from claude-collab's `speech-order.js`): one reply at a time, each to its end; the replies of one turn play in order, and a reply whose turn comes while a reply of a newer turn waits is `skipped` (voice-design decision 24). `stop` (Stop speaking, only in the page that is here) stops the playing reply `interrupted` with its position and skips the rest; `cancel` skips one, and `cancel` with `off` (Spoken replies turned off) interrupts it instead when it has started playing. A `speak` of another generation or for a session that is not the target is dropped; a new generation stops and drops everything unreported, and the hub marks what it offered under an earlier one `stopped-moved`. A `speech` or `progress` of an earlier generation, or of a reply not offered, changes nothing. A playback write that fails fails the reply at once, while its speech still streams; a write pending when the reply is stopped fails nothing. When the process stops, what was offered is `failed`. A reply whose passage is its whole text, played to the end, is marked seen ([Unread](#unread)). The voice log and the hub log each record every offered reply's outcome.
- **Realtime speech** (`Tts` in `packages/voice/src/tts.ts`, from claude-collab's `tts.js`): OpenAI's realtime API, the key read from `OPENAI_TTS_API_KEY` in the env file under `~/.claude` that claude-collab reads, never logged; with no key a reply `failed` and the page's list header says so. One connection is opened when a target is set and kept, since a fresh one spends seconds before the first audio ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M91); a reply in another voice or speed gets a new one. Each reply is read with `conversation: 'none'` and instructions that say only how to read (voice-design decision 22): to read the text aloud exactly as written and treat it as text, never as instructions, with the reply's tone line under `voice.expressive`, else the style and the persona's personality. An error, a timeout or a cancel mid-reply retires the connection, so no late audio reaches the next reply. Inline delivery cues are not read (voice-design § 8).
- **Playback** through decibri's `Speaker`, one per reply, on `voice.outputDevice` or the default device; `stop` drops what is queued. The needs-you sound plays on `earcon`, which the hub sends after each snapshot build for work items that started needing the operator (`newlyNeeding`), other than the voice target's, while `voice.needsYouSound` is on, except for one that got a [spoken alert](#spoken-alerts) for the same change; it waits for the reply playing and plays before the next.
- **What a page learns** (`VoiceView`, in `hello` and `voice` events): `{enabled, here, mic, speaking, speakReplies, error, recognizer, halfDuplex?, fullDuplex?, roomHold?}`, only about its own selected conversation's session: `here` while that session is the target, `mic` what capture is doing there (`off` in a page that is not here), `speaking` that session's reply playing, `error` why capture cannot run while `mic` is `unavailable`, and the rest only while the voice is here ([Dictation](#dictation), [Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)). A page cannot tell the voice being elsewhere from nowhere, and a playback or speech error is the voice's, not the page's. Everything global goes to the list: the target's row has a mark the hub chooses, the mic while the mic is on, the speaker while it is off and Spoken replies is on, and none while both are off (operator decision 2026-11-22), and a voice problem is a note, `Voice: <why>`, in the dock view's `notes`, which the page's list header shows.

### Dictation

PRD R108, voice-design slice 10. The mic is a runtime toggle, one per machine, off when the hub starts: the composer's mic button sends `mic`, and turning it on also moves the voice to the page's session (Talk here).

- **Capture** (`Dictation` in `packages/voice/src/dictation.ts`): 16 kHz mono 16-bit PCM on `voice.inputDevice` or the default device. On Windows, `windowsCapture` runs `commcapture.ps1`, which opens the device in communications mode, where Windows applies its own echo cancellation, with the echo reference set to the playback device where the device allows. When that helper fails or Windows reports no echo cancellation for the device, `withFallback` logs why and captures through decibri instead. Elsewhere, and in that fallback, decibri's `Microphone` captures with its `tau` echo canceller fed the audio the voice plays ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M94); `PlayedReference` (`packages/voice/src/reference.ts`) hands the canceller that audio paced as it plays, since pushed as it was written it ran seconds ahead and overflowed the canceller's reference queue. Capture runs while the mic is on, a session is the target, the here page's composer does not stop it (the page's keeps capture running, as the focus rule below says), no other app records, the mic idle timeout (`voice.micIdleMinutes`) has not run out and recognition can run; otherwise the device is closed. `audio.mic` says which: `off`, `starting`, `listening`, `hearing` (an utterance is open), `typing`, `paused-other-app`, `paused-by-voice` (spoken commands, below), `idle-off` (the idle timeout ran out, or no session is the target) or `unavailable` with `error`; turning the mic on again tries again. While the recogniser is what cannot load, each attempt to listen loads it again, so a model put back needs no restart.
- **Mic on's start** ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M122, M129): loading the recogniser for a set of terms takes seconds and blocks the voice process, and was most of the wait from a tap to listening. So the recogniser is loaded ahead of Mic on, whether or not a device has the voice, while nothing plays and no utterance is open: once the voice process has its settings, as after it restarts onto a new build, for no repository's terms when no target is set yet; for the target's terms once the target has settled for a moment; and at once for the target set when a phone takes the voice, while its page opens the microphone; a load ahead that fails keeps no error, since Mic on tries again and says why; each load runs a second of silence through it, since its first decode is the slowest. Mic on loads it, when not loaded yet, while the other-app watcher starts rather than after its first answer. Once capture has gone unused for 30 minutes (`RECOGNISER_IDLE_MS`: no capture open, opening or wanted, as with the mic off, on with no target, past the mic idle timeout, after a capture error, or paused for the composer or another app, and no enrolment or spoken alert's answer), the recogniser is freed, since it holds 3.5 GB of memory and 3.3 GB of GPU memory ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M162), and the voice log says so; the turn model's process closes with it, and starts again at its next request (M166); nothing then loads ahead, for a target, the settings or an enrolment's end, so focus moving between pages does not load it back, until Mic on, a spoken alert's answer, capture for dictation or a phone taking the voice loads it again, which makes that first start wait the seconds of a load. The voice log has one line per start with each step's time (`mic on → listening in … ms: recogniser …, other-app check …, capture open …`), and the hub log a `debug` line from the `mic` command to the voice process's first state past `starting`. A page posts `mic-timing {steps, outcome}` once its tap's start ends, which the hub logs as one `info` line with the outcome and the time to it, and a `debug` line with each step as milliseconds from the tap (the phone's microphone allowed, its audio, voice socket and worklet when the tap started them, capture connected, Mic on sent, the hub's answer, the voice's confirmation); refused with 400 past 12 steps, a step past 120 s, or an outcome past 200 characters. A paired device's tap starts its audio, voice socket and microphone together; its audio worklet is named by a hash of its content, which the browser keeps for good, and the page fetches it once voice is on, so the tap loads it from the cache rather than over the remote host. A page of another build that asks for its own worklet name gets this build's, uncached.
- **Half-duplex listening** (`Dictation`): each capture stream says whether it cancels the echo (`CaptureStream.halfDuplex`). Windows' communications-mode capture does, as does the phone's ([Remote access](#remote-access), Voice on a phone); decibri's does not, since its canceller removed almost none of the echo ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M94), and over Remote Desktop the "Remote Audio" device gets no capture effects, so capture falls back to decibri there. On half-duplex capture the audio is dropped unheard while the voice plays and for `voice.halfDuplexTailMs` after, so words said meanwhile are lost and there is no barge-in; an utterance open when the voice starts playing is cut as when the composer takes the focus. `voice.duplex` decides, separately on this computer (`local`) and over Remote Desktop (`remote`): `half` always pauses, `full` never does, so talking over a reply stops it, and `auto` pauses only on capture that cancels no echo, until the echo check hears none. A changed setting applies to the open capture at once. `audio.halfDuplex` and `audio.fullDuplex` carry the reason, which the mic button shows and the voice log records.
- **Echo check** (`EchoCheck` in `echoCheck.ts`, `AutoDuplex` in `duplex.ts`), under `voice.duplex` `auto` on capture that cancels no echo, so that headphones get full duplex: after each stretch of playback the process correlates the capture's level envelope with the played audio's and judges it `echo`, `none` or `unclear` ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M98). Capture starts half-duplex and turns full duplex after enough `none` in a row (one when the playback device on this computer is headphones or a headset; the hint is not used over Remote Desktop, whose "Remote Audio" says nothing of the client's headphones); `unclear` changes nothing; `echo` makes it half-duplex at once. Full duplex goes on measuring and turns half-duplex at once on `echo` or when the words held over a reply are the reply's own (`novelWords`), as when the headphones come off; those words are then dropped. A change of the mic, the capture's reason, Remote Desktop, or the playback or capture device starts the check over, half-duplex. The voice log records each measurement and each start over.
- **Other apps recording** (`watchMicUse` in `micUse.ts`), while the mic is on: a watcher, Core Audio session notifications on Windows (`micuse.ps1`) and `pactl` on Linux, reports whether an app other than the voice process and its children records; on Windows it also reports the playback and capture devices and whether this is a Remote Desktop session, for the echo check. Capture starts only after its first answer, and a watcher that cannot run counts as no other app. When another app starts recording, the utterance being heard is dropped (no automatic send and no `[partial]`), its pieces already in the draft stay, and listening resumes when that app stops.
- **Leaving the computer** (`watchLock` in `lockWatch.ts`, `VoiceService`): when the session locks, the computer goes to sleep, or the session disconnects, the process turns the mic off with any enrolment, stops speech as Stop speaking does, logs why and sends `mic-off {reason}`, on which the hub sets its mic off. The hub sends its mic to a process it connects to as `mic {on: true, restore: true}`, which the process answers with `mic-off` while it last turned the mic off on its own and the operator has not set the mic since, so a lock no hub heard still keeps the mic off. While any reason holds, a reply offered is `skipped`, a needs-you sound dropped, `mic {on: true}` answered with `mic-off` again and an enrolment start fails. When every reason ends, speech plays again and the mic stays off until the operator turns it on. The watchers are `lockwatch.ps1` (session notifications and power broadcasts) on Windows and logind through `gdbus` on Linux; a watcher that cannot run is logged, and the mic then stays on through a lock.
- **Recognition** (`Transcriber` and `sherpaRecognizer` in `asr.ts`, from claude-collab's `asr-local.js`, and `SherpaRuntime` in `sherpa.ts`): a streaming speech model through sherpa-onnx with hotwords, on the machine: on the GPU when it can run there, else on the CPU, each with the model variant faster on it. The GPU needs Windows, the NVIDIA driver and a CUDA build of sherpa-onnx with its runtime under `<home>/voice/models/`; the build does not ship these, which, like the models, are the operator's data. The build is chosen once per process, and recognition and the voice check both use it, since Windows binds a second sherpa-onnx addon to whichever `onnxruntime.dll` loaded first. Because sherpa-onnx falls back to the CPU silently when CUDA cannot load, `gpucheck.js` first runs the CUDA build in a child process, and only a check that passes loads it; a GPU recogniser that fails to load is replaced by the CPU's. `voice.log` says once where recognition runs and why, and `audio.recognizer` carries it. One stream runs for a whole utterance; words that stopped growing settle into a piece. A new stream replaces it at each utterance's end, half-duplex resume and barge-in, and the one replaced keeps several megabytes of native memory until V8 collects it, which nothing else prompts, so the voice process runs a collection a second after the last stream was replaced, once no utterance is open and no words are being heard ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M166). The model loads when capture first starts and again when the target's term list changes, never mid-utterance ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M97). Hotwords are fixed when a recogniser loads, so other terms load another; only one is loaded at a time, since each holds gigabytes of GPU memory that sherpa-onnx frees only when V8 collects it, so the voice process frees the old one, with a collection, before the new one loads (M131), and frees it after 30 minutes unused (Mic on's start, above). The recogniser runs the fewest onnxruntime threads whose decode keeps well within real time, one on the GPU and two on the CPU, since more threads spin between chunks without decoding faster (M166). Silence is not decoded: below a fixed level, decoding stops a moment after the last loud audio or new words, and resumes with the half second before speech (M131).
- **Hotwords and the glossary** (`terms.ts`, `glossary.ts`, `hotwords.ts`, `vocabulary.ts`, from claude-collab): the fixed terms are the operator's (`<home>/voice/terms/user.txt`) and the target's repository's (`<home>/voice/terms/repos/<owner>/<repo>.txt`, the repository read from its folder's `origin` remote and sent in `targets`), then the glossary's learnt terms. A reply's heard tag (`splitHeard`, read from transcript copies only, once per reply and tag) goes to the voice as `heard`; the glossary (`<home>/voice/glossary.json`, one per home) promotes a meant term after the second identical tag, only for a name or technical term, keeping a bounded number of learnt terms, the most recently used first. The spoken commands' phrases are always hotwords.
- **Turn detection** (`turn.ts`, from claude-collab's `wrapper.js` and `turn-text.js`): an utterance ends after `voice.endPauseMs` with no new words, longer after a dangling last word. A brief quiet asks LiveKit's turn model, with the session's last reply as context (`context`, which the process keeps, and the hub counts as sent, only while the session is among the `targets`, so a session that returns gets it again), whether the words so far are a finished turn, and the more likely the end the sooner it comes; a final `.`, `?` or `!` ends it soon too. The turn model runs in its own process (`turn.js`), because onnxruntime cannot load beside sherpa-onnx's runtime, with threads that do not spin waiting for work after each request, which cost about a CPU-second a request (M166). Without the model, the pause alone decides.
- **Spoken commands** (`Dictation`): "stop listening" or "pause listening", and "start listening" or "resume listening" (`SPOKEN_COMMANDS`). A command counts only as a whole utterance that turn detection ended and that passed the voice check; its first pieces are held unreported while they may be a command and released as dictation otherwise. A stop pauses dictation (`paused-by-voice`): capture and recognition keep running but nothing is reported and there is no barge-in, and only a resume phrase whose voice match clears `voice.matchThreshold` itself, without the short-piece allowance, resumes it. Turning the mic on also resumes. A command goes to no session: it is logged in `voice.log`, and the hub logs `command` in its log and the fragments log. Without an enrolment anyone's command counts.
- **Reports**: `words {gen, utteranceId, sessionId, text}` as the live words change, empty once they settle; `piece {gen, utteranceId, sessionId, pieceId, text, score, f0, dropped}` with the voice check's results ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)); `utterance {gen, utteranceId, sessionId, text, final, interrupted, timing?}`, `text` its kept pieces, `interrupted` the reply a barge-in spoke over or null, and `final` true when turn detection ended it, false when it was cut off (the composer took the focus, the mic went off, the voice moved, the device failed or another app started recording), so the hub sends nothing. `timing` says when its words last changed, when and how the end was decided and when it was sent, for the message's timing line ([Typing a message](#typing-a-message)).
- **The focus rule, in the hub** (`VoiceControl`): the hub keeps one composer per page and conversation (session): its focus and draft from `composer`, which the page sends as they change, and the dictation in it; the page joins each piece the hub joins to the draft (a `draft` carries it as `added`) to the end of the box's text, never replacing the operator's edits or moving the caret. A focused composer stops capture (`typing {on: true}`), unless it sends `listen: true`, as the page's does: capture then keeps running (`typing {on: true, listen: true}`, which still keeps spoken alerts' answers out and cuts one being waited for) and each piece joins its draft, but no turn's end, `[partial]` or card command sends it until the operator sends it, and the page shows a hint saying so. A turn that ends during a room hold is held whether or not the composer has the focus. Dictation goes to the page the voice is in: the focused page showing the target, else the one that last moved the voice there, else any showing it. Each piece joins that page's draft (`draft {text, sessionId}`); live words, display only, go to every page showing the session. A final utterance whose page's composer does not have the focus sends the whole draft once as a `[voice]` message and empties it, unless the whole draft is a card command while the session has an open card (`cardUtterance`: a letter with the recogniser's homophones, an ordinal, "the recommended one", "comment, …" or the older "other, …", "back", "next", "repeat" or "send"): it then changes that card's draft instead, and the hub says what it did and reads the next question; a comment joins the question's pick. Once every question of the card's batch has an answer, a comment or "send" sends them as a `[spoken] [voice] [answers]` message, while a pick asks for either, since a comment could not be added once sent; one whose composer has the focus, or that was cut off, sends nothing and leaves the draft. While an utterance is open, its session is in a turn and the composer is not focused, the draft goes as a `[voice] [partial]` message at intervals, keeping the last settled piece, so the message that ends the utterance always carries words; no part goes while another app records. A message the voice typed that is then not sent goes back to the draft of every page showing its session (`giveBack`). Turning `voice.enabled` off turns the mic off. `dictate` refuses a session the page no longer shows, one that is not `full`, a message over the message length limit or a holder that cannot type, storing nothing. The composer's own `send` of a draft that holds dictation types it as a `[voice]` message with its pieces, however much the operator edited it; a slash command never. The page posts `send` and `composer` as separate requests, which can reach the hub in either order, so a send takes the draft in the same step as it accepts the message, and no update has to empty it first: a card's answers, a refused send and a repeated `opId` take nothing. Each joined piece is numbered in its `draft` event (`piece`), by one count for the hub that starts at its start time in milliseconds, so a number from another page key, or from an earlier hub unless the clock went back between the two, is below every later piece; the send carries the last number the box holds (`heard`), and only the pieces up to it go with the message, while later ones, which its text does not hold, stay as the new draft, with the reply they spoke over when their utterance spoke over one. Every `composer` and `send` carries a page-wide number (`seq`, `composerSeq`): the hub ignores a focus older than one it set and a draft older than one it set or a send took, so an update posted before Send that arrives after it cannot put the sent text back. A composer update carries `heard` too, so a draft posted before a piece arrived keeps that later piece. While a message is on its way the page posts only the focus, so nothing it posts then empties the draft.
- **The composer** (`renderMicButton` in `packages/conversation/src/voiceControls.ts`): one mic button beside Send, whose glyph and tooltip carry the mic's state, including where recognition runs and why listening pauses during replies; while the mic is on, in any state, a click turns it off, and turning it on again retries capture; a paused mic listens again on a click. From a Mic on tap until `audio.mic` confirms it (`MicStart`), the button shows starting, a turning ring around the mic, which turns under reduced motion too, as every spinner does, so the wait for the hub, the phone's microphone and the voice process to open capture is visible; a tap that fails, as when the phone refuses the microphone or the hub refuses `mic`, shows why on the button until the next tap, as does a start not confirmed within 30 seconds. A `draft` sets the box's text, or the draft of its own conversation, and one that comes while a message is being sent takes the box once the hub answered. Live words show dimmed right after the box's text and settle into it in place. A message sent while the voice is here with the mic on takes the focus from the box once the hub took it, so spoken turns send on their own again. While it is on its way the box keeps the focus, a press on Send included, so what is heard waits for the answer: the page keeps only the pieces heard meanwhile, since the hub's draft still holds the message until it takes it. Taken, the box holds those pieces, or the work item's draft does when the page shows another one by then; refused or not answered, the box keeps its text and the focus, and the pieces join its end. Any other `draft` that came meanwhile applies once the send answered, unless a piece of the same session came after it. A dictated message is marked so under its bubble, with its pieces in the tooltip, and when it spoke over a reply ([Voice check and barge-in](#voice-check-and-barge-in)).
- **Fragments log**: `<home>/voice/fragments-<local date>.jsonl`, one JSON line per piece `heard` or `dropped`, per message `submitted`, `refused`, `held` (a room hold kept its turn), `sent` or `not-sent`, and per spoken `command`.

### Voice check and barge-in

PRD R110, voice-design slice 14, from claude-collab's `voiceprint.js` and `barge.js`.

- **Voiceprint** (`Voiceprint` in `packages/voice/src/voiceprint.ts`): a speaker embedding model, `<home>/voice/models/wespeaker_en_voxceleb_resnet34_LM.onnx`, through sherpa-onnx on the CPU in the build recognition uses ([Dictation](#dictation)). Without it no piece is scored, only the pitch rule of an earlier enrolment applies, and enrolment fails with why. The enrolled print, `<home>/voice/voiceprint.json`, is the normalised mean embedding of a recording, with the recording's pitch range and speaking level, kept only on the machine. A print claude-collab enrolled reads as it is, with no level.
- **Enrolment.** Settings' Dictation page posts `page-voice-enroll {action: 'start' | 'status' | 'cancel'}` ([Settings](#settings)); the hub starts the voice process for it if no page wants it and sends `enroll`. The process cuts any utterance short, records a minute of capture that goes to no session, then saves the print, reporting `enrollment {state: 'recording' | 'enrolled' | 'failed' | 'cancelled', error}`; a print that cannot be saved fails it and the earlier print stays. The route answers `{enrollment: {state, endsAt, error, enrolledAt}}`, `state` also `idle` or `starting`, and refuses a start while `voice.enabled` is off (409); turning voice off cancels a recording, and a voice process that stops or cannot start fails it.
- **The voice check**, on every piece of dictation: the piece's recent audio is dropped when its match with the print is under `voice.matchThreshold`, with an allowance for a piece of one or two words, or when too few of its voiced frames are low enough for the enrolled voice by `voice.pitchLowFactor` and `voice.pitchMinLowShare` (voice-design decision 27); audio too short to judge passes. A dropped piece joins neither the utterance nor the draft and is logged `dropped`; an end the turn model judged on words that included it is judged again without them, so someone else's "go ahead" does not end the operator's turn.
