An end the turn model judged early waits for the check of its last words. Without an enrolment every piece passes.
- **Holding a reply for the operator** (`Speech`, `Dictation.replyHold`): a reply about to start waits while words are being recognised or an utterance holds words that passed the voice check, and until 1.5 s after the operator's last word (`REPLY_HOLD_MS`), so it never starts over the operator mid-thought, as when a short pause ended an utterance and the operator goes on ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M127); the voice log says once that it waits. A piece the voice check drops holds nothing, nor does anything heard while paused by voice, and a reply waits at most 30 s (`HOLD_MAX_MS`) on words not yet known to be the operator's, so speech that never stops, as a television's, cannot hold it for good; on the operator's checked words it waits however long they go on. Each offer of a reply waits afresh, so a replay is held as the first offer was. A reply of a newer turn offered meanwhile skips it, as when it waited behind another reply. An alert line does not wait.
- **Barge-in** (`BargeIn` in `barge.ts`), only on full-duplex capture, since half-duplex capture hears nothing while a reply plays ([Dictation](#dictation)): while a reply plays and no utterance is open, words heard are held back as its possible echo, with no live words, pieces or turn checks. Each recent window of loud audio is checked against the operator's print and against the print of the voice the reply is spoken in; it passes when it matches the operator by `voice.matchThreshold`, beats the reply voice by a margin and is pitched as the operator is, with a higher bar before that reply voice's print is learnt. A strong pass fires at once, an ordinary one when confirmed by the next; without an enrolment or the model, words heard that are not the reply's echo fire it. Firing stops the reply `interrupted` (`Speech.interrupt`), skips the replies waiting and any later reply of the same turn, and opens an utterance, starting recognition again from just before the window, that names the reply with what of its passage was played (`said`) and not (`rest`). Words heard just after a reply ends stay held and checked briefly, then are dropped as its echo. The reply voices' prints, `<home>/voice/tts-voiceprints.json`, are learnt from the start of each reply's audio.
- **The room hold** (`voice.roomHold`, on by default): a piece the voice check drops is someone else in the room, unless it may be a reply's echo (a reply playing or just stopped, or a match with a learnt reply voice's print by `voice.matchThreshold`). It starts or restarts a hold of `voice.roomHoldSeconds`, logged in `voice.log` and carried in `audio.roomHold` as its end. In the hub, a spoken turn whose end comes during a hold is not sent: its words stay in the page's draft, its pieces are logged `held`, and the page gets `held {sessionId, note}`, which the composer shows as it shows a refusal, and no `[partial]` goes. A held draft stays held after the hold ends, so a later turn joins it unsent, until Enter sends it or the operator empties the box. A held turn for a session's terminal, which has no composer, is dropped and logged. Turning `voice.roomHold` off ends a hold.
- **The interruption note.** An `utterance` with `interrupted {replyId, said, rest}` leaves it with the page's draft, so the message that sends that dictation stores it as `interrupted` and is typed with a note after its tags (`interruptNote` in the conversation library) that tells Claude how much of its reply was heard: none of it, all but a short unheard end (quoted), or up to a point, quoting the last words heard and the first unheard; none when it was all heard. A `[partial]` part never carries it, an emptied draft drops it, and `stripMessageTags` removes a note only after Switchboard's tags. The dictation rules tell Claude what the notes mean: not to repeat what was heard, and to raise the unspoken part only if it still matters.

### Spoken alerts

PRD R112, voice-design slice 12. With `voice.enabled` and `voice.spokenAlerts` on, the voice calls the operator when a `full` session that is not the voice target, nor the focused conversation, needs them.

- **In the voice process** (`Alerts` in `packages/voice/src/alerts.ts`): an `alert` waits while anything plays or waits to play, for a short gap after the voice last played, while the operator speaks, during a room hold, while another app records and during an enrolment; alerts play one at a time, in order, and a newer alert for a session replaces its earlier one unless that one is being read. Its line names the operator (`voice.operatorName`), the session's persona, the cause and the work item's title (`alertLine`, PRD R112). It plays in the speech queue as an alert line, in the alert's voice, style and personality: it is not reported to the hub, a move of the voice target does not stop it, words heard over it are held as its possible echo as over a reply, so a line never answers itself, and Stop speaking, a lock or sleep stop it. When an answer can be heard (`Dictation.answerable`: the mic on and listening normally, the composer not focused, no enrolment) dictation listens from the line's start even with no target or after the idle timeout, and the next utterance is the answer, which goes to no session. The window closes `voice.alertAnswerSeconds` after the line, later while an answer is still being spoken, and capture then returns to what it was. A refusal said alone ("no", "not now", "later" and the like, `declines`) drops the alert; any other answer moves the voice target to the session with Talk here when it can be a target and reads the alert's `detail`. With nothing heard, or no answer possible, it ends unanswered after its line. It ends cancelled when its cause is resolved (`alert-cancel`), an answer is cut off, its session becomes the voice target or the focused conversation before it is read, the mic stops listening during the window, its line is stopped, spoken alerts are turned off, or the operator leaves the computer, when every waiting alert is dropped too. Each end is `alert-done {alertId, sessionId, outcome}`, `outcome` `announced`, `unanswered`, `declined` or `cancelled`, logged in `voice.log` with the line and the answer.
- **In the hub** (`VoiceControl.sessionsChanged`, after each snapshot build): each live Code Mode session whose conversation is `full` has a kind (`alertKind`): `running`, `idle`, `question` (waiting on a question, or idle with a question open), `permission` or `other`. A change from `running` to `idle` is `turn-finished`, and a change to `question` or `permission` is that cause; the first sight of a session, as after a hub restart, alerts nothing. While `voice.enabled`, `voice.spokenAlerts` and the cause's `voice.alertOn` key are on, a voice process is connected, and the session is neither the voice target nor the focused conversation (the focused page's selected conversation), the hub sends `alert {alertId, sessionId, cause, title, persona, detail, voice, style, personality}` after a short delay, so the transcript has given the turn's last reply, checking all of it again then. The `detail` (`alertDetail`) says, for a permission prompt or a question menu, that the session waits in its terminal, else gives the latest reply's primary response, else its text, else the latest state, as speech without Markdown and cut short at a sentence, followed, while questions are open, by how many and the current question. Another change of the session's kind, or its end, drops the alert, sending `alert-cancel` once it was sent; `alert-done` is logged in the hub log. A work item alerted gets no needs-you sound for the same change.

### Personas

PRD R106, voice-design slice 13. A persona is a name, a personality and a voice with its speaking style, in the roster `voice.personas` ([Settings](#settings)), empty by default; with no personas every session speaks with `voice.voice` and `voice.style` and its prompt has no tone section. Personas are part of voice: with `voice.enabled` off no session speaks with one and no prompt gets a tone section.

- **Resolved at the start** (`resolvePersona` in `packages/core/src/persona.ts`): when the flow starts a Code Mode or Codex app-server session it takes its work item's playbook's persona (`voice.playbookPersonas`), else a persona of the roster no other live session has, else the one the fewest have, earlier in the roster first, and stores it as `Session.personaId`. A playbook's persona may be shared on purpose; the pool shares one only when the roster runs out. The persona is kept for the session's life, across resume and hub restarts; a session that started with none, such as one from before the roster, keeps none. Background sessions have none.
- **No pin.** The operator cannot pin a persona to a session: personas are assigned per playbook in Settings (PRD R106, operator decision 2026-11-26). Store migration 2026-11-26 dropped `personaPinned`, so a session pinned before keeps the persona it had, and the activity log's earlier `persona` events stay as history.
- **Speech.** `VoiceControl` reads the session's persona at each offer, so a pin changes the voice from the next spoken reply: `speak` carries its `voice`, `style` and `personality` ([Voice](#voice)).
- **Tone section** (`toneSection`): a "## Persona" section naming the persona and its personality, saying that it sets only the tone, never how the work is done, what is decided, checked or reported, or the length and format of replies, and that the last Persona section counts. None for a persona with no personality; a resume and the contract hook give a session that has a persona whose section is none (no personality, gone from the roster, or voice off) `PERSONA_RESET` in its place, since the launch's prompt may still carry an earlier section. It comes after the conversation rules at the start, in the resume's appended prompt, and in the contract hook's text on resume and compaction, read from the settings each time. Each delivery stores its `toneVersion`, a hash of the section, as `personaVersion`, so a live session whose stored version differs from its persona's current section shows `tonePending` until its next resume or compaction. A compaction keeps the launch's appended prompt beside the hook's text, so a session can see two Persona sections; it follows the last.
- **Shown.** The page shows no persona in the conversation; [Spoken alerts](#spoken-alerts) name the persona.

### Web page

PRD R109: the web page the hub serves is Switchboard's only interface, on this computer and on paired devices ([Remote access](#remote-access)). It is its own code (`packages/web`); it speaks the [Conversation API](#conversation-api) and its own routes below, and draws with the browser-safe modules it shares with the hub: the question cards' reading and renderer, the provider marks and the voice controls (`packages/conversation`), and the marks, session icons and snooze choices (`packages/core`). Its conversation pane is the [Conversation view](#conversation-view). The page sees only the work item it selected ([Window scope](#window-scope)).

- **Files.** `packages/web`'s build carries the page's files as strings (`PAGE_ASSETS` in `dist/index.js`) inside the hub bundle, so each hub serves the page that matches its build. The page draws from a copy of the colours of VS Code's default themes, 2026 Dark and 2026 Light, as the `--vscode-*` variables a webview gets (`theme.css`), and follows the operating system's light or dark setting as it changes, with no setting of its own (the operator's decision: light and dark only, no high contrast). Content (the right column's views, the work item list and the Changes tree) takes the editor's colour, the deepest of each scheme; the conversation and the bars take the side bar's (the operator's decision). Text and marks keep a 4.5:1 contrast in both copies, where a copy takes another VS Code theme's value for a colour its own theme leaves below it (`test/theme.test.ts`). Claude's mark keeps its brand orange, #D97757, on the session dropdown's rows, hovered, focused or selected as well, as Codex's mark keeps its colour; there it stays 3:1 or more, a mark's minimum, rather than 4.5:1 (the operator's decision). Row stripes, the hidden list's sliver and the Returned pill's outline take status colours of their own: warning amber, error red and the operator's turn blue, each 3:1 or more on every row background, with warning clearly lighter than error so the two stay apart with protanopia or deuteranopia; text in the warning or error colour keeps VS Code's text colours. Mermaid's build, too large to carry in the bundle, is installed under the Switchboard home with the other carried files ([Hub lifecycle](#hub-lifecycle)); without it diagrams show as their code. Diagrams are drawn in Mermaid's light or dark theme to match the scheme and drawn again when it changes, since Mermaid bakes the colours into each drawing.
- **Port.** An installed page is tied to one origin, so the hub also serves the page's routes, and nothing else, on the fixed port `web.port` of `127.0.0.1` ([Settings](#settings)), read at hub start; the hub's own port serves them too. A taken port is tried again briefly, since a hub being replaced may still hold it, and then the hub logs why and serves the page on its own port only, which changes at each start.
- **Credential.** `POST /page/link` (hub token) answers `{url}`, a link holding a one-time token that works once within two minutes (`PageAccess` in `packages/hub/src/page.ts`), on the page's fixed port, or on the hub's port with `notice` saying why when the page is not on its fixed port. `hub.js web` gives it. `GET /page?t=` uses the token up and answers 303 to `/page/` with the cookie `sb_page` (`HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`), and 403 for a used, expired or unknown token. Tokens live in the hub's memory. Each cookie is a remembered sign-in for one device, kept in the store's `pageSessions` table only as its SHA-256 with its device's name, so it outlives a hub restart; a sign-in works only from where it was made, this computer or the remote host and login a device paired through ([Remote access](#remote-access)). A sign-in unused for 30 days is refused and deleted, and use renews it. Signing in again from a browser deletes that browser's old sign-in and ends the page streams opened with it, and the hub keeps a bounded number of sign-ins, dropping the one used longest ago. `POST /page/revoke` (hub token; `hub.js web revoke`) deletes every sign-in, ends the open pages' streams and answers `{revoked}`. `/page/` without a valid cookie answers 401 with a page saying how to sign in. Cookies are not scoped by port, so a browser also sends `sb_page` to any other server on `127.0.0.1`, which another local user's process could run; the hub token, by contrast, is readable only by the operator's account, so the page never holds the hub token.
- **Installing.** The page is a web app a Chromium browser installs in its own window, which `127.0.0.1` allows over plain HTTP since it counts as a secure context; its manifest's `id`, `start_url` and `scope` are `/page/`. The manifest, the service worker, the icons and `/page/ping`, which answers `{build, signedIn}`, need no cookie, because a browser fetches a manifest and its icons without one; they hold nothing private.
- **App shell.** The build names the page's script and stylesheets by content hash (`page-<hash>.js`), and the hub serves them under those names for good, gzipped, and `index.html` with no caching and an ETag, so a second launch fetches only the page and its stream. `index.html` paints a boot screen in the system's light or dark scheme from an inline style before any file loads, and an inline boot script, which the page's CSP allows by its hash, shows Switchboard is not reachable with Try now when the script or a stylesheet fails to load or the page has not started within 8 s. The service worker (`packages/web/src/sw.ts`) keeps each build's whole shell, the page, those files, the icons and the manifest, or fails its install, staging the files so a failure never touches a complete cache, and the worker before stays, and keeps the build before for a page of that build still open; it keeps nothing else (the operator's decision: no API answers, artifacts or files), so the page keeps no work items on the device. A page load takes the hub's answer within 3 s, a sign-in page included, and otherwise the kept page; a 502, 503 or 504 counts as no answer, because `tailscale serve` answers 502 with an empty body for a stopped hub, so a phone gets the page's own screen rather than the browser's error page (M137).
- **iPhone and iPad.** Added to the Home Screen from Safari, the page runs standalone from the manifest under the opaque default status bar (`black-translucent` gets a system blur over the page's top on iOS 26.1 and later, and WebKit treats the key as deprecated). iOS draws its scroll edge blur over the page's top unless WebKit finds a fixed or sticky box at the page's top edge, at least 90% of the width, and paints the status bar in its colour instead. Each view's own top bar is that box, with no room given up above it: the header, the list header and full-screen Settings' bar are sticky at the top of a page that never scrolls and opaque in the bars' colour. An open sheet's dimmed backdrop covers the whole screen, for which WebKit keeps the colour it had. In landscape an iPhone shows no status bar, and WebKit paints none. At the bottom the page uses the whole screen, as a native app does, and keeps its last item clear of the home indicator. The page never scrolls the window itself: a script that scrolled it back while the keyboard showed put the composer under the keyboard. On an iPhone in the installed app, portrait, iOS scrolled the document to keep the focused composer above the keyboard and back to 0 when it closed (M155); iPadOS, landscape and Safari outside the app are unverified. A text field never makes iOS zoom the page when it takes the focus, and iOS never enlarges a long block's text in landscape, so every text keeps its set size in either orientation. The Home Screen icon is opaque, since iOS fills a transparent icon with black, and needs no cookie, as the other icons; iOS reads these only when the page is added, so an installed copy takes a change to them only once it is removed and added again.
- **Sending.** A send, from the composer or a question card, brings the conversation to its end wherever the operator had scrolled, and the conversation follows its end from there, with the sent message, the turn row and the reply, until the operator scrolls away.
- **Drawing.** A burst of stream events, such as a long transcript read as new, is drawn together rather than once per event, so it cannot hold up the browser; what reads the drawn page, such as an embedded frame's rectangle, waits for that draw.
- **Width.** The page never scrolls or bounces as a whole; its list and panes scroll only up and down, and a reply's long links and words wrap while its code blocks and tables scroll inside their own box, so nothing makes the page wider than the screen. Nothing outside the scrollers moves either: no pane, popup or dialog that scrolls passes a drag on to the page, at its end or with nothing to scroll, and a one-finger pan that starts outside every scroller does nothing while a pinch still zooms, because WebKit on iOS still pans a page with `overflow: hidden` while its visual viewport is shorter than its layout viewport. A vertical pan that starts on anything inside a pane, such as a table or code block that scrolls sideways, a nested scroller or a title cut short, scrolls that pane, so only the panes, popups and dialogs keep a drag to themselves; Chrome gives a pan to the innermost box that keeps its drags even when that box cannot scroll that way (M125).
- **Connection.** One state drives every surface, the same on this computer and on a paired device (`Connection` in `packages/web/src/connection.ts`): connecting until the first `hello`, live while events or the hub's `tick`, sent every 15 s in place of the heartbeat comment a browser never shows the page, keep arriving, reconnecting for the first 4 s after a loss, offline after that, and signed out once `/page/ping` says the cookie no longer signs in. The page owns its stream: on any error it closes it and, after asking `/page/ping`, opens a new one with backoff from half a second to 30 s with jitter, so a refused connection and a proxy's 502, which a browser's own reconnect treats differently, behave the same; a stream silent for 40 s, or with no `hello` within 10 s, is given up, and a page brought back to the front, online again or out of the back-forward cache tries at once. Nothing the operator sees changes during the grace period, a new stream's first `hello` included; then a banner says the page is offline and since when, with Retry, what it shows dims, and the composer, the row actions and other commands are off with a one-line reason (the operator's decision: no queue of messages sent offline). A phone's voice socket that brings nothing, not even a pong, for three pings is taken for dropped. A page that never connected shows Switchboard is not reachable in its place, with the time to the next try, Try now, and on this computer Start Switchboard, which opens the `switchboard:` link ([Hub lifecycle](#hub-lifecycle)); a signed-out page shows Signed out with the next step for the device. A dot in the heads is green while live, amber offline and grey after a minute. The page never reloads by itself: a hub of another build shows an Update ready card above the composer, in the accent colour with what Reload does, whose Reload reloads (the operator's decision), and a reconnect to the same build keeps the page as it is. Drafts live in local storage.
- **Requests in time.** Every request the page makes ends within a time limit (`request` and `timedFetch` in `packages/web/src/request.ts`): opening a work item 10 s, a command 20 s, a read 15 s and an upload 120 s, the body's reading included, so no lock waits on a hung request. A failure reads as one sentence: no answer, too slow, Switchboard restarting for a 502, 503 or 504, signed out, or the hub's own reason for a 403 or 409, except the stale-generation refusal, which the page words itself ([Window scope](#window-scope), Refusals); the status goes to the console. A message's send and a card's answer are tried again for 30 s on a transient failure, each try with the page key the page has then and the same `opId`, which the hub answers once, and the same text sent again from the same work item, after a failure or a reload, reuses that `opId`, kept in local storage until the hub takes the message, refuses it for good or the work item sends another, so a message whose answer was lost is never typed twice (M137). A 2xx answer stands only with its body.
- **Opening a work item.** Another work item opens under its own title from the list with a placeholder at once, and its row is marked; the conversation shown before never shows under it. Nothing moves meanwhile: the list, the conversation and the History and Artifacts column keep their places and widths, the strips keep their height with History and Console as dimmed placeholders, the column shows a loading note, and the composer keeps its place hidden, so the work item's own head, tabs and composer replace them in place; an open that fails keeps the same layout. One the hub refuses, or that cannot be reached in time, shows why in its place with Try again and Back to the list, and is not asked again until Try again. A conversation of the shown work item's dropdown opens under the same head, and a refusal there keeps the shown one with why above its box.
- **Route and restore.** The address's hash names the open work item, its tab and what the tab shows (`packages/web/src/route.ts`): `#w=<work item ID>&t=<tab>` for History, Console or `artifact:<path>`, `t=link&u=<URL>` for the link tab shown, `t=changes&f=<file>` for the Changes tab at the file last gone to in its diff, `t=file&p=<target>&l=<line>` for the local file tab shown, and `#list` for the phone's list; an older address's `t=browser` reads as a link tab with no URL, `t=status`, from before History took the Status tab's place, as History, and one from before History was a tab names it as `h=<ticket ID>&hk=<key>`, which opens the work item's History tab. Local storage keeps the route as `switchboard.route`, but never `#list`, so a reload opens the address's route and an installed app the system ended and started again at `/page/` with no hash opens the work item this device last showed. One function, `applyRoute` in main.ts, applies a route at the start, on the browser's back and forward and on an address edited by hand, never on a `tabs` event: it opens the work item as a row's click does and, once its `hello` has brought its kept tabs, opens the route's tab through `openDoc` as no choice of the operator's, so it posts no `page-tabs` and never opens an artifact closed on every device, which gives way to History. Where the column folds into the conversation's strip, the route's tab becomes the column's choice and the page shows the Chat tab, as every opening does there (PRD R109), so the address then names the Chat tab. Session storage keeps a scroll place per work item and tab, the conversation's by work item and session, so an earlier session shown keeps its own place and a reload, which shows the current session, never takes it: the conversation as the first item in view and its offset, and History, an artifact's document and a file tab the page draws, as Markdown or a folder, as their own views' (History's body scrolls under its toolbar; the Changes tab, a file in the code view, which keeps its line, and the Console and link tabs scroll in their own views), when the page is hidden or goes, when another tab is chosen and when another work item opens, and a tab, or a conversation first drawn in the page's life, comes back to it; History's place waits until the ticket's History has loaded. The Changes and file tabs follow the work item, so a reconnect's new page key and a new session of the work item, as Approve, Retry or Reopen starts, keep them; the Changes tab reads its list again once the new stream shows the work item, and when another session of it starts. Each device also keeps each work item's tabs as it left them, the latest 30 work items, in local storage as `switchboard.tabs`: the column's and the conversation pane's tab and the artifact shown, whether the Changes tab was open and its file, the file tabs in their order with the one shown, and the link tabs (`LinkSet` in iframeBrowser.ts): those open, the ticket's and those added from the conversation, each with the name it was opened by, the closed ones and the one selected; updated as tabs open, close or are chosen, added to what the device's other pages kept; a set kept before History took the Status tab's place reads `status` as History and drops whether History was open. Another work item and back, or a reload, opens the work item on them once its `hello` comes, ahead of the hub's kept tab below, a tab no longer there giving way as above; the link tabs come back with or without the extension and over the extension host's reconnect, a row's links that come again never open a closed ticket link, and the kept selection holds only while its link is still among the tabs. A New session in this work item… draft left unsent is kept in local storage by work item as `switchboard.newDrafts`, with its provider, text and opId, until it is sent, cancelled or left for another work item or session, so a reload shows the draft again. The artifact tabs need no copy: they follow the work item's artifacts and the closed ones the hub keeps.
- **Requests.** Every other page route needs the cookie, and every page route a `Host` of `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, `<port>` being the one the request came in on, so a name that rebinds to this machine is refused (403), unless it comes through `tailscale serve` from a remote host ([Remote access](#remote-access)); `GET /` answers 303 to `/page/`. The page's files go out with `X-Frame-Options: DENY` and a CSP of their own that allows only the page's own scripts and its inline boot script by hash, styles, fonts, frames and connections, no objects or forms, and no framing. A page posts a conversation command without the hub token, with its cookie, an `Origin` equal to its own and a page key as `window`; anything else is 403. Only the commands in `PAGE_COMMANDS` (`packages/hub/src/server.ts`) are taken, and those in `LOCAL_PAGE_COMMANDS` only from this computer's page.
- **Page key.** `GET /v1/conversation/events?page=1` (cookie) registers a page (`WindowScopes.openPage`) and opens its conversation stream; its `hello` carries `key`, the page key, the list's `groups`, and `scope: null` until the page selects. The key dies with the stream. A page's `select` takes any work item the store knows; it binds the page to that work item, raises its `scopeGen` and sends `scope` and a fresh `hello`, and from then on the [window scope rule](#window-scope) holds: the scope is `{kind: 'page', …}` with the work item's title, its window's first folder (else its ticket's `repoRoot`) and its current session, and every other work item's session, reply, state or artifact is refused. A page's binding is never stored; a reload selects again from the route ([Web page](#web-page), Route and restore). A selected work item that is deleted unbinds the page, raising `scopeGen`. A `needs` event makes the page announce work items that started needing the operator ([Needs-you alerts](#needs-you-alerts)).
- **Earlier sessions** (PRD R109). A page's dropdown entries carry each conversation's earlier sessions, newest first (`sessions`: ID, title, state, start and provider); the page draws the shown conversation's under its row, at the same edge. Each entry also carries `sessionTitle`, its current session's name, so every row of the page's dropdown, the current session's and the earlier ones' alike, names a session the same way: as its CLI names it (`SessionTitles` in `packages/hub/src/sessionTitles.ts`). For Claude Code that is its transcript's latest `custom-title` (`customTitle`, which /rename and a launch's `--name` write, so a Switchboard task's is its launch name, `switchboard · <playbook> · <source> <key>`), else its latest `ai-title` (`aiTitle`, Claude Code's own), looking back through the transcripts it was resumed or forked from; for Codex the thread's latest `thread_name` in `$CODEX_HOME/session_index.jsonl`, which Codex appends to when it names or renames a thread. Each file is read only past where it was last read, and a transcript not found is looked for again after 10 s. Until the CLI has named a session, its name is its first message that stands, cut to its first sentence as a new conversation's title is (`summarizeMessage`), else Setup or Task session; never its ID, which for a Codex thread is a long UUID. On each conversation poll the hub reads the names of the sessions the open pages list, and sends their dropdowns again when one changed, so a rename in the CLI shows within the poll. Every row draws, in one column each, the session's state codicon and its provider's mark, the New session form's Claude or Codex mark with no background of its own (an empty mark of the same width when the provider is unknown), then its name; the dropdown's button shows the shown session's state codicon and provider's mark, an earlier session's while the page shows one, and the work item's title over the session's name, both in its tooltip; a hub that sends no `sessionTitle` leaves the work item's title on the current row. The dropdown widens to show names whole, up to 720 px and the room to the viewport's edge. `select {workItemId, sessionId}` from a page shows one of the work item's sessions, and the scope then names it with `earlier: true` while it is not the current one; naming the current session, or none, shows the current one, and a session of another work item is 403. The page stays on the chosen session while newer ones start, and asks for it again on a new stream. The scope's folder stays the current session's, so the work item's artifacts and changes do not move with it. The hub sends such a session `read-only` with the reason whatever its state, and refuses every command that names it but the reads (`history`, `states`, `commands`, `seen`), and uploads and dictation into it, with 409; the voice treats the page as showing no session (`liveScope`), so nothing types into, resumes or pins an earlier session that still runs, and its Console tab only watches. Row actions name the work item and still apply.
- **List.** `GET /v1/page/list` and the stream's `list {groups}` events, sent when it changes, carry the groups and rows built from the dock view (`pageList` and `pageRow` in `page.ts`; [Rows](#rows)), so the page groups, orders and marks a work item by the hub's rules alone. Pool tickets' rows in Unstarted and Snoozed have no conversation, so they carry their `ticketId` and no `workItemId`. Each row carries the controls the page offers ([Row actions](#web-page)), what it opens, and its search text. The selection colour and `aria-current` mark only the row of the work item whose conversation is open, or is opening; keyboard focus shows as the focus ring and moves nothing else, and neither a hover nor an open row menu selects a row (the operator's decision, 2026-11-23). A click selects the work item and opens its conversation (voice-design decision 19); a second click on the row being opened is ignored, a click on another row replaces it, and a refused select says why in the toast. [Hosted conversations](#hosted-conversations) sit in their host's group, with their host's name. A page's `conversations`, its header's dropdown, are its selected work item's group (`pageGroups`), which keeps the selected work item when it is done.
- **Groups.** A group heading is a light tint of the focus blue, a little stronger on hover, with a chevron, the group's codicon, its title and its count in VS Code's neutral badge colours, muted at zero, since the accent badge means unread ([Unread](#unread)); a click opens or closes the group, quick second clicks toggling each time. Blocked on you, Ready for review, In progress and Unstarted start open and Done and Snoozed closed, until the operator's choice, which each browser keeps in local storage. Blocked on you and Snoozed show only with rows; an empty Ready for review, In progress or Unstarted shows its empty text in muted italics, and a list with no rows at all shows one message instead of the groups. Done's date bands (Today, Yesterday, Earlier this week, Last week, Two weeks ago, then older Done rows by month) are muted labels with a count and a rule that open or close, Today and Yesterday open at each load. A collapsed group or closed band still shows the selected row. Done draws only the rows in view, with a small buffer, keeping the selected and focused rows mounted and their measured heights, so hundreds of rows stay fast ([mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M134). The heading of the group at the list's top stays pinned on an opaque bar, keeping its toggle and count, while its rows scroll under it, until the next heading pushes it up, and a row scrolled into view lands below it. On a phone the list runs under the home indicator and its last row scrolls clear of it.
- **Rows.** Line 1: a priority dot, with its immediacy and severity in its tooltip, on a priority ticket only; the title in semibold, cut with an ellipsis; the voice target's mark ([Voice](#voice)); and the time, muted, with what it counts and since when in its tooltip, a Done row's being its done time, redrawn at least every minute. Line 2: the comment icon with every comment and its time in its tooltip; the playbook with its namespace muted, or a hosted conversation's host; the ticket key pill in its source's colour, its source's logo (GitHub's mark, Zoho Desk's, or the logo a source plugin gives, such as DPAF's) in place of its source's label, so `gh 12345` shows as GitHub's mark and `12345`, with the whole key its accessible name and tooltip; a key another repository's prefix starts, an ad hoc key, which starts with the repository or folder name, and a key of a source without a logo, which shows a plug, keep their whole text; then link pills (a pull request purple with its icon and number; an issue by GitHub's mark and its number, so `gh 21517` shows as GitHub's mark and `21517`, with the whole key its accessible name and tooltip, while a key another repository's prefix or `owner/name` starts keeps its whole text (the operator's decision, 2026-11-25); a link by the link icon and its label, a link the ticket's text names grey and last) and the Returned pill, outlined and filled in the warning stripe's colour with its return icon and count. Line 2 is laid out with CSS alone, never measured, so a redraw cannot change it (the operator's decision, 2026-11-22), and it is one line: its items never wrap under one another. Whether the pills show their texts depends on line 2's width alone, the same for every row (the operator's decision, 2026-11-25, replacing 2026-11-24's shrinking each link pill on its own while the key always showed whole): where line 2 is at least 300 px wide, a list about 320 px wide, every pill, the key, the link pills and Returned, shows its icon and its text; narrower, every pill shows its icon alone. Link pills and Returned that do not fit are left out whole, Returned first, then the links from the last, at any width, while the rows still change at the same fixed widths (the operator's decision, 2026-11-25); the key is never left out. The comment icon is never cut; a key takes at most 60% of the line, a longer one, such as a long ad hoc key, cut with an ellipsis; the playbook or host shrinks instead, and where it does not fit beside them, as a long name can in a list about 200 px wide, it is cut with an ellipsis, its tooltip naming it whole, so line 2 never wraps (the operator's decision, 2026-11-25). A link pill's text is whole or not shown, never cut. An icon-only pill keeps its text as its accessible name, and its link, click, keyboard stop and tooltip; a pill left out is no keyboard stop. Line 3: the mark, then the hub's details with a muted `·` between them, each in its tone, the last cut with an ellipsis, or the mark's title when there are none. A Ready for review row on the agent's turn is dimmed to 60%. Every mark, pill, time, comment, priority and detail has its meaning in a tooltip, and the marks and the priority dot have an image role and a label. No row shows an unread count ([Unread](#unread)).
- **Marks and stripes.** Each mark is a codicon from `MARK_GLYPHS` and `MARK_ICONS`, in the foreground colour and only Failed in the error colour; Running and Triaging spin, under reduced motion too. A 1 px left stripe, inset 8 px top and bottom, marks a failed row in the error colour, a blocked or asking one in the warning colour and the operator's turn in review in blue (`--sb-status-*`), and none on the agent's turn or snoozed; the selected row's stripe is full height and the row is in the selection colour, its texts keeping theirs. The list's scrollbar is the browser's own, always shown, thin and in the theme's slider colours (the operator's decision).
- **Row menu.** A right-click opens a row's menu at the pointer, and the Menu key or Shift+F10 at the row's corner; Escape returns the focus to the row. Its groups, separated, are the main action, work, state, notes, order, opens, and the controls that ask first. Choosing a work control, Copy or Open in VS Code on another row's menu opens its conversation before running it; opening or dismissing the menu leaves the conversation alone. Open ticket and an Open item per link come with the opens, more than two links in an Open link submenu, labels cut at 48 characters; a submenu (Open link, Copy) opens in place under its item, which works at a phone's width. Delete and Stop session ask first in a sheet, with the hub's question and detail; Snooze… offers the hub's choices, a date from the earliest timed choice waking at its time of day, and a comment, and Cancel posts nothing. The selected row has an Actions button that opens the same menu; its main control is a button in the conversation's header, and the first row of that menu. In the phone's layout the button is its icon alone, a 44 px target beside Actions, with its label as its tooltip and accessible name, so the work item's title keeps the rest of the header (the operator's decision, 2026-11-25, replacing 2026-11-21's leaving it to the menu alone).
- **Search** (PRD R63). The list header's Search shows or hides the search box; hiding it, or Escape, clears the search and its range. It matches the rows' search text (the title, key, linked pull requests and issues, playbook, group and branch), every word in any case, 250 ms after the last key from 3 characters, and at once for a ticket or link key such as `18941`, `gh 18941`, `PR #19002` or `#107`. Groups with a match show open and the rest are hidden, and bands open while filtering. Its range menu (Last 2 weeks, Last month, Last 3 months, All time) asks the hub for older Done rows (`page-search`), which join under their month; the box says Searching…, No matches for … in the …, with Search further back and All time, or Showing the newest N matches…, and a newer search or range drops an older answer.
- **Keyboard.** Rows, group and band headings and link pills take the focus, shown as a focus-blue ring. The list is one Tab stop, so Tab passes it in one press: the row or heading the focus was last on, else the selected row, else the first heading; a redraw keeps it and moves no focus. Up and Down move the focus along rows and headings and scroll it into view below the pinned heading, selecting nothing until Enter or Space opens a row (the operator's decision, 2026-11-23); Enter or Space on a heading toggles it; Tab from the stop's row reaches its key and visible link pills, the only pills that are Tab stops, then leaves the list. F8 and Shift+F8 open the next or previous row on the operator's turn in Blocked on you and Ready for review, wrapping in list order, opening its group and clearing a search that hides it. A redraw that moves the focused row keeps the focus on it, or on its closed group's heading, without taking the focus from another part of the page. The header's icon buttons have accessible names.
- **Settings.** PRD R54: Settings… in More actions shows Settings as a dialog over the page (`packages/web/src/settings.tsx`), the whole screen on a phone; what it edits, how and with which page commands is in [Settings](#settings), Editing. Settings… is on this computer's page only: a paired device's page leaves it out, and the hub refuses a paired device's Settings commands ([Remote access](#remote-access)).
- **List header.** The list's header (`packages/web/src/listHeader.tsx`) holds one icon per source kind, grey when off, which turns that kind's source on or off or, for a kind with several, opens a check menu of them, a kind without a logo showing a plug; then Search, New session and More actions, which keep their width at the right. A plugin's icon appears in both the header and the phone's More actions menu, grey while its source is off. Outside the phone's layout it has no title, which the tab or app title shows; in the phone's layout the app's icon and "Switchboard" start the row. `hello` and `list` carry `header` (`pageHeader` in `page.ts`): the dock view's notes (settings, playbook, hook, source and voice errors and setup notes, in the warning colour, then, untoned, why no device has the voice while the phone was lost), held-session note, source groups, repo roots, schedules and [holder notice and result](#restarting-the-holder). In the phone's layout the notes and the held-session note fold into one line above the groups, the first note cut with an ellipsis and how many more, which opens them all until pressed again, so they never take the list's first screen; elsewhere they show whole. Each list header command takes only a page key (403 otherwise) and runs its control: `page-source` turns a source on or off ([Rows](#rows), Source toggles); Search filters the list (Search, above), and a range past two weeks posts `page-search`, answered from the hub's Done search (`DoneSearch` in `packages/hub/src/dockSearch.ts`) as page rows; `page-poll` refreshes the sources ([Rows](#rows), Refresh); New session opens the [New session form](#standalone-sessions) as a sheet, whose Browse… is the hub's folder browser (`page-folders`), and keeps its Run as in local storage; `page-new-session` makes its conversation or task and `page-adhoc {schedule}` runs a schedule, each answering a repeated `opId` with its first success; `page-holder-restart`, on this computer's page only, restarts the holder with the detail the page showed ([Restarting the holder](#restarting-the-holder)). Pair a device… and Settings… are on this computer's page only ([Remote access](#remote-access)). Background sessions, from More actions or the note of background sessions without a work item, shows every held session in a sheet, orphans first, with each one's fields, its work item named by key and title, Open in terminal where it has one and Stop, asked first, while its process is live; it refreshes every five seconds while open, pausing during a Stop's question, and Open in terminal opens a new attach terminal window each time. Its authenticated page commands share the holder handlers: listing and Stop work on either device; opening the attach terminal app is local-only, and a hub refusal shows verbatim. Show Switchboard log opens the read-only hub log sheet described below. Get the browser extension… downloads the extension for this device and shows how to load it unpacked ([Browser extension](#browser-extension)). More actions holds Background sessions, Check sources now, Reload page (an installed app on a phone has no browser reload), Show Switchboard log, Get the browser extension… and Download app shortcut…, then, on this computer's page, Pair a device…, Settings… and, while the voice is not on this computer, Voice on the computer, and on a paired device Voice on this device ([Remote access](#remote-access)); in the phone's layout the source icons are check rows at its top.
- **Hub log lines.** Each line of `hub.log` is `<ISO UTC time> <level> <area>: <text>`, written by one writer in the hub process (`logLine` in `packages/core/src/log.ts`), including the start refusals and crash reports the hub process writes to its standard error, so no line lacks a time. Levels: `error` when something failed and needs the operator, such as a refused start or a crash; `warn` when something degraded or was skipped but Switchboard carried on; `info` for an event worth seeing; `debug` for timing and internals, kept in the file and hidden on the page by default. A log call without a level is `info`. The hub's log function labels each session a line names with its work item, `session bfd12580 (work item 123)`, from the store (`labelSessions`), but never with a title, since titles can carry ticket or customer text and the log is readable from a paired device. Temporary diagnostics use `debug` with the area `trace`, so they stay hidden and are easy to find and remove. To keep the log about what went wrong (operator decisions 2026-11-25): a sent message gets a `debug` line, and a `warn` line only when it took over 3 seconds or its text was not seen in the input box, with each step's duration on another `debug` line; the voice target is logged only when its session changes, never its generation, changes within 10 seconds merging into one line with a count, at `info` while the mic is on and `debug` otherwise; a warning that repeats on each poll, such as a roster or conversation read that fails, is logged in full once, then at most once an hour with a count, and an `info` line says when it stops (`RepeatedLog`); a triage model of the other agent is logged once per hub start, and the settings no longer used in one line per start or reload. The log never holds draft text, spoken or heard words, alert text or the Tailscale login, nor does `voice.log`, which counts words where it once quoted them. When a hub starts, through `hub.js start` or the supervisor once no hub holds the lock, or as a restart's successor, a `hub.log` of 10 MB or more is first renamed to `hub.log.1`, replacing the previous one (operator decision 2026-11-25), so the log and its one previous file stay near 20 MB. The check is at start because the hub's output is a handle its starter opened; the rename succeeds while processes still hold the file, since Node.js opens files with delete sharing on Windows, and a hub handing over writes its last lines to `hub.log.1`. A rename that fails anyway is written to `hub.log` as a warning and tried at the next start. A hub whose output was rotated away all the same, by a second start in the moment before it took the lock, finds so at its next line and writes its lines, crash reports included, to `hub.log` by path (`logWriter`).
- **Hub log** (PRD R109). The hub owns access to its fixed logs under the injected Switchboard home, `hub.log`, `voice.log` and `holder.log`; the page cannot choose a path or write to them. `GET /v1/page/hub-log?lines=<N>&log=<hub|voice|holder>` (`log` defaults to `hub`; any other name answers 400) needs the page cookie and the usual host and login checks, defaults to 500 lines and accepts 1–20,000. The hub reads backwards with a 4 MiB cap and answers `{text, more, limited, missing, file, size}` without caching, where `file` names the file read (its inode and birth time) and `size` its length; incomplete lines at the read boundary are omitted, and timestamps remain as written. Missing files answer an empty, marked result; read failures answer an error. Authorization is checked again after the read, so revocation during a read withholds its result. The sheet keeps its own lines, at most 20,000: every two seconds while visible it reads the newest 500 and appends those after the last line it holds, found by matching the last five lines, so the text never shifts under the reader; a read that does not reach that line, a new `file` or a smaller `size` starts the view again with a note. While the view is scrolled up or paused the new lines are held back and counted. Load earlier lines, at the top of the text, reads 500 more and adds the lines before the first one held, raising the scroll position by the height added. One read runs at a time, ending when the sheet is hidden or closed. The sheet reads each line's time, level and area; a line with no level, written before levels, counts as `info`, and a line with no time, such as a stack frame, belongs to the line above. It shows local times, with the UTC time in the tooltip and a date line where the local day changes, colours `warn` and `error`, and filters by level chips (Details, the `debug` lines, off by default), by area and by text. Filtering and wrapping are local to the sheet; copy takes the shown lines as written in the file. A paired device uses the same allowed host and Tailscale login and the sign-in bound to them as other page reads ([Remote access](#remote-access)); because the logs contain local paths, the allowed logins should contain only the operator's own Tailscale login. `voice.log` lines written before 2026-11-26 can hold alert text and heard words, so the hub answers the voice log only once `voice-log.json` exists in the home: the first time it starts a voice process after that change it moves `voice.log` to `voice.log.1`, which the page never reads, and writes the marker; until then the sheet says the voice log shows once the voice process restarts on this build. The voice process and the holder write their lines in the hub log's format, with a level, and the hub rotates `voice.log` and `holder.log` as it rotates `hub.log`, when it starts each process. Likewise `hub.log` lines written before 2026-11-26 can hold draft text, spoken commands and the Tailscale login: the first hub of this build writes `hub-log.json` with its start time, and the hub leaves the lines before it out of every answer, saying so (`earlierWithheld`), until rotation moves them to `hub.log.1`; with the marker missing or unreadable it gives no lines and says why, and the next hub start writes it again from its own start time. The cut is by time, so an older line stamped later than the marker, after the clock moved back, would still show. The sheet's picker chooses Hub, Voice or Terminal holder and starts the view again for the chosen log.
- **Artifacts.** `GET /v1/conversation/artifact/<page key>/<owner>/<file>`, and deeper for the files an artifact uses, serves a file of the selected work item's artifact folder ([Artifacts](#artifacts)), resolved by real path inside it (`artifactFile`), so `..`, a dot segment, or a link or junction out of it is 404, as is a segment that ends with a dot or a space, holds a backslash, a colon or a control character, or names a Windows device, which Windows reads as another name; spaces, `#` and other letters in a name are served; an external artifact's name, `@<id>/<file name>`, serves that listed file and, as `@<id>/<path>`, any file under the folder it is in ([Artifacts](#artifacts), Opening), by real path inside that folder, with the same name rules, outside `.git` and the Switchboard home and up to 32 MiB, from a page on this computer or a paired device alike; the hub opens the file, checks the open file is the one it checked, and streams from that handle no further than the size it checked, so a file swapped for a link meanwhile is not served. It is authorized by the page key in its path alone, and only from where the page that holds the key signed in, this computer or its remote host and login ([Remote access](#remote-access)), because a sandboxed frame's own requests carry no cookie and an HTML artifact's relative links must resolve; the key cannot post without the cookie. HTML, SVG and unknown types go out with `ARTIFACT_CSP`: a script-only sandbox without `allow-same-origin`, no `connect-src` and no forms. The page opens an HTML or SVG artifact in a frame sandboxed to scripts only, with no referrer, so its scripts have an opaque origin and reach neither the page's cookie, the page, nor the hub; CSP does not cover WebRTC, so a script could still open a peer connection. A PDF opens in the browser's own viewer in an unsandboxed frame, since the viewer does not run in a sandboxed one. Pictures, audio and video play in the page, text, data and code show escaped, Markdown is drawn by the page's own renderer, and a file of another kind shows that the page cannot show it. A new version of an open artifact replaces it.
- **Title.** The page's title counts the work items in the Blocked on you group, "(2) Switchboard" (`pageTitle`), so a background tab shows when something needs the operator. A page that raises [needs-you alerts](#needs-you-alerts) shows the last message in its toast, and a click on it selects its work item.
- **History.** The **History** tab (PRD R59, R98) is the first fixed tab after Chat, in the column's strip and in the conversation's strip on a narrower page, always shown and never closed. It answers, in order, what this is, where it stands, what needs the operator and what happened: a head with the key, repository, links, the title as the page's only `h1` and one line with the work item's status, or Waiting on you while its row waits on a question, a permission prompt or an approval, its playbook and R59's summary; then, while the row offers it, one timeline of the ticket's History, newest first, with the work items as groups, the open work item's first with the pinned state ([States](#states)) at the head of its rail. The head shows from the row and scope before History loads, after a failed read and on a row with no ticket History; a pool ticket's has no state. History from the work item's row menu in the list opens it on the tab; the header's actions menu has no History, since the tab stands beside it (operator decision 2026-11-26). A pool ticket has no work item to show it beside, so its menu on the page has no History. The tab reads the ticket's History from the hub when it shows, every 30 seconds and soon after a list change or a state event that changes the pinned state's ID while it shows, and not while it is hidden; it keeps what it showed for the same ticket until a read answers, and the chips, filter, groups, runs and state bodies the operator set per ticket while the page is open. The newest three states in the timeline show their body, older ones behind Details; Expand all opens the groups, runs and bodies. The timeline's text filter has a line of its own with Expand all and Collapse all, and the chips wrap on the lines under it, so at no width does a chip or a count cover the filter (the operator's decision, 2026-11-26); a narrow pane folds the chips behind a Filters button on their line. History is the tab every work item opens on unless Browser is chosen (`openOn`, below). `page-history {window, ticketId}` takes only a page key (403 otherwise) and answers `{view, categories}`, the History tab's view built by the hub (`historyView` in `packages/hub/src/history.ts`) and its filter chips' categories, or 409 for a ticket the store does not know; the page draws it with its own code (`packages/web/src/history.tsx`). Its links open on the page: an http or https URL in a browser tab, a file in a file tab, a worktree with Open in VS Code, and a session in the conversation, an earlier one read-only, by the work item and Switchboard session ID the hub adds to the session link's `open`.
- **New session in this work item.** The header's dropdown ends with **New session in this work item…**, which starts a new session of the work item the page shows, never a new work item. Choosing it starts nothing: the page shows an empty draft in that work item (`View.newDraft`), its composer focused and empty, the dropdown's button reading New session under the work item's title, and above the composer a note with the New session form's provider switch, while more than one provider is installed, preset to the provider of the conversation shown, and Cancel. The draft has no model choice: the session takes the work item's model, or the provider's configured default. Its composer sends text alone, with no attachments, slash commands, dictation or context meter, since no session exists yet; the hub sees the box unfocused, and dictation for the session shown stays in the hub's draft for it. Cancel, another selection or an earlier session's row leaves the draft, creating nothing, and the work item's own composer draft comes back. Sending posts `page-conversation {window, scopeGen, opId, text, provider?}`, which takes only a page key with a selection (403 otherwise, 409 for a stale `scopeGen`) and sends the flow's `item-session {workItemId, prompt, provider?}` for the scope's work item, never one the page names. The flow starts a task session at once, with the message as its prompt, in the chosen provider or else the latest session's, in the folder `startSession` gives the work item (its worktree, or its repository root or folder), with the playbook's launch options only in the playbook's own provider, no guidance and no change to `taskPrompt`; a hosted or standalone conversation's session keeps the conversation's report contract. It refuses an empty message, a work item that is unstarted, queued, triaging, in setup, awaiting approval or done, and any session of the ticket in a turn (PRD R17), and a Claude start without `sessions.installHooks`. As for any new session, an older Codex thread leaves its subscription and an older held Claude session keeps running ([Earlier sessions](#web-page)). The hub then selects the work item's current session for the page and answers `{workItemId, sessionId}`; a repeated `opId`, also from the page reconnected under a new key, answers the first session and starts none while the page shows that work item, a retry after the page moved to another work item is refused as stale, and a refusal leaves the draft and its text with the reason in the toast.
- **Prompts in the terminal.** While the selected conversation's Claude Code Mode session waits on a permission prompt or a question, the page reads it from the session's screen and answers it there. `prompt {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId}`, which the page asks while the session waits and the page is visible, answers `{prompt}` for a page key and its current Code Mode session waiting on a question or permission (409 otherwise): the prompt's lines, an `id` that hashes them, its numbered options and whether a question form's tab strip shows, or null (`readPrompt` in `packages/conversation/src/prompt.ts`, [mechanics.md](mechanics.md) M102). `prompt-key {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId, launch, prompt, key}`, `key` an option's digit, `escape` or `next`, writes that one key through the holder only when the launch is the one the page showed, the screen still shows the prompt with that `id`, the key is one of its own and no key was typed in a terminal within the typing pause ([Typing a message](#typing-a-message)), else 409 with why; a repeated `opId` answers the first result. A key is pressed only on the operator's click, never repeated, and typed answers stay in the terminal.
- **Context meter.** The composer's footer shows the selected conversation's [context meter](#context-meter) ([Conversation view](#conversation-view), Composer).
- **Console.** `GET /v1/conversation/console?window=<page key>&sessionId=<id>` (cookie) streams the selected conversation's current session as the terminal holder holds it (`PageConsole` in `packages/hub/src/console.ts`), for a Code Mode session, or a Codex app-server session with a thread, whose [Codex terminal](#codex-runner) the hub starts first. The hub opens its own holder `view` connection, which never resizes the session and is not one of its `clients` ([Terminal holder](#terminal-holder)), and sends SSE events `size {cols, rows}`, `data {data}` (the replay, then the output), `replayed`, `exited {exitCode}` and `closed {error}`, or one `unavailable {reason}` and the end when the key is not a page's, the session is not the one the page shows or not in the holder, a Codex terminal cannot start, or the holder is not running or cannot view. The hub ends a console, delivering nothing more, when its page closes, its scope generation changes, the session runs under another launch, its stream falls too far behind, or on `hub.js web revoke`. `console-input {window, scopeGen, opId, sessionId, launch, data, cols?, rows?}` takes only a page key and types `data` into that session with the checks of `prompt-key`: its scope and `scopeGen`, the launch the page showed (409 `SESSION_RELAUNCHED`), and the session's input reservation, through holder `write` with `key`, so the keys count as keys typed in a terminal for the composer's typing pause; with `cols` and `rows` it first resizes the session to them. A repeated `opId` answers the first result, and one page's keys are typed in the order posted. Only this route resizes a session for the page, and the page sends a size only when the console starts typing and when its pane then holds other cells, because each resize of a held session can repeat a line or two of its screen in its scrollback, which every terminal on it then shows (mechanics M104); a console that only watches never changes the size a terminal set. The page draws it on its **Console** tab (`packages/web/src/console.ts`), loading xterm.js only the first time a console shows. It follows the page's scheme, live, in VS Code's terminal colours for that scheme (`--vscode-terminal-*` in `theme.css`). Claude Code draws for its own theme, `dark` by default, mostly in 24-bit colours that no light palette can map (mechanics M106), so in the light scheme the page rewrites each 24-bit colour of Claude Code's dark theme into its light theme's, turns any other dark background light, keeps VS Code's 4.5:1 minimum contrast for text and draws dim text at 3:1 or more; the dark scheme draws the program's colours as they come. Since a colour is rewritten as it arrives, a console still streaming when the scheme changes draws the session again from a new replay, and an ended one keeps what it drew until Reconnect (mechanics M126). It draws the session at the size the program draws, never re-wrapping, in a pane that never scrolls, scaling its font down until the screen fits; `npm run check:console --workspace @switchboard/web` checks this layout in a real headless browser ([testing.md](testing.md)). It watches until clicked, then types every key, Esc, Ctrl+C, arrows and Enter included, until it loses the focus. A phone's on-screen keyboard has no Esc, Tab, Ctrl or arrows, and a web page cannot choose another keyboard, so on a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`), while it types, a keys bar under the screen offers Esc, Tab, Shift+Tab, Ctrl, held for the next key from the bar or the keyboard, and the four arrows, sent as xterm.js sends them, `ESC O` in application cursor mode. Its buttons never take the focus, so the keyboard stays open, and, since a phone's browser keeps the page's layout when its keyboard opens, the console pads its bottom by as much as the keyboard covers the bar, so the bar sits on the keyboard and the screen above it refits as for any pane change, leaving the cursor's line in view. Only data a key, a paste or an input method produced while it types is sent, so the terminal's answers to the program's queries never are. Mouse and focus reporting stay off (mechanics M101), so a pointer over it never types and selecting text works. An exit, an `unavailable` or `closed` event, or a lost stream ends it with why and a Reconnect button; an ended console never reconnects by itself, which would replay the screen over itself.
- **Row actions.** The page runs the row's controls on its selected work item only (`pageActions` in `page.ts`): those of the row's hub controls whose action is in `PAGE_ACTIONS` and that are not in a submenu, with the row's labels, groups, order and questions, and asks in a sheet for the text a control needs (Start with guidance…, Start with prompt… with its playbook choice, Add comment…). `row-action {window, scopeGen, opId, workItemId, label, until?, comment?, text?, choice?}` names the control by its label, since one action type can back several controls (the Move controls, the Starts); it takes only a page key and the page's selected work item (403 otherwise, 409 for a stale `scopeGen`), looks up the work item's dock row as it is now, and sends the row's own control's flow event (`pageActionEvent`), with the input's fields set by the hub; 409 "The work item no longer offers this." when the row no longer offers it, and 409 with the flow's refusal. A repeated `opId` answers the first result. A pool ticket's row has no conversation to select, so a click on it opens its menu under the row, with its pool controls (Start, Start with guidance…, Triage again, Snooze… or Unsnooze); `pool-action {window, opId, ticketId, label, …}` takes a page key and any pool ticket the list shows and posts its row's control as `row-action` does. `row-move {window, opId, workItemId, after}` is a drag of a Ready for review row: it takes a page key and any work item the list shows, since a drag selects no row, and sends the flow's `move` when both work items are in Ready for review now, else answers 409 with why; a repeated `opId` answers the first result. The row's opens (`pageOpens` in `page.ts`) are Open in VS Code, below, and Open report, which names its draft (the page shows History itself, as its [History](#web-page), and leaves out Open changes and Open in terminal, because its Changes list and Console tab show the same). Open report opens the draft in a file tab through `local-file`, which reads the selected work item's awaiting-approval draft as that one file wherever it lies ([Local files](#web-page)). `row-paste {window, scopeGen, opId, workItemId, label}` answers `{text}`, the drafted file Copy offers by that label as it is now, as Copy reads it (PRD R60); it only reads. One draft is a Copy item of its own and several are a Copy submenu of their labels, a copy that worked says `Copied <label>.` in the toast, and a control that asks first opens its sheet with the focus on Cancel, so Enter cancels it and no key elsewhere in the sheet runs it; a clipboard the browser refuses shows the text in a sheet to copy by hand.
- **Open in VS Code.** The one VS Code feature left: the row's Open in VS Code, in the Actions and row menus, runs `row-command {window, scopeGen, opId, workItemId, label}` (`commandInWindow` in `packages/hub/src/app.ts`), which opens the work item's window in VS Code. VS Code runs without any Switchboard extension, so the hub queues nothing for the window and nothing claims anything: the window opens no focus or report tabs and never closes itself. The command takes only a page key and its selected work item (403 otherwise, 409 for a stale `scopeGen`) and checks that the row offers it now (`pageCommandOf`). A row's window (`windowOf` in `packages/hub/src/dock.ts`) is:
  - for a work item, its latest session's `cwd`, else its worktree, with the worktree second when it differs; there is none without either, or while the latest session is `external` and not ended, because that session lives in the operator's own window (PRD R81). Its name is the ticket key, or the ad-hoc key for a ticket a ticket report moved (PRD R83), the first folder's name when it is not the worktree, and a short hash of the first folder's path, such as `gh-18941-3f2a91`, so two folders with the same name, or a moved worktree, get different windows; its file is `<Switchboard home>/windows/<name>.code-workspace`.
  - for a [hosted conversation](#hosted-conversations), its host's: the host's workspace file, or a folder host, which opens as a folder with no workspace file; a host that is no path, such as a page key, names no window, and the conversation opens its latest session's `cwd`, else its ticket's repository root, so no workspace file is ever named after a key.
- **Workspace file.** `writeWorkspaceFile` (`dock.ts`) writes the window's file just before VS Code starts: its folders, a `window.title` of the ticket title, the active editor and `sb:<name>`, with each `$` of the ticket title replaced by a fullwidth `＄` because VS Code reads a `$` as the start of a variable and drops the text after it (M26), and `extensions.ignoreRecommendations: true`, because VS Code keeps a dismissed recommendation prompt per workspace, so every new window would ask again (M50). It writes through a temporary file and a rename, and only when the text differs, because VS Code applies any change to an open window. A hosted conversation's file is written from its host's own row; when no row writes it and it is gone, the conversation's own folder opens instead. The files stay in `~/.switchboard/windows/`.
- **Launch.** `openCode` (`packages/hub/src/launch.ts`) starts VS Code detached and without a shell on the file or folder, in the hub's environment without its Electron and VS Code variables (`launchEnvironment`): on Windows `Code.exe` beside the `bin\code.cmd` found on PATH, else in the per-user install folder, and elsewhere `code` on PATH (`codeExecutable`). VS Code opens the window, or brings forward the one already on it; Open in VS Code from a phone opens it on this computer. The hub waits until the program has started, so a launch that fails after the launcher started it, as a `Code.exe` that is found but cannot run, answers 409 with the reason, "VS Code did not start: …", as do a workspace file that cannot be written ("The workspace file was not written: …") and a row that no longer offers it; it answers 200 only once VS Code has started. A repeated `opId` waits for and answers the first result.
- **Order** (PRD R73). A Ready for review row drags to a new place, with a mouse or pen once it moves a few pixels up or down, and with a finger once the finger has held still on the row for a moment, so a touch that moves at once still scrolls the list and a held one does not; a drop line shows where it lands, the release posts `row-move` and is not the row's click, and Escape, a cancelled pointer or a lost capture ends the drag without a move. Alt+Up and Alt+Down, outside a text field and the console, run the selected row's Move up and Move down through `row-action`.
- **Toast.** The page has one place for what an action reports: one message at a time over the page's top, above every sheet and Settings, so it shows on every tab and in the phone's list and conversation alike. A refusal or failure (a select, a row's control, a message not sent, a source toggle, a file not attached) shows in the warning colours with a warning icon; a message that reports no problem, such as a copy that worked or the needs-you message, in neutral ones with an information icon. It is sized and edged to be noticed on a wide desktop page, and its Dismiss is a 44 px target on a touch screen. A message hides once it has shown for 10 seconds on a visible page with the pointer off it, the pointer leaving it starting that time again, and Dismiss hides it; the same message again starts its time again, and a later action that succeeds, or opening another work item, clears a refusal. A refusal for a work item the page does not show, such as a message not sent from another one, shows when that work item is opened. A dialog's own error, such as a Settings field's or a pairing sheet's, stays in the dialog that asked, and a question card's answer that was not sent or dismissed says why in its card, which keeps the picks for another try. Any module of the page can report in it through `showToast` (`packages/web/src/toast.tsx`).
- **Links.** With embedding enabled and ready, a web-link pill selects its work item and opens or focuses its embedded tab (R125). Ctrl/Meta+click skips selection and embedding, opening through the local hub's default-browser command or an outside tab on a paired device. Otherwise ordinary links use the browser extension's Switchboard tab group (R123), or a new browser tab without an opener or referrer. An installed local page without that extension asks the hub's `row-open` command to use the system's default browser. That command validates the page key and the row's current http or https link. A repeated operation answers its first result.
- **Files.** `GET /v1/conversation/files?window=<page key>&q=<text>` (cookie) answers `{files}`, the `@` picker's files and folders under the selected conversation's current folder whose path holds the text (`findFiles`), a bounded search that skips dependency, build and dot folders; 403 for a page with no selection.
- **Changes** (PRD R118). `GET /v1/conversation/changes?window=<page key>` (cookie; `PageChanges` in `packages/hub/src/changes.ts`) answers the page's selected conversation's worktree changes, read with the git CLI in its folder, which must be its repository's top. The base is the merge base of HEAD with `origin/HEAD`, else `origin/main`, else `origin/master`, and without one the rows compare HEAD with disk. The answer also names the commits from the base to HEAD, newest first, at most 200, each with its first parent, subject and time, for the page's tree. `from` (`base`, `HEAD` or a commit's full ID) and `to` (`worktree`, the files on disk; `HEAD`, read as HEAD's commit at each read; or a commit's full ID) name another range, which the page's tree sends: `from=HEAD&to=worktree` is the uncommitted changes alone, and a commit's own changes are `from=<its parent>&to=<it>`. A commit must be one since the base, any of them and not only those listed, or for `from` the first parent of one, so the page can name no other ref; any other commit is refused 409 with `stale: true`, which the page answers, from the list, a diff or a file read, by going back to every change since the base, and another value 400. A range to a commit has only that range's committed changes, both sides read from commits. The `diff` and `file` routes and the `viewed` command take the same `from` and `to`, so a diff, a file and a tick are read against the list the page shows, and a held diff from HEAD is read again after a commit. The scope is the page's own and is not kept: the tab opens on every change since the base, and a chosen range ends when the page leaves the conversation or reloads; the bar names it, and its one back control closes a file view, else returns to every change since the base. The file view lies under the bar, so the bar and the tree's button stay in reach; the bar names the file and holds its controls, so the file view draws no header of its own and keeps that row for the file. The rows are `changesPlan`'s base-to-disk rows (`packages/core/src/changes.ts`, PRD R57), each with its status, the stages that changed it and its line counts; untracked files always show and ignored ones never do. git runs without a shell and with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, so a read never takes the index lock a session's own git commands need, and with literal pathspecs, no external diff or textconv, no prompt and the caller's `GIT_DIR` and its kin left out. 403 for a key that is not a page's or has no selection, 409 with why when the folder is not a repository's top or has no commit, and 409 "The window's conversation changed." when the page's scope generation, folder or work item changed while git read. The change routes answer with an `ETag` and 304 to a matching `If-None-Match`, gzipped to a client that takes gzip. The page reads the list (`packages/web/src/changes.tsx`) when its Changes tab first opens in a conversation, on Refresh and when the session's turn ends once the tab was opened.
- **Changes tree** (PRD R118, R128). The tree beside the diffs (`packages/web/src/changesTree.tsx`) draws from four answers the page keeps apart: the list the diffs show, the uncommitted list (`changes?from=HEAD&to=worktree`, which also gives FILES · All its status letters), the stack and the worktree's files, so choosing another range changes only the first. They are read with the list, except that another range reads only the list. An expanded commit's own files come from a `changes` read of `from=<parent>&to=<it>` that is not made the shown list, the last 20 kept. A file of FILES · All, a search result and a Markdown link open with `file?from=HEAD&to=worktree&side=disk`, so they show the file on disk whatever range is chosen. Pinned or unpinned, its width and the diffs' layout are each device's, in local storage, as Wrap is. Its search box keeps the box's width for the text at every width: Match case, Whole word, Regular expression and the text search are check rows of one menu from a button at the box's right end, whose badge counts those on without widening it (the operator's decision, 2026-11-26). Choosing a row keeps the menu open, and Up and Down move along its rows; a press outside it or Escape closes it, Escape giving the focus back to the button and leaving an unpinned tree open.
  - `GET /v1/conversation/stack?window=<page key>[&refresh=1]` (`PageStack` in `packages/hub/src/stack.ts`) answers the branch's stacked bases, read apart from `changes` so the list, diffs and files never pay for it.
